# Aegis Design Decision Log

Codename "Aegis": a terminal (TUI) single-player RPG. Elden Ring / Kingdom Come: Deliverance tone and depth, turn-based instead of real-time. High fantasy, classless (stats + skills + equipment define capability), mostly procedural world with authored-feeling storylines, death as setback not permadeath, no two playthroughs alike, infinite NG+ where a finished character enters a fresh harder world, multiple characters with selectable races / starting stats / starting gear.

Research backing these decisions lives in `research/` (00-overview.md is the synthesis).

## Pillar emphasis (user-stated, 2026-07-16)

What each inspiration actually contributes:
- From KCD: starting from nothing and earning competence (zero-to-hero), and a LOT of different things to spend your time doing (activity breadth). NOT the fencing simulation specifically.
- From Elden Ring: classless design, equipment and stats as identity, grinding as a legitimate and enjoyable loop, the danger of what dying does, exploration, and story.

## Decided

### D-001: Presentation is layered maps (2026-07-16)
Walkable coarse overworld (one tile = a landmark or region), local tactical tile maps for dungeons/towns/sites worth exploring, and full-screen prose scenes for dialogue, events, and quest beats.
Rationale: keeps real "what's over that ridge" map exploration (Elden Ring's core joy) while giving narrative a first-class surface (skill checks and dialogue in prose scenes), and keeps procgen cost sane at epic world scale. Rejected: full local-scale map everywhere (Qud-style; enormous procgen surface at epic scale, story becomes skimmable pop-ups) and prose-first travel (loses map exploration).

### D-002: Tech stack is C# / .NET (2026-07-16)
Rationale: the hard 80% of Aegis is a deterministic simulation core (world fact graph, storylets, hierarchical-seed procgen, seed+delta saves), and C# has the best iteration loop, debugger, and query ergonomics (LINQ over the fact graph) for that work. TUI layer will be a lean custom render layer or Terminal.Gui v2; NativeAOT single-file publish for distribution. Runner-up was Rust + ratatui (research's pick on rendering/binary-size grounds, but its costs land on the sim core, which is the hard part). Research: `research/05-terminal-ui-tech-stack.md`.

### D-003: Epic world, medium critical path (2026-07-16)
The world carries an epic amount of content (30h+ if you explore), but the authored main storyline is finishable in roughly 10-15h if you beeline. Elden Ring's own structure.
Rationale: preserves both pillars, deep storylines AND actually reaching NG+. Skipped content is not wasted because the next world is different. Matches research pattern: compact authored spine as the fixed anchor, procedural everything-else (`research/01-procedural-world-plus-authored-narrative.md`).

### D-004: Combat is a telegraphed-intent grid, lean version (2026-07-16)
Turn-based combat on the local tactical maps. Enemies display intents for their next action (attack arcs, windups, guards, repositioning); the player's turn is a response (step out, block, parry, trade, punish the windup). Commitment runs both ways: heavy player attacks take a visible windup enemies react to. Core modules at launch: stamina economy (attack/parry/dodge draw one pool), weapon-family movesets (weapons change verbs, not just numbers), posture/stagger bar, and knowledge-sharpened telegraphs (telegraph clarity scales with Perception, weapon familiarity, and bestiary knowledge of that enemy family; banked knowledge carries into NG+).
Shelved as possible later additions: zoned armor, body-part targeting menus, directional guards (KCD fencing sim de-emphasized per pillar statement). Menu-duel verbs may return later as formal 1v1 duel set-pieces reusing the same systems.
Pacing at epic length: lethality in both directions (trash dies in 2-3 turns), simple intents for simple enemies, and one-keypress overwhelm resolution when the player's threat massively exceeds the enemy's.
Rejected chassis: plain grid only (no reads, no duel feel), full timeline system (party-scale machinery for a mostly-solo game; we keep only action-speed differences and windup interrupts), menu duel as main system (no positioning, starves ranged/stealth/mage builds), deck-driven (genre transplant, randomness undermines reads).

### D-005: Uniformly humble starts (2026-07-16)
Every origin starts near the bottom of the power curve. Race/background decides WHICH skills are seeded and the flavor of your nothing (the hunter reads trails, the apprentice reads books), never how rich or strong you start. Literacy is a learnable skill; some origins start illiterate, and books gate recipes, techniques, and lore.
Rejected: wide origin spread (origin-as-difficulty dilutes zero-to-hero), universal destitution (kills early-hours replay variety).

### D-006: All four activity families are must-haves (2026-07-16)
Crafting trades (smithing, alchemy/herbalism, cooking), wilderness living (hunting, tracking, foraging, fishing, camping), crime (lockpicking, pickpocketing, burglary, fencing goods), and town life (gambling, carousing, tournaments, trading, eventually property).
Design discipline: every activity must feed at least two of (skill growth, money, world-state/reputation, story hooks), or it does not ship. Activities should hook into generated world facts (e.g. the apothecary buys herbs because her supplier died in the generated bandit raid).

### D-007: Growth is organic AND deliberately grindable (2026-07-16)
Use-based skill growth happens organically through normal play, and purpose-built diegetic grind loops also exist (sparring partners, training dummies, hunting grounds, dangerous farm spots) with diminishing returns and real costs (time passes, materials, durability, fatigue, danger). Every repeatable action costs something real, so grind is always a fair in-world investment and never an exploit (the Skyrim degenerate-loop failure is designed out).

### D-008: Death loop: banked vs at-risk, corpse run, Wounded (2026-07-16)
On death you respawn at your last rest point. Banked and untouchable, always: stats, skills, owned/worn gear, quest and relationship progress, recipes, bestiary knowledge. At risk: unspent coin plus all unbanked loot gathered since you last reached safety; it drops where you fell, one recovery attempt, gone forever if you die again first. Creates the expedition rhythm (carrying too much value is a decision; greed kills). You also wake Wounded: a temporary, visible, debt-framed debuff (e.g. reduced stamina cap) that fades with rest or can be worked off. Never any stat/skill/power loss (EverQuest death-spiral lesson). Every penalty is legible at the moment it lands.
Dependency resolved by D-014: unspent Essence joins the at-risk bucket, completing the full runes loop (coin + unbanked loot + unspent Essence drop on death).

### D-009: Scars via the Death's Toll meter (2026-07-16)
A visible meter fills on each death and drains over in-game time. Routine, spaced deaths never scar. Clustered deaths or boss-tier deaths risk converting into a scar: a rare, permanent-ish, characterful consequence (lost eye, crushed hand, haunted look) with mechanical weight and dialogue hooks. Calibration anchor: Wildermyth, a handful per playthrough. Every scar has a costly, in-world path back to parity (surgeon, pilgrimage, salve, occasionally a superior prosthetic). The meter makes the fairness legible before the consequence lands.

### D-010: The Aegis as diegetic device (2026-07-16)
The codename becomes the fiction. The Aegis (exact flavor TBD in narrative session: artifact, pact, ward) is what catches your soul at death and returns you to your last rest point, why rest points work (the Aegis anchors there), why you wake Wounded (the Aegis is spent), and what carries you between worlds at NG+ transition. The main storyline is about it. Bearers are singular, giving the chosen-one frame a mechanical justification.

### D-011: NG+ contract and scaling (2026-07-16)
Three-bucket carry-over at NG+ transition:
- Character: full carry (stats, skills, gear, recipes, lore, bestiary knowledge).
- World: always fully regenerated (map, NPCs, factions, quests, dungeons, history).
- Legend: bounded meta-layer with diminishing returns (0.5-0.8 exponent per incremental-game research): titles, Hall of Legends, small boons. Big enough that a new world's first hour feels like a visible step up; never big enough to trivialize content.
Difficulty composition: discrete World Hostility Tier as a first-class GENERATION input (each rung adds new enemy families, hazards, hostile-faction world facts, scarcer havens, modest stat bump; never a post-hoc multiplier, avoiding both the Elden Ring NG+7 cap-out and the Risk of Rain 2 decoupled-scalar failure) PLUS optional stackable covenants chosen at world creation (Hades Pact-style) totaling a visible Threat score whose rewards are legend/cosmetic/challenge currencies, never raw power.
Death consequences scale with tier in magnitude, never in shape: tighter reclaim windows, longer Wounded durations, faster Death's Toll fill, death reputation spreading further. (White space: no shipped game scales the death penalty across NG+ tiers.)
Amendment (2026-07-16): coin does NOT carry across NG+; it is converted at the crossing (conversion target TBD with the economy design: leading candidates are Legend credit or physical carryables). Keeps each fresh world's humble economy meaningful; gear and Essence-bought power still carry per the Character bucket.

### D-012: Save system (2026-07-16)
Autosave fires at the moment of death, before the penalty screen, carrying all consequence state (scars, reputation, world flags), so a naive reload lands post-death. Manual saves exist for normal life reasons. Optional Ironman toggle at character creation enforces single-slot autosave-only. Penalties stay mild enough that save-scumming is not worth the friction.

### D-013: Past characters enter world mythology (2026-07-16)
Completed characters' stories become candidate facts for future world generation (any new world: fresh character or NG+): statues, ballads, tombs containing their actual gear, factions that revere or curse their name. Multiple characters stop being parallel saves and become a personal mythology. (Precedent: Wildermyth legacy heroes.)

### D-014: Two-track growth: use-based skills + Essence-bought attributes (2026-07-16)
Skills grow by use, with every gain cost-gated per D-007 (ingredients, durability, fatigue, risk; skill XP never derives from gold value, closing the Skyrim alchemy loop by construction). Attributes are raised by spending Essence at rest points; Essence is earned from meaningful deeds (kills, quests, discoveries, first-time feats). Fiction: the Aegis gathers the essence of your deeds and reshapes you where it anchors (D-010). Unspent Essence is at-risk on death (amends D-008).
This overrules the research's pure-use-based recommendation deliberately: the research's real target was the Morrowind/Oblivion coupling of skill choices to attribute budgets, which Souls-style deed-currency does not have. Essence restores the runes loop, aspirational stat grinding toward gear requirements, and grind agency, all named user loves.

### D-015: Seven attributes with soft caps; gear prints requirements (2026-07-16)
Might (melee power, heavy gear, carry), Grace (speed, evasion, finesse, quiet movement), Vigor (health, stamina pool, resistances), Wits (perception, initiative, ranged aim, telegraph clarity per D-004), Mind (learning, lore, magic reservoir), Will (posture, fear resistance, magic control, Death's Toll resilience), Presence (social gravity, persuasion, leadership). Mind/Will reserved as casting stats pending the magic session; mental and physical power kept on independent axes (Qud lesson) so hidden classes never re-emerge. All scaling stats soft-capped (diminishing, never zero, returns). Weapons and armor print hard attribute/skill requirements visible before investment; under-requirement use is penalized, not blocked.

### D-016: Skills (~18 draft), perks, proficiencies, respec (2026-07-16)
Draft skill list (content, freely editable): Combat: Blades, Hafted, Polearms, Ranged, Brawling, Warding. Craft: Smithing, Alchemy, Cooking. Wilderness: Hunting, Survival, Athletics. Subterfuge: Stealth, Larceny. Social: Persuasion, Commerce. Mind: Lore (incl. literacy), plus reserved magic skill slots. Kept compact deliberately (no PoE-scale graph; equipment combinatorics carry build variety).
Perks: at skill thresholds, choose 1 of 2-3 mutually exclusive perks (KCD pattern). Proficiencies: discrete know-how (recipes, techniques, faction customs) from books, mentors, quests (CDDA pattern; where literacy pays off).
Respec: attributes only, via a rare lore-flavored "unbinding" of the Aegis, a handful per world, refreshed at NG+ transition (Larval Tear pattern). Skills never respec: use-based skills only ever reflect what you actually did.

### D-017: Races: familiar anchors, procedural cultures (2026-07-16)
Recognizable races (human, dwarf, elf, orc-ish, plus 1-2 originals TBD) as fixed anchors; each generated world rerolls their cultures, clans, feuds, and social standing. Mechanically a race gives small attribute tilts, one qualitative racial trait, and a social position in the generated world; the separately chosen background seeds starting skills. All starts uniformly humble per D-005.

### D-018: The narrative engine stack (2026-07-16)
Five layers, adopted per research consensus (`research/01`, `research/06`):
1. World fact graph as source of truth: causal-grammar history generation at worldgen (settlements, factions, notable NPCs, grudges, wars, relics as ID-referenced facts), extended at runtime by player deeds; past characters' legends (D-013) enter as facts.
2. Storylets as the only content unit: atomic, precondition-gated, handwritten chunks indexed by qualities; scales additively forever (the infinite-NG+ requirement). Accepted cost: a long "unimpressive middle" until the library is dense.
3. Role-casting (Wildermyth): pivotal scenes written against role slots, cast at runtime from existing world NPCs.
4. NPC memory (Nemesis): structured per-NPC logs of player interactions (including witnessed player deaths per D-008); dialogue is memory-driven SELECTION over large authored line banks, never open generation.
5. Pacing director (RimWorld): decides when content surfaces, separate from what it is; NG+ hostility tiers tune it.
Two enforced disciplines: every generated quest must trace to an existing world fact or is not generated; every important fact appears on multiple surfaces (quest, rumor, item text). Filler jobs stay honest ambient texture with salience-picked flavor variety.

### D-019: The Aegis is a bound intelligence (2026-07-16)
Ancient, sentient, fastened to the player; its nature is the game's central mystery. Provides a narrative voice at every death (Hades pattern), diegetic tutorialization, and the only continuous character across all worlds and NG+ cycles. (Refines D-010.)

### D-020: Two-layer main quest (2026-07-16)
Per world: an authored world-story spine template drawn from a growing pool (launch target ~3: e.g. usurped throne, creeping blight, war of faiths), structure handwritten and never proceduralized, cast/geography/factions filled from that world's fact graph. Across worlds: the authored Aegis mystery arc, advancing at world completions and NG+ transitions, unfolding over the first several cycles and resolving into an earned steady state (Hall of Legends, covenants, and fresh world-stories carry motivation beyond it).
Reference to study when writing the arc: FFXIV story summaries at `_external_resources/ffxiv_good_story/` (user-provided; the model for long-arc setup and payoff).

### D-021: Hybrid dialogue + no runtime LLM (2026-07-16)
Dramatic storylet scenes use choice menus with visible skill checks. Ordinary NPCs additionally expose a Morrowind-style "ask about" topic system with topics drawn live from the fact graph (the delivery mechanism that makes generated history queryable in-fiction).
Runtime is fully deterministic: zero LLM in the live loop (testability, reproducible seeds per D-011, self-contained offline binary). AI is an authoring-time tool only: mass-producing storylet prose and line-bank variants into the deterministic format, structure human-designed and machine-validated.

### D-022: Magic: rare and feared, diegetic acquisition, one economy (2026-07-16)
Magic is uncommon in the world and treated with awe or suspicion (grounded tone; caster reputation interplays with Presence and factions). Spells are acquired only diegetically: grimoire pages as loot (literacy gates them), mentors, shrine rituals tied to worldgen facts; never a level-up menu. Spell attunement capacity comes from found world objects (Elden Ring Memory Stone pattern) so attributes never double-tax hybrids; Mind scales potency, Will scales control. No separate mana bar: casting draws the shared stamina pool (one economy for true martial/magical hybrids), strong spells consume components, overreaching Will risks miscast. Casts are telegraphed windups on the intent grid, interruptible both ways. Use-based magic skill growth is spam-proofed by D-007. Research: `research/07-magic-systems-classless.md`.

### D-023: Factions: event-driven world-state layer (2026-07-16)
No full agent simulation (X4/A-Life cost-and-invisibility trap). Each faction is a small state vector with causal-grammar transition rules evaluated on a coarse tick; every transition writes a fact into the world graph AND carries a mandatory narration hook (rumor, notice, refugees, price change): if the player cannot perceive a change, it does not fire. Conflicts have designed exit conditions (Bannerlord stalemate lesson). Named leaders/lieutenants form a bounded Nemesis-style memory roster. Reputation is dual-scalar Fame/Infamy per faction (New Vegas). The pacing director throttles faction events off summary stats; hostility tiers raise faction aggression as a generation input. Research: `research/08-living-world-factions.md`.

### D-024: Companions: summon + mortal guests + pack animal (2026-07-16)
Three non-overlapping niches, no permanent tactical party (combat stays balanced around one character):
- One Spirit-Ash-style summon slot: autonomous, resource-gated against the player's own pool, no inventory/orders/scaling; conjurer build expression with near-zero UI.
- Story-scoped guest companions, role-cast from world NPCs, with one or two contextual command verbs, and they CAN permanently die: guests carry the mortal stakes the immortal player cannot (Wildermyth/Tyranny precedent).
- Pack animal / mount for logistics (carry capacity, travel).
The Aegis (D-019) remains the persistent banter/relationship voice. Research: `research/09-companions-hirelings.md`.

### D-025: Economy: auto-scaling sinks, crafting lane, patronage crossing (2026-07-16)
Coin is fully separate from Essence and must matter all game: upkeep/repair costs scale as a percentage of the player's own gear value (KCD pattern, auto-scales with wealth), plus a rising-cost aspirational sink ladder (property, retinue, master training, masterwork commissions) whose next rung is always priced above current means. Crafting lives in its own lane (consumables, augments/sockets, regional specialties), never racing loot for best-in-slot. Prices derive from world facts (regional scarcity, war, blockades); caravan/arbitrage play exists as productive capital. At the NG+ crossing, coin does not carry (D-011 amendment): it converts via patronage deeds (endow guilds, raise monuments, fund shrines, commission statues) into Legend credit AND candidate facts for future worldgen (D-013), so later characters encounter what past wealth built. Research: `research/10-single-player-economy.md`.

### D-026: Aegis-arc canon: The Ledger (2026-07-16)
FFXIV study complete (`research/11-ffxiv-storytelling/`, synthesis in `00-synthesis.md`). Canon A "The Ledger" adopted: the worlds are links on a Chain descending toward the Hearth where worlds are kindled; the Aegis is a ward-intelligence forged by the Shieldwrights to find and temper a soul fit to keep it; Essence is the scale, the Severed are prior bearers who broke or refused, the Unbinder (respec service) is the first bearer and fixed ideological anchor, hostility tiers are depth on the Chain, past-character mythology is bearer echoes. Arc structure: five-cycle reveal ladder (every touch a complete micro-reveal), ~15% content budget concentrated at world edges plus the crossing scene, threshold choice at cycle 5 (endings differ in fiction and voice, never mechanics), steady state converts the Aegis to a candid equal and makes covenant selection diegetic, no new grand mystery layered on. Full spec: `design/story/aegis-arc.md`. Rejected alternatives preserved there: B "The Loom" (worlds-are-woven; risks unrealizing mortal companion stakes), C "The Warden" (bearer carries a sealed danger; single-spend twist, fights the earned-heroism fantasy).
World-story templates spec'd canon-light at `design/story/world-story-templates.md`: template contract, twelve iron rules, shared role-slot library, three launch templates (usurped throne, creeping blight, war of faiths).

### D-027: Dev harness: headless sim + pilot channel as first-class run modes (2026-07-16)
The engine (`Aegis.Core`) is I/O-free and renders to an in-memory text frame; every frontend consumes that frame. Three run modes ship in the same binary: normal TUI, `--pilot` (named-pipe JSON control channel serving the exact screen as text and accepting injected keys, alongside or instead of the console), and `aegis sim` (synchronous scripted headless runs with JSON output). Rationale: the deterministic sim core was already the architecture (D-002, D-011); making observability and drivability first-class means agents and scripts can playtest, regression-test, and balance-sweep without window automation, and the pilot screen is guaranteed identical to what a player sees because it IS the render path. Single-threaded engine loop with all input sources marshalled through one channel preserves determinism. Spec: `docs/dev-harness.md`. Implementation note: pipe writers must use BOM-less UTF-8 (a BOM flushed into a fresh unread pipe deadlocks both ends).

### D-028: Save v1 is seed + input journal (2026-07-16)
Implements D-012 by construction rather than by serialization: the engine is deterministic and advances only on input, so a save file is the versioned header (seed) plus every key applied, appended and flushed as it happens. Loading replays the journal. Consequences: autosave-at-death is automatic (the killing input is already durable), quitting loses at most nothing, save-scumming requires manually copying files (Ironman is nearly the default), and no per-system serialization code exists to drift out of sync. Costs accepted: replay time grows with session length (checkpoint compression is a planned optimization layered on top, not a format change) and any simulation-affecting code change breaks old saves (version gate refuses them; migrations TBD when they matter). Essence spending at the shrine (D-014's rest-point spend, rising global cost, Vigor/Might/Grace wired to HP-stamina/damage/dodge) landed with it, journaled like everything else.

### D-029: NG+ crossing prototype: waygate, derived world seeds, coin-to-Legend (2026-07-16)
First working slice of D-011's crossing. A waygate is placed on every overworld at generation (deterministic, path-carved reachable), shut until the world's deed is done (camp cleared in the slice), then `>` crosses. Mechanics: the character bucket carries (attributes, essence, deaths); coin converts 1:1 into a Legend counter at the threshold (placeholder for D-025's patronage deeds, which need an economy to patronize); an unreclaimed remnant is forfeited; wounds lift and the bearer arrives whole at the new shrine. World N+1 generates from `Derive(masterSeed, "cycle", N+1)` at Hostility Tier = cycle, where tier is a generation input (goblin count 3+tier-1 capped at 6, goblin HP 8+2*(tier-1)), never a live multiplier. The completed deed is written into the new world's fact graph as an "echo" fact and surfaces as a ballad on arrival (D-013's mythology pipe, single-character form). The first crossing delivers rung 1 of the reveal ladder (other worlds exist; this has happened before) in the crossing scene, per the arc spec. Cycle 1 uses the master seed directly, so all pre-crossing saves and tests replay unchanged, and a crossing is just more keys in the journal: save-through-crossing holds by construction (proven by a key-driven bot test that plays a full crossing and replays its journal to identical state). Deferred to real content: patronage conversion, Legend boons, covenant selection at the crossing, crossing-scene dressing beyond log lines, tier content bands beyond goblin scaling.

### D-030: Storylet format v1 and fact-graph schema conventions (2026-07-16)
The storylet engine (D-021's content unit) is implemented; format spec at `design/storylets.md`. A storylet is pure data (id, trigger surface, scope, once/cooldown/weight, declarative fact patterns for Requires/Forbids, line templates with tones, optional effect) plus two small compiled escape hatches (`When` predicate, `Effect` action) kept minimal so the format maps 1:1 onto external data files later. Six trigger surfaces: Arrival, EnterTile, NearHouse, Rest, DeedWritten, AmbientTurn (the pacing-director seam; its chance die rolls every eligible turn so the draw sequence is stable as the catalog grows). One trigger event fires at most one weighted-random winner from a dedicated per-world RNG stream. Fact-graph schema stays `(Type, Subject, Object, Detail)` with naming conventions and wildcard patterns; Requires captures feed line templates (`{r0.detail}`). Storylets v1 are additive to simulation (lines, facts, small grants; never combat/movement/worldgen), so every pre-storylet save journal still replays: no save version bump. Authoring lives in a C# catalog for now (AOT-safe, type-checked, zero parser); the external data format is deliberately deferred until content volume or modding demands it. Slice content: eight storylets proving chained gating (grievance writes `met`; gratitude requires `met` + `deed`), scope semantics (world-scoped resets at crossing, character-scoped never), the mythology pipe surfacing via `echo` facts, and cooldown-gated ambient flavor.

### D-031: NPCs v1: cast role slots, bump-to-talk, topics live from the fact graph (2026-07-16)
People exist. Worldgen casts three role slots (steadholder, herbwife, woodward) with generated names onto walkable tiles beside their houses, keeping the shrine road clear; each casting writes a `person` fact. Bump-to-talk opens an ask-about menu (D-023's Morrowind surface): topics are computed from the fact graph at the moment the conversation opens, so what people can discuss tracks world state with zero authored branching (raids topic swaps to quiet-nights after the deed; the black arch answer notices the hum; Old Songs appears only when an `echo` fact exists, surfacing the mythology pipe conversationally). First meetings write `met` facts. The menu is journaled exactly like the shrine menu, so conversations replay from saves; a key-driven test walks to an NPC, talks, and replays the journal to identical state. Storylets gain a Talk trigger. v1 limits, deliberate: NPCs are static, share one voice register regardless of role, and topic answers are template prose; per-role voices, schedules/movement, and topic-driven storylet hooks (ask-about as a storylet trigger surface with topic context) come with the template compiler, which can now cast real people into template roles.

### D-032: World-story template compiler v0: role casting, cast-bound storylets, priority tiers (2026-07-16)
The pipeline from `design/story/world-story-templates.md` exists in miniature. A template compiles at worldgen: it casts role slots onto generated entities (drawing from a dedicated `world-story` seed stream), writes `role` facts, and emits per-world storylets whose closures bind the cast (names, ids), merged with the global catalog per world and swapped at each crossing. First template is The Raided Stead, the slice story formalized: the plaintiff role is cast onto one of the settlement's NPCs (varying by seed, visible variation between worlds), act 1 is their personal plea (Talk-triggered, plaintiff-only, writes a `promise` fact), act 2 is the deed, act 3 is the witnessed ending at the moment of completion (writes `story_complete`) plus the kept-promise payoff, which requires having actually been asked: skipping act 1 is a legitimate playthrough where the personal payoff never opens. Kept promises pay Essence (weight), not coin. Alongside: storylets gained a Priority selection tier (only highest-priority eligible candidates enter the weighted draw), so dramatic template beats are never lost to ambient asides. The storylet format itself needed no changes to support compilation, as the spec predicted. Launch templates (Usurped Throne etc.) compile through this same seam once worlds generate the sites and factions they cast.

### D-033: Sites v1 and the first hostility-tier content band: the barrow and its wights (2026-07-17)
Sites are first-class: a `Site` is an enterable place with its own map, entrance, generated spawns (kind, cell, stats baked at generation per D-011), chest, and cleared/looted state; the goblin camp is site one and every prior behavior is unchanged. The barrow is the first tier content band: it generates only at tier 2+ (a corridor-and-chambers long barrow, deliberately unlike the camp's drunkard-walk cave, with its own `site-barrow` seed stream), so tiers now differ structurally, not just numerically. Its wights are the second monster family with genuinely different mechanics: grave-slow (a step only every other turn, so they can be kited), a cold grasp that drains stamina alongside damage, a heavier telegraphed barrow blade, true pathfinding in cardinal steps (they have walked their halls for an age; goblins keep their greedy stumble), poor coin but dense essence. Clearing writes a `deed barrow_stilled` fact feeding two new storylets (barrow-shadow drip on first touch, the bent-silver-pin thanks) and a talk topic; the waygate still answers to the camp deed alone, so the barrow is optional depth, never a longer mandatory path. Tier-1 worlds draw no new RNG and replay byte-identically; tier-2+ worldgen changed, so the save format bumps to v2 (first exercise of D-028's version gate). Live playtest found and fixed a template bug: The Raided Stead's witnessed ending fired on the barrow deed; DeedWritten storylets that answer a specific deed must Require it. Deferred: more families per band, site-specific loot beyond coin, tier-tilted template variants.

### D-034: The Unbinder v1: wandering respec service, guise deck, cross-world recognition (2026-07-17)
Attribute respec (D-016) is live, delivered by the arc's fixed anchor (D-026) in their v1 form: a wandering mender cast into every world at every tier under a fresh guise (name from its own `unbinder` seed stream, trade from a six-entry guise deck whose work lines live beside it so voice never drifts from casting). They camp well away from the stead on plain ground, path-carved reachable; villagers gain a "The wanderer" topic pointing the way (a `wanderer` fact with compass direction). The service: loosen one raised attribute point, refunding exactly what re-buying it will cost at the shrine afterward (the marginal cost under D-014's rising curve), so a respec round trip is lossless by construction and the scarcity is the cap, not a price: three unbindings per world, refreshed at each crossing (D-016's Larval Tear pattern). Vigor unbinds clamp HP and stamina. The talk menu grows a service entry; a new unbind menu mirrors the shrine menu and journals like everything else. Cross-world character state exists for the first time beyond the fired-set: the Player records the first-meeting cycle, and a Character-scoped recognition storylet fires on meeting a later world's Unbinder, completing its own small answer and writing a `noticed` fact that unlocks a topic (the early-clocking reward the arc spec asks for; the deeper reveal tiers stay gated for cycles 3-4 content). Their farewell line is the arc's planted motif-distortion; their worldview topic is the never-disproved position, worded identically in structure every world. The template compiler casts villagers only: the Unbinder can never be drawn into a world-story role. Worldgen changed at every tier (new NPC, new facts), so saves bump to v3. Deferred: reveal tiers 1-2 (cycle 3-4 gated confrontations), skill respec stays never (D-016), guise-deck growth, the standing irony content (arc spec sec 7).

### D-035: Template selection v1 and the Creeping Blight at slice scale (2026-07-17)
Worlds now choose their story. Templates are pool entries (id, eligibility predicate over a worldgen context, compile function); the generator collects eligible templates and picks one from the world-story stream, writing a `story` fact naming the choice (surfaced in the pilot snapshot as `storyTemplate`). When exactly one template is eligible there is no selection draw, which keeps tier-1 worlds (where only the Raided Stead qualifies) consuming exactly the RNG they always did; tier-2+ worlds gained a draw, so saves bump v3 to v4. The second template is the Creeping Blight compiled at slice scale against the barrow as its source site (the D-033 prerequisite paying off): something seeps downhill from the mound, and the story everyone tells about it is wrong. It exercises the two contract features the Raided Stead lacks: an accepted-history fact planted at compile and voiced as ambient settlement talk, flipped by an evidence beat the player finds by walking deep enough into the barrow itself (an EnterTile storylet gated on site and depth, no new trigger surface needed); and two endings on the barrow deed that branch on whether the evidence fact exists (truth published vs truth buried, distinct coda facts either way). The cast keeps the plea/promise/kept-promise chain shape (afflicted role, promise fact, essence payoff) so both templates prove the same spine contract with different content. Selection across seeds runs roughly 2:1 stead:blight at tier 2 (blight requires the barrow AND winning the draw); a master seed exists in tests for each branch. Live-verified end to end on seed 43: stead world 1, blight world 2, plea, evidence, truth ending, kept promise. Deferred: selection weighting against recent repeats across cycles, template-driven map dressing (a visible blight-front), the institution/zealot/Warden roles from the full template (need factions and more cast).

### D-036: Coin sinks v0: rations, wound-dressing, fact-derived prices (2026-07-17)
Coin gets its first in-world uses (D-025's economy started at slice scale). The steadholder sells rations as purchase entries appended to the ordinary talk menu (no separate shop mode; the menu stays open so repeat buys are repeat key presses): 4 coin each, carried up to 5, eaten anywhere with the new `e` key for +6 hp and +3 stamina at the cost of a turn, which makes a mid-fight bite a real gamble and gives the game its first healing that works away from the shrine. Rations live on your person, so unlike coin they survive death (the remnant takes coin and essence only) and cross waygates untouched: converting coin into provisions before a risky dive is now a genuine hedge against the remnant drop, which is exactly the kind of decision D-025 wants coin to carry. The herbwife dresses the wound for coin priced by remaining convalescence (ceil(WoundedTurns/4), so a fresh 80-turn wound costs 20 and waiting is always the free option); since death drops your purse where you fell, affording her usually means completing the corpse run first, which turns the existing death loop into an economic one without touching its rules. Prices read the fact graph (D-025's fact-derived pricing, v0): while a blight story stands uncompleted the ration costs 6, recovering to 4 when the `story_complete` fact lands, so the world's story state has a price signal for the first time. Talk menus gained journaled purchase keys, so saves bump v4 to v5. Deferred: the aspirational sink ladder, upkeep/repair (needs gear), crafting, caravan play, patronage deeds at the crossing (coin still auto-converts to Legend).

### D-037: Arc rungs for cycles 2-3: the hollow, flag-gated crossings, the vision, reveal tier 1 (2026-07-17)
The reveal ladder's second and third rungs land (design/story/aegis-arc.md sec 6 holds the full spoiler-level content; this entry stays structural). Rung progress lives on the character as flags (the fact graph is per-world), and every rung gates on the previous rung's flag, never on a cycle count, so beeliners and lingerers climb the same ladder in the same order; the pilot surfaces progress as a spoiler-free `arcProgress` flag string. Cycle 1 gains its missing ambient beat as a one-time AmbientTurn storylet (pure text, no worldgen change). Tier 2+ worlds cast the hollow: a small authored site (fixed layout, own seed stream, placed after all existing draws) holding one severed one, the arc's recurring antagonist class made literal: full-speed BFS pursuit, a heavier telegraphed cut, a touch that thins essence along with hp, a 15-essence 0-coin payout, and a keepsake cache instead of a strongbox. The encounter is optional like the barrow, recurs every deep world (pool principle: each defeat pays one card, never the deck), and is arc-cast rather than template-cast so every template still passes the delete test. Beating one the first time completes rung 2's micro-reveal on the spot and unlocks the crossing-scene admission; the following shrine rest delivers rung 3's bottle beat as a witnessed text vision (the cheaper option from arc sec 11, chosen for v1); the vision unlocks both the Unbinder's reveal-tier-1 confrontation (Talk storylet plus a persistent gated topic; tier stored on the character) and the ledger crossing scene. Crossing scenes are hand-coded line banks in AegisVoice keyed to pending flags, not storylets, since the crossing is systemic real estate the arc owns by construction. The forge-name placeholder is chosen (AegisVoice.ForgeName) and delivered as the cycle-2 cold open; the bearer-myth ambient anchor is a worldgen fact voiced near houses rather than a topic, which keeps villager talk menus within their nine digits. Live tuning note: the severed one at tier 2 beat a baseline-stat bearer down to 5/20 with a ration spent; it is deliberately the hardest optional fight in the band. Worldgen changed at tier 2+ and villager topics shifted, so saves bump v5 to v6. Deferred: cycle 4's two sympathetic Severed storylets and reveal tier 2, cycle 5's threshold, death-line register swaps by arc stage, the Severed trait deck.

### D-038: Arc rungs for cycle 4: the argument, in three voices, and the commission (2026-07-17)
The reveal ladder's fourth rung lands (design/story/aegis-arc.md sec 6 holds the spoiler-level content; this entry stays structural). The arc doc mandates two sympathetic Severed encounters with structurally different sympathy models, neither with a mandatory fight; both land as flag-gated content that opens once the ledger crossing has been heard. The agency model is a new NPC kind cast into every tier-3+ world on its own seed stream (Unbinder pattern: placed after all existing draws, so pinned worlds keep their layouts and old tiers replay byte-identically): a hermit at a small fire, met as a person, with their own talk surface and a worldview written to the never-disproved rule; their designed beat is a Talk storylet, and hearing it unlocks a further topic. The game gives them no fight path at all: bump-to-talk is the only verb NPCs answer to, which satisfies "can end without violence" by construction (a fight-or-spare verb is deferred). The essence model deliberately casts no one new: it is the hollow's keeper, watched instead of fought, via an EnterTile storylet on the threshold tile that fires before any choice to enter: the recurring fight the player has known since cycle 2 is re-filed without adding a proper noun, which is the study's cheapest and strongest reveal shape. Walking away afterward completes the beat; the keeper needs never die. With both witnesses held, the Unbinder's reveal-tier-2 confrontation fires on the next conversation (priority above tier 1, gated on tier 1 having landed first, so the two reveals can never collapse into one visit), stating the threshold offer plainly and unlocking a persistent topic; the following crossing speaks the commission line bank once ever, completing the recovered memory the cycle-3 vision cut off mid-sentence. arcProgress grows to truth,guilt,vision,ledger,peace,cost,tier2,commission. Tier-3+ worldgen changed (a new person stands in deep worlds), so saves bump v6 to v7. Live-verified end to end on the real binary: last session's full key journal replayed byte-identically into world 2 (confirming the no-RNG-shift claim), then a real expedition climbed every rung through the cycle-5 crossing, including a tier-4 camp fight against six goblins that forced a 1-hp retreat, a shrine trip, and a rematch. Deferred: cycle 5's threshold, death-line register swaps, the trait deck, a fight-or-spare verb for Severed NPCs.

### D-039: The threshold: the last stair, the keeping's choice, register swaps, the local epilogue (2026-07-17)
The reveal ladder's final rung lands and the arc resolves (design/story/aegis-arc.md secs 6, 8, 9 hold the spoiler-level content; this entry stays structural). Tier 5+ worlds generate the arc's fixed final stage as a bottle site inside an otherwise normal procedural world: a fully authored corridor-and-chamber site with no carve RNG, no spawns, and no loot, placed on its own seed stream after every existing draw so pinned deep worlds keep their layouts. The site exists by tier because worldgen must stay a function of seed and tier; the readiness gate lives on the door instead, which stays shut until the cycle-4 crossing flag is held (the same pattern as the camp-keyed waygate, so rungs still gate on flags, never on cycle counts; a bearer who ignores the arc can stand on the stair at tier 20 and the door will still be waiting). The approach fires three staged Character-scoped beats keyed to corridor depth, and the arc sec 11 open question about the threshold's mechanical dressing is resolved as pure approach, no gatekeeper fight: cycle 4 just established that no Severed encounter mandates violence, and a mandatory kill at the door would have unsaid it. The choice itself is a proper journaled menu that opens on stepping to the site's heart, reopens freely until answered, and never reopens after; both answers write a single character-level Resolution flag and nothing else. The guardrail from arc sec 8 is enforced by a test that reflects over every snapshot field and requires the two branches identical except the narrative fields, and was verified live the same way on two pilot sessions. What the answers do change is register, permanently: death lines gain a worried second register once the ledger is known and a candid final register once the threshold is answered (the twice-deferred register-swap work, landed here where it belongs), crossings speak a per-answer final-register line from then on, the shrine rest line swaps, and the Unbinder gains a persistent per-answer topic that keeps the one permitted long thread alive without re-mystifying anything. The mandated final beat is honored structurally: after either answer the scene points the bearer home, and a witnessed stead epilogue storylet fires beside the houses, mechanically inert, with the Aegis silent in it and the closing line local (technique commitment 7: the mystery's resolution is never the last emotional note). arcProgress grows a kept or refused token. Tier-5+ worldgen changed and a new menu exists, so saves bump v7 to v8. Live-verified by reconstructing the full two-session key journal from transcripts (1781 keys), replaying it byte-identically into the recorded world-5 state on the new build, then walking the stair, the beats, both branches, and the epilogue through the pilot. Deferred: the covenant upgrade at resolution (needs covenants to exist), post-resolution steady-state content families beyond the crossing/death/rest/topic swaps, restoring Severed post-resolution, the trait deck.

### D-040: The deep content band: the quarry and its graven men, and story repeat-weighting (2026-07-17)
The second hostility-tier content band (D-033 landed the first). Motivation: the arc now marches every bearer to tier 5, and tiers 3+ differed from tier 2 only numerically (more goblins, tougher wights), which two consecutive live runs surfaced as sameness exactly where the game should read as descent. Tier 3+ worlds now hold the old quarry: an optional site on its own seed stream placed after every existing draw (pinned worlds keep their layouts), reading at a glance unlike both the camp's twisty cave and the barrow's linear passage: one open pit with scattered freestanding pillars. Its tenants are the graven men, the game's first ranged monster family and its first ambush family: half-finished stone figures that stand as statues until the bearer comes near in their line of sight, then hold their ground and hurl quarry-stone at telegraphed cells from range. Line of sight is real (Bresenham over the site map) and the pillars block it, so the fight introduces two new tactical verbs at once: approaching under fire by weaving through telegraphed cells, and using cover to break the throw and pull sleepers one at a time. They are stone-slow when they do walk (a step every third turn, slower than wights), adjacent they trade a heavy telegraphed fist against a weak scrape, and they pay out mostly essence, little coin. The pillar generator only places pillars whose eight neighbors are all open floor, so cover can never disconnect the pit: reachability holds by construction. Clearing the quarry writes its own deed with its own witnessed settlement reaction; like the barrow it is pure optional depth, and the waygate stays camp-keyed. The stead points at it through the crossing-arrival rumor and ambient beats, deliberately not a villager topic: talk menus cap at nine digits and tier-5 steadholders already sit at the cap (the D-037 lesson, applied). Riding along, the D-035 deferral: template selection now weighs against repeats. The crossing passes the finished world's story id into worldgen as a generation input (legitimate because it is itself a pure function of the seed lineage, never of player choice), and when more than one template is eligible the selection draw halves the previous template's weight. The weighted path still consumes exactly one draw, and the unweighted path is byte-identical to before, so direct-seed worlds, tier-1 worlds, and every test pin survive; only crossed tier-2+ worlds can differ. Tier-3+ worldgen changed and crossed worlds draw differently, so saves bump v8 to v9. Deferred: a third band for tiers 4+ (the quarry covers 3+ everywhere), graven variants in other sites, template-driven quarry dressing, and any player ranged verb to answer theirs.

### D-041: Gear v1: the smith, five pieces of iron, printed requirements, wear as the first auto-scaling sink (2026-07-17)
Equipment lands, the last unbuilt pillar of the classless identity (attributes and gear now both exist; skills remain). The model: two slots (weapon, armor) plus a pack, over an authored five-item catalog. The smith stocks the plain three (woodsman's axe, quilted jack, riveted shirt); the deep site chests each hold one signature piece as loot beyond coin (the D-033 deferral): the barrow's grave-iron blade, the quarry's carver's maul, and a bearer who already owns the twin leaves it where it lies, so five items exist per character, never five per world. Requirements follow the D-015 contract exactly: printed on the shop label and the pack menu before any coin moves, and falling short penalizes rather than blocks (half the bonus, an extra point of stamina per swing, and the smith says plainly that the iron will teach you what to become; the live run proved the loop by raising Vigor to meet the shirt it had bought early). Weapons add to every full swing; armor thins every hit taken, never below 1, so nothing makes being hit free. Wear is the D-025 auto-scaling sink made real: swinging wears the edge, turning a blow wears the jack (a hit armor could not reduce costs it nothing), a fully worn piece gives half its good and says so once, and the smith's repair prices each item off its own value (half the value at full wear), so wealth in iron taxes itself. Gear is banked per the death contract (the remnant still takes only coin and essence) and crosses whole with its wear, both already canon (vision secs 8, 10). The smith is a new NPC kind cast at every stead at every tier on its own seed stream after all existing draws (pinned layouts survive), with their own two-topic menu so the villagers' nine digits stay unbreached, and a placement rule found the hard way: the forge may not take any neighbor's last open cardinal tile, because the first live seed walled the steadholder in. Two lessons came out of the live session: menu digits must never shift under a buyer's fingers (sold stock now stays listed as owned instead of vanishing; the pre-fix build sold this bearer a riveted shirt they were aiming a digit past), and the D-040 combat lesson generalizes (striking through a telegraph eats the blow at any tier; armor turned a 7 into a 3 and made the mistake survivable). The 'i' key opens the pack anywhere; menus stay turn-free. Saves bump v9 to v10 (a person and a key gained meaning at every tier). Deferred: weapon movesets as verbs (D-004's spears and arcs), a player ranged weapon (D-040's deferral stands), gear in the Legend or mythology pipes (a dead bearer's iron in a future world's tomb), stat requirements beyond Might and Vigor, and any crafting lane.

### D-042: Skills v1: four use-grown skills, derived levels, the sheet (2026-07-17)
The use-based track lands, completing the classless triad (attributes D-015, gear D-041, and now skills per D-014/D-016). Scope is deliberately the first slice of the draft eighteen: the four skills today's verbs can actually feed. Blades, Hafted, and Brawling split the melee swing by what is truly in hand (the axe and maul are Hafted, the grave-iron blade is Blades, bare fists are Brawling; GearItem carries its family), and Warding is armor-craft, fed only by blows the worn iron actually turns. The core representation choice: counted uses are the only state and levels are derived from them, never granted, so a skill can only ever reflect what the bearer actually did (this makes D-016's "skills never respec" true by construction rather than by policy) and save-journal replay is trivially exact. The curve asks four more uses per level (8, 20, 36, 56, 80...): diminishing, never zero, returns. Cost-gating is by construction, not bookkeeping: a full swing already pays stamina and edge-wear, a turned blow already pays armor-wear, and the two free actions teach nothing (a winded feeble swing costs nothing and counts for nothing; a raw-1 bite passes the min-1 floor untouched and teaches Warding nothing). The live run surfaced a pleasing invariant: on fresh gear, Hafted uses equal weapon wear and Warding uses equal armor wear, because training and wear are the same events. Effect kept deliberately light for v1: +1 flat per two levels (damage for the weapon family in hand, absorb for Warding while any armor is worn), so skill seasons a build the way gear and attributes carry it, and no track dominates. Level rises speak in the log per family; the Aegis remarks exactly once, at the first rise ever, that this ledger is not its doing: the one growth track it does not govern, planting the two-track fiction. The 'c' key opens the sheet anywhere, turn-free like every menu: both ledgers on one page, seven attributes and four skills with progress toward the next line. Skills live on the Player, so death banking and whole crossings need no new code, and no worldgen or RNG stream was touched: bonuses are additive after existing rolls, so every pinned world and test seed survives untouched. Saves bump v10 to v11 ('c' gained meaning, and swings now change later damage). The live loop on seed 42: Brawling rose to 1 at exactly its 8th counted swing mid-camp with the one-time Aegis aside; the axe then trained Hafted only; Warding rose to 1 precisely on an 8th turned blow (a crushing blow eaten through a telegraph, the recurring lesson); the ledger crossed to world 2 whole; the sheet rendered both fresh and trained. Deferred: perks at thresholds (D-016's choose-1-of-2-3, the natural Skills v2 once thresholds are being reached), the remaining fourteen-ish skills as their activities land (Ranged and Polearms need weapons, craft/wilderness/subterfuge/social need their verbs), telegraph clarity from weapon familiarity (D-004's knowledge-sharpened telegraphs), and any diegetic training surface (sparring partners, dummies).

### D-043: The names pass: the working names are the names (2026-07-17)
Arc sec 11's standing open item (final names for the forge-name, the Shieldwrights, the Chain, the Hearth, the Severed, the Unbinder) is resolved, and the resolution is that the working names are canon. Rationale: the game's register was set long ago by its own diction (stead, barrow, hollow, quarry, wight, waygate: spare, concrete, Anglo-Saxon), and in that register plain weighted English is what authored names sound like; the genre reference points the vision cites name their entities exactly this way (the Tarnished, the Erdtree, the First Flame). Coined words are reserved for the two places they read as names rather than vocabulary: persons and made things, meaning the forge-name "Skeld" (D-037's working pick, now final: short, shield-rooted, a maker's plain word for a made shield) and the generated NPC name pools. Two authored name-facts turn the keeps from defaults into fiction, both text-only additions to existing surfaces (no new topics, no key gained meaning, no save bump): the Aegis admits in the cycle-3 vision that Shieldwrights is its own word for its makers and their name for themselves has not come back (the worn-memory motif doing naming work), and the Unbinder's reveal-tier-2 answer states they laid their name down with everything else, a name being a binding too (namelessness as the character's own proudest act, and the deliberate mirror of the Aegis recovering its name: of the two constants, one got a name back and one laid one down). The Chain and the Hearth already read correctly in shipped lines ("down the chain" plain in the ledger crossing; "The Hearth burns alone"). All placeholder hedges are removed from the arc doc and code comments, so future story content builds on settled names with no rename debt accruing. Deferred, related but distinct: generated proper names for worlds and regions (a name-generator feature, not a rename; today only the settlement carries a generated name), and the Unbinder's constant guise tells from arc sec 11.

### D-044: The tiers-4+ band: the fallen hall and its iron hounds (2026-07-17)
The third hostility-tier content band (D-033 landed tier 2, D-040 tier 3). Motivation: the arc marches every bearer to tier 5, and past the quarry the last leg went numeric again, exactly where the descent should read strangest. Tier 4+ worlds now hold the fallen hall: an optional site on its own seed streams placed after every existing draw including the smith's (pinned worlds keep their layouts exactly), reading at a glance unlike the camp's warren, the barrow's passage, and the quarry's open pit: one great roofless room behind a porch a single body wide, two side chambers behind door-slots just as narrow, and roof-fall rubble placed under the quarry's eight-open-neighbors rule so cover can never disconnect the room. Its tenants are the iron hounds, the game's first pack family: made watch-beasts that outlived whoever fed them, individually the weakest monsters in the deep bands (bites of 2-3) and collectively its fastest, the first family to walk at the bearer's own speed (BFS every turn; wights step every second turn, graven men every third). The pack IS the mechanic: a hound's bite grows a point for every packmate also beside the bearer, and its one heavy telegraph, the throat-lunge, is only attempted while a packmate holds the bearer's attention, so the counterplay is ground rather than feet: in the porch or a door-slot only one hound can reach you, and a hound that cannot flank never lunges. The single-file denial needs no special AI: full-speed BFS around occupied tiles queues the pack at the mouth by construction, and the live run surfaced the intended second lesson for free (the slot mouth still exposes both diagonals; one cell deeper is true single file). The coffer holds the catalog's sixth piece and its first deep armor, the wright's mail (armor 3, Vigor 9, the requirement ladder's next rung), under D-041's twin rule. Clearing writes its own deed with its own witnessed stead answer; the stead points at the hall through the crossing-arrival rumor, never a topic (the nine-digit lesson, applied third time running). Riding along, an arc sec 4 debt: the motif-inscription ambient anchor lands as an EnterTile storylet deep in the hall, once per world, read aloud and left unexplained. Tier-4+ worldgen changed, so saves bump v11 to v12; tiers 1-3 draw nothing new and replay byte-identically. Live-verified end to end on the real binary: a journey bot played worlds 1-3 legitimately through ApplyKey (three camps, shrine raises, three crossings, zero deaths) and wrote its journal as a v12 save; a live pilot session loaded it and fought the first hall: the pack queued at the porch, four hounds fell at the slot for the cost of one death to the fifth taken mid-room bare-fisted, the corpse run reclaimed the remnant inside the hall, and the mail's grant line turned out to be the maul's (the flavor switch's default arm), fixed and tested on the spot. Deferred: what tier-5+ worlds add structurally beyond the threshold site, hound variants outside the hall, and the player ranged verb (D-040's deferral stands).

### D-045: Steady state v1: the laying-down, the argument, hearth-signs, the long song (2026-07-17)
Post-resolution cycles get their promised content (arc sec 9, the D-039 deferral) as four small families, each a complete story, none a new mystery. The headline is the game's first post-resolution verb: bumping the hollow's keeper as a resolved bearer opens a journaled choice instead of a swing: the old way, or lay it down gently. The gentle path pays nothing: the keeper is unmade with its count spoken aloud, the site clears, the same deed lands in different words, and where the closed count goes is register, never a number (the sec 8 guardrail holds: both resolutions get the same verb at the same price; choosing the old way closes the moment for that world, and tests cover both paths plus the step-back). Second, the one permitted long thread: the Unbinder argument resumes as sequenced Talk beats, at most one per cycle by construction (each beat stamps the cycle it fired in and the next requires a later one), opening per answer, drawing once, and settling into an unhurried steady shape with no one conceding; the bearer's first laying-down preempts the thread with its own beat, the standing irony from arc sec 7 surfaced at last (the mender's trade, shared). Third, hearth-signs: deep worlds (tier 4+) read the answer back once per world, in text only: a rest line for keepers, a waygate line for refusers. Fourth, the long song: the crossing now records every finished world on the character and writes a compounding song fact from the third world on; a once-ever stead beat sings the whole descent back garbled (wrong order, one world too many) while the final register corrects it gently: the mythology pipe (D-013) compounding for the first time. The hermit also answers the resolution, per answer, once. Mechanical footprint deliberately tiny: no worldgen draw changed anywhere, so every pinned world and test seed survives byte-identical; the new surface is the laying menu plus the crossing's fact, and saves bump v12 to v13. Tests 148 to 158. Live-verified end to end: the journey bot grew to climb the whole reveal ladder legitimately (worlds 1 through 5, every rung asserted, one death to the tier-5 camp, the threshold answered Kept, 1470 keys, replay-exact), then a live pilot session played the steady state on the real binary: the kept hearth-sign at two worlds' shrines, the long song sung with a genuinely repeated world name in it (the name pool collided across cycles 1 and 3, and the song wore the repeat well), the laying-down chosen mid-pursuit with the menu freezing the fight, the trade-shared beat preempting the argument's opening exactly as the priorities intend, the hermit's answer at a far fire, and a fresh load replaying all 1610 turns to identical state. Options considered and set aside: per-world repeating dramatic beats (verbatim repetition reads as broken authoring, so the families are character-scoped stories over world-scoped ambient), pricing the laying-down in the Unbinder's per-world unbindings (couples the verb to the respec pool for no fictional gain), and paying the gentle path the fight's essence (mercy would strictly dominate; the price is the point). Deferred: restoring a Severed (arc sec 9's rare authored case), argument beats beyond stage three, a refused-side live run (unit-covered; the guardrail test still reflects over every snapshot field), hearth-signs inside sites, and the long song surfacing in talk topics.

### D-046: Skills v2: knacks at thresholds, the sheet answers (2026-07-17)
The perk half of D-016 lands, under an in-register name: knacks. When a skill crosses level 2 its training settles into a question of style: two mutually exclusive answers, put by the sheet ('c') one question at a time, answered with a digit, chosen forever (skills never respec, and neither does the way you learned them). Every v1 question pits doing more against spending less. Blades offers the drawn cut (blades cut 1 deeper) or the spare motion (blade swings ask 1 less wind); Hafted the follow-through (a hafted killing swing hands 2 wind back) or the kind grip (the edge wears only every second counted swing); Brawling knuckle and bone (bare fists hit 2 harder) or the deep breath (2 more wind, always: the one knack that pays any build, against one that pays only commitment); Warding the braced shoulder (worn iron turns telegraphed blows 2 further) or the mended strap (armor wears only every second turned blow). Structure: pending questions are pure derived state (catalog times uses times chosen list), so nothing new needs saving, resetting, or death handling; the rise that crosses a threshold announces the question once, and it stands open forever until answered; answering keeps the sheet open so a second waiting question is put immediately. Effects are deterministic flat modifiers and parity-clocked wear skips, no new RNG draws, so no stream drifts and no worldgen changed: every pinned world and test seed survives byte-identical. The wear-skip knacks deliberately break D-042's pleasing uses-equal-wear invariant on fresh gear: that was an observation, never a contract, and the divergence is the knack visibly doing its job. The Aegis remarks exactly once at the first knack taken, extending D-042's two-track fiction: the body's ledger keeps choices now, not only counts. The sheet's digits gained meaning and chosen knacks change later damage, wind, and wear, so saves bump v13 to v14. Tests 158 to 169. Live-verified end to end: the journey bot's full-ladder journal replayed byte-identically into a v14 save; the loaded bearer turned out to be a five-world bare-fist brawler (Brawling 5, 84 counted swings) with the brawling question pending, the sheet put it, knuckle and bone was taken with the one-time Aegis aside, and the perked fists dropped five tier-5 iron hounds at the fallen hall's porch, two punches to a hound throughout; a quit and reload replayed all 1550 turns, knack included, to identical state. Options set aside: opening the choice in a popup at the moment of the rise (menus freeze fights, and the moment belongs to the log), pricing knacks in essence (the threshold is the price: cost-gated uses already bought it), and Unbinder respec for knacks (D-016 says attributes only, and the fiction holds: the body is not the Aegis's to loosen). Deferred: second questions at level 4+ (the catalog carries arbitrary skill-and-level groups already), three-option questions (the sheet box fits two today), knacks for skills that do not yet exist, and the proficiency track (the other half of D-016).

### D-047: Covenants v1: oaths at the waygate, the terms of the crossing (2026-07-17)
The covenant half of D-011 lands under an in-register name: oaths, sworn at the waygate. Pressing '>' at an open gate now opens the terms menu instead of crossing at once: digits swear or unswear oaths onto the NEXT world, '>' again crosses under whatever stands sworn (none is always allowed and always plain), and any other key steps back with the selection let go. Four oaths at launch, one per pillar, every one a penalty and nothing else: the crowded dark (every den holds one more tenant: a generation input on spawn counts, stepping past the tier caps), the hungry road (bread costs double, multiplying whatever the fact-derived price already was), the spent edge (every wear event moves the ledger by two, edge and straps alike; the kind grip and the mended strap still skip whole events), and the slow mending (the death-wound holds 160 turns instead of 80: D-011's magnitude-never-shape clause, exactly). Oaths are the world's terms, not the character's: they live on the World as a generation input beside tier and prevStory (player choice as a worldgen input is legitimate because the choosing keys are journaled before Generate is ever called), and they lapse at the far gate, so nothing needs death handling or crossing resets. The summed weight is the visible Threat score (D-011), called the burden. What a burden buys is never power: 10 Legend per point when the oath-bound world is completed at its far gate, and the echo fact crosses with "under oath" in it, so the next world's songs remember the harder walking (D-013's pipe carrying challenge as legend). The arc sec 8 resolution upgrade rides along as register only: the menu's opening line is per-answer (the old terms read out before resolution; the keeper naming the kindling's terms; the refuser answering to no one), same menu, same oaths, same prices, and the sec 8 reflection guardrail test passes untouched. First worlds never have oaths: nothing is sworn before the first gate (the Hades pattern: the pact opens after a clear). No-oath generation draws exactly what it always did, so every pinned world and test seed survives; the gate's digits gained meaning and the crossing became two keys, so saves bump v14 to v15. Tests 169 to 180. Live-verified end to end: the journey bot now swears two oaths crossing out of world 2, cleared the crowded tier-3 camp without dying, and had its burden honored at the far gate (Legend 72 to 131: 39 coin plus 20 for the burden), its 1492-key journal replaying exact; a live pilot session then loaded the resolved keeper, took the pending brawling knack, opened the terms in the kept register, swore all four, and crossed into a tier-6 world where every number answered (burden 4, bread 8, 28 tenants: one more in each of four dens), punched two of the seven crowded-camp goblins down at a corridor mouth, withdrew at 14 hp, and cold-reloaded to an identical 1590-turn state, menu keys and fight included. One render fix found live: the first oath's menu line ran two columns past the box border; the blurb was trimmed to fit. Options set aside: choosing covenants at character creation for world 1 (nothing is sworn before the first gate, and the teaching world stays clean), paying oath rewards in coin or essence (adjacent to raw power; D-011 says legend and cosmetic only), oaths persisting until unsworn (per-world lapse keeps each crossing a fresh ritual; a standing pact can layer on later without a format change), and eased-world anti-oaths for docked Legend (breaks never-raw-power from the other side). Deferred: more oaths as their systems land (weather, factions, anchoring, companions), oath-tiered Legend boons (Legend still has no spend), covenant-pinned template selection (templates sec 9's open question), and the burden surfacing in villager talk.

### D-048: Legend v1: standing, the songs' titles, and the welcome (2026-07-17)
The boon half of D-011's Legend meta-layer lands, and its center is the representation choice D-042 proved: Legend stays the only state (minted at crossings, never spent, never drained), and standing is DERIVED from it on a square curve (25, 100, 225, 400, 625; capped at five rungs), so the meta-layer needs no new saved state, no death handling, and no crossing resets, and diminishing returns hold by construction: each rung costs more worlds than the last, at the research's bounding exponent. Spending was considered and set aside: renown is not a purse, and a spend would have made Legend a fourth currency racing coin and essence instead of the ledger above them. Each standing carries a title in the songs' own words (a name in one song; a name at the hearths; a storied bearer; a walking song; the songs' own), announced at the crossing where Legend is minted, which is the only place standing can rise, so the announcement has exactly one home; the Aegis remarks exactly once at the first rise, completing the three-ledger fiction begun in D-042 and extended in D-046: the body's counts, the body's choices, and now a ledger no body keeps, which the Aegis cannot read but only hear sung. The boons answer the vision's first-hour test and are hospitality, never combat power: the welcome (bread set out by the shrine stone at each arrival, one loaf per standing up to three, never past the ration cap: the songs walked ahead, and the stead answers them in bread), the hearth-price (the steadholder takes a coin off bread from standing two, before the hungry road doubles what remains, so oath and boon compose legibly), and the menders' honor (a fourth unbinding per world from standing four); the fifth rung is the crown title and nothing else, so the ladder visibly ends. First worlds always owe nothing: Legend is minted only at crossings, so standing is zero before the first gate, the mirror of D-047's clean first world. Oaths ride on the world and boons ride on the character, and the two halves of D-011 now meet in one loop: the burden pays Legend, Legend sets out bread, and a storied bearer who swears the hungry road pays (base - 1) * 2, every step printed. No worldgen draw changed anywhere, so every pinned world and test seed survives byte-identical; but prices, rations, and unbindings now read Legend, so an old journal would replay to different state and saves bump v15 to v16. Tests 180 to 190. Live-verified end to end: the journey bot asserts the weighing and the welcome at every crossing (standing 1 at legend 72 entering the oath-bound world, standing 2 at 131 leaving it with bread down to 3, the welcome scaling with standing and capping at five carried, bread at 5 in a standing blight world), its 1492-key journal replaying exact; a live pilot session then loaded the resolved keeper, read the title under Legend in the sidebar and on the sheet beside the still-pending brawling question, saw the steadholder offer bread at the discounted 5, refuse a sixth loaf at the cap, and refuse an idle bite at full health, and a cold reload replayed all 1460 turns to identical state. Options set aside: a spendable Legend shop (above), boons as combat power (the vision's never-trivialize clause; power is already paid through essence and gear), per-title one-shot gifts (a rung should behave like a rung wherever the save is, not like a coupon), and villager greetings swapping by standing (talk registers are template prose today; it belongs with the per-role voice work). Deferred: the Hall of Legends as a place (D-025's patronage deeds want an economy to patronize first), rungs past five, standing surfacing in villager talk, and oath-tiered boons.

### D-049: Names v2: the weave and the walked list (2026-07-17)
The name-generator feature D-043 deferred, forced up the queue by evidence: the world-name pool was 1,800 combinations, a character's worlds 1 and 3 both drew Pelildor on the pinned master seed, and D-045's long song then sang the repeat back every run (the one surface where a name collision reads as broken authoring rather than coincidence). Two changes, one per problem. First, the weave itself: worlds now draw from their own opener pool (24 openers no stead or person shares), so no world sounds like its own steadholder and the world-tongue is distinct by construction; all pools grew (15 middles, 22 stead closers, 16 world closers, 16 person closers); every kind weaves in two shapes (the full three-syllable weave and a short compound: Thrumalund beside Corstad, Kerihythe beside Fenford, Pelardric beside Dunred) drawn from the same stream; and a seam guard rerolls any name with a doubled letter where syllables meet, which for worlds means no doubled letter anywhere, since no world syllable carries one internally. Closer pools stay disjoint across kinds, so a world can never share a full name with a stead or a person. Second, the guarantee the pool alone cannot give: WorldGen.Generate takes the character's walked-world names as a generation input, and the world weave rerolls against them (bounded at 64 attempts, the last draw standing so the weave never fails). The taken list is Player.WorldsWalked, which D-045 already maintains and which is journal-derived, so determinism and replay-exactness hold exactly as they do for tier, prevStory, and oaths; the reroll consumes only the names stream, so placement, story, and combat draws never shift. First worlds pass no list and draw free, the third mirror of the clean first world (no oaths sworn, no standing owed, no verses yet sung). Uniqueness is per character because the long song is per character: two different bearers may both walk an Eldador, and that is the mythology working, not failing. Every generated name changed, and names land in snapshots, facts, and the song, so saves bump v16 to v17; maps, spawns, and story selection are untouched. Tests 190 to 195 (seam cleanliness across 500 draws, the reroll surviving 300 sequential draws with certainty-level collision pressure, disjoint tongues, determinism per stream, and five crossings on one character never repeating a verse). Live-verified end to end: the journey bot added a verse-unsung rung at every crossing and walked Thrumamora, Gormedun, Eldador, Randimar, Corstad (five distinct openers, both shapes represented) with every D-047 and D-048 rung still green and its 1,466-key journal replaying exact; a live pilot session loaded the v17 save and read Corstad and Kerucombe in the header at turn 1,432, identical state throughout. Options set aside: a hand-authored name list (infinite NG+ outlasts any list), global cross-character uniqueness (above: the repeat across characters is a feature), dedupe by epithet ("the second Pelildor" is the collision wearing a costume), and naming regions or biomes now (no region entity exists to hang a name on). Deferred: culture-flavored pools keyed to the race list (D-017), region names when regions become entities, and world epithets earned through the mythology pipe rather than drawn at birth.

### D-050: The bow and the loosed line: the bearer's ranged verb (2026-07-17)
The player ranged weapon lands (the D-040 deferral, standing through four decisions: the graven men have thrown since tier 3 and nothing answered). The weapon is the hunting bow, the smith's fourth ware (bowyer's work the forge takes in trade, so the forge stays a forge), and it is the catalog's first Grace-gated iron, answering D-041's requirements-beyond-Might-and-Vigor deferral: Grace 7 on a baseline of 5, printed on the label like every asking. It hangs in its own gear slot, so the axe never leaves the other hand, wielding costs nothing new, and death banking and whole crossings need no new code (AllGear simply grew a slot). The verb is two journaled keys: 'f' sets the shaft turn-free like a menu, the next direction key looses along one of the eight lines, and anything else lowers the bow free. The shaft stops at the first body or the first stone and reaches eight cells: one short of a graven man's throw, so the pit is never outranged from safety, and the pillars break the loosed line exactly as they break the hurled one; the counterplay the quarry taught now cuts both ways. Costs follow the swing's grammar: three wind, one more under the unmet asking (D-015's tax, visible live), but a winded draw refuses outright where a winded swing lands feebly, because at range keeping the shaft costs only tempo; and wear is the bow's whole ammunition: every draw frays the string, hit or miss, a worn string throws half, and the smith restrings it like any edge, so there is no arrow inventory and the sink recurs by use (D-025). The Ranged skill (D-016's draft list, the fifth counted ledger) grows only on shafts that find a body: stone and distance school no one, so wall-grinding cannot train it; Grace pays a flat aim bonus mirroring Might's melee bonus, and the level-2 threshold opens its question in D-046's shape (the hunter's eye, shafts strike 1 deeper, against the light draw, a loose asks 1 less wind). One guardrail: a shaft at the hollow's keeper from a resolved bearer is choosing the old way, and the Aegis says so as the moment closes ("the old way has a reach"), so the D-045 choice is never bypassed silently. No worldgen draw changed anywhere, so every pinned world and test seed survives byte-identical; 'f' gained meaning and the smith's menu digits shifted by one ware, so saves bump v17 to v18. Tests 195 to 204. Live-verified end to end: the journey bot buys the bow in world 2 and looses on every clean line thereafter, reaching the threshold with 17 marks struck and its 1,520-key journal replaying exact (bow wear equal to uses, the D-042 invariant echoing: a line-checked shaft never misses); a live pilot session then loaded the v18 save, walked to the tier-5 quarry, and fought the intended duel: a sleeper woken by a shaft at six cells, a telegraphed stone dodged by feet, a third shaft raising Ranged to 2 at exactly its 20th mark, both pending knack questions answered through the sheet in queue order, and a cold reload replaying all 1,549 turns to identical state. Options set aside: arrows as inventory (bookkeeping the game refuses everywhere else; wear already is the consumable), a thrown stone needing no gear (every other verb is bought or grown; the bow should be both), the bow in the weapon slot (menus are turn-free, so slot competition would have been fiction; a real second slot is honest about it), and outranging the graven men (their extra cell is the band's identity).
