Deferred: a signature deep ranged weapon in some future site's chest, weapon movesets (D-004, still), monster kinds that punish archery (shields, chargers), and the Ranged level-4 question (D-046's second tier, whenever any skill gets one).

### D-051: The terms grown and the terms felt: three oaths, and the stead's own voice (2026-07-17)
The two deferrals D-047 and D-048 left standing in the same room land together: the oath catalog grows its second wave, and the burden and the standing stop being numbers only the sidebar knows. Three new terms at the waygate. The old blood (every blow lands 1 deeper) is the first oath priced at weight 2, because it bites in every fight of the world rather than at a purchase or a den count, and the burden scale finally says aloud that terms are not all one size; it lands on the raw blow before iron, so armor still earns its keep against it. The lean dark (essence comes up halved, rounded against the bearer) is the first oath against progression itself; coin is untouched, because it was never the dark's to give. And the hushed name (the songs fall silent): the deed's echo is not written into the sworn world at all, so nothing there can hum it or sing it, no welcome bread is set out, the hearth-price discount and the menders' honor are withheld, and the arrival says plainly that no song came ahead. The hushed name is the slow mending's mirror: one costs nothing to the careful, the other costs nothing to the unsung, and both are terms a bearer grows into; the long song still sings in a hushed world, because the children's garbled walking-song is myth about the walked worlds, not the stead knowing its guest. The surfacing rides what already exists, because the villager menu sits at its nine-digit cap (the third design that rule has steered): the raids answer gains a sentence under the crowded dark (the stead lives in the oath-bound world too), the Old songs answer now scales with standing (at one rung the singer half-suspects; at three, no one pretends; in a hushed world the topic silences itself, since the fact it reads was never written), and two storylets do the rest: the-hard-season (once per oath-bound world, by the houses: a villager counts short sacks, and the Aegis names whose terms hold over the season, the count honoring what is carried without asking who else carries it) and the-known-face (once per character, the first villager spoken to at standing 2 or higher: the stead knows the bearer before a word is said, and the Aegis calls a door opened before knocking a kind of debt). No worldgen draw changed anywhere (the three new oaths act at combat, payout, and crossing time), so every pinned world and test seed survives byte-identical; but digits 5-7 at the crossing menu gained meaning and a hushed world is born holding different facts, so saves bump v18 to v19. Tests 204 to 212. Live-verified end to end: the journey bot now swears the old blood and the lean dark out of world 3 (burden 3 honored for 30 Legend at the far gate, with a new burden-honored rung asserting the arithmetic at every crossing), was greeted by name in world 4 at standing 2, and felt the lean dark plainly (10 essence banked in world 4 against 25-30 in every other), its 1,678-key journal replaying exact; a live pilot session then loaded the v19 save at the resolved keeper, read all seven terms in the box, swore the hushed name and the old blood into a tier-6 world, and watched every surface answer: the stranger's arrival, the discount withheld at standing 3 (bread 4, not 3), the hard season spoken by the byre with the Aegis naming the terms, the steadholder's menu missing Old songs and greeting a storied bearer as a stranger, and a cold reload replaying all 1,647 turns to field-identical state. Options set aside: an oath against skill growth (a counted ledger that counts nothing is a shape change, not a magnitude), an oath suspending shrine rest or rations outright (that removes a safety anchor instead of pricing it), villager greetings swapping registers by standing (per-role voice work, as D-048 already ruled), and new talk topics for burden or standing (the nine-digit cap; and a menu that grows and shrinks by state is a menu whose digits lie). Deferred: oaths for systems still unbuilt (weather, factions, companions), oath-tiered boons and covenant-pinned templates (still), a standing pact that persists until unsworn, and world epithets or reputation carrying differently once regions exist.

### D-052: Lessons: the proficiency track, shown by the stead's own hands (2026-07-17)
The proficiency half of D-016 lands, the oldest still-open half-decision, under an in-register name: lessons. Where skills are counted and knacks are chosen, a lesson is shown: discrete know-how put into the bearer's hands by someone who knows, kept for good, banked on the Player like skills and gear (death never takes it; it crosses waygates whole). Three lessons at launch, one per mentor, each acquired a different way, which is the track's real contract being proved. The gleaning (the woodward's, 10 coin, a teaching entry on the talk menu): every world scatters gleaning spots on forest tiles at generation, on their own seed stream after every other draw, placed whether or not anyone can see them, and only a taught bearer sees the marks and gathers a ration by stepping on one (know-how literally making the world legible; worldgen never reads the character, so determinism is untouched and pinned worlds keep their layouts). The tended iron (the smith's, 15 coin, a teaching entry after the wares so the repair digit never shifts): resting settles every piece's wear back to half, never past, so the fully-worn half-good penalty becomes avoidable by discipline while the wheel keeps the deep half of the sink whole. And the clean dressing (the herbwife's, priced at nothing but taught only through her hands: the first bought mending shows it, so a bearer who never falls or never pays never learns): eating while wounded also tends the wound, sixteen turns per ration, which is her own rate at the plain price of bread, so the convenience is the good and the arithmetic is hers; the meal is refused to the whole of body but never to a taught wounded bearer. Teaching entries follow the D-041 menu rule (a learned lesson stays listed as yours already; refusals never take coin), and the nine digits hold everywhere: the fullest villager list is eight topics, the woodward sells nothing else, and the herbwife's menu is untouched at its exact nine. The sheet gains a Taught row, the fourth ledger, and the Aegis marks it exactly once, completing the fiction begun in D-042: counts, choices, songs, and now what another's hands put into yours, none of it its doing. No RNG stream shifts anywhere (the gleanings stream is new and placed last), so every pinned world and test seed survives; mentor digits gained meaning, taught steps gather what a v19 walk stepped past, and rest and meals do new work, so saves bump v19 to v20. Tests 212 to 221, first run green. Live-verified end to end: the journey bot buys the gleaning in world 3 (gathering a third ration on the walk home) and the tending in world 4 (its bow honestly under half, so the rest had nothing to do and said so), reaches the threshold with all rungs green and its 1,698-key journal replaying exact; along the way it confirmed a pleasing D-032 interaction live: world 4's steadholder was the Raided Stead's plaintiff, her plea outranked the known face at that bump exactly as template beats are supposed to, and the greeting simply waited for the next villager. A live pilot session then played the whole track on the real binary: the Taught row on the sheet, the cap refusal at a marked spot, the string frayed past half in the barrow and eaten against while winded, a deliberate fall that dropped the remnant on the barrow ladder, the untaught refusal, the corpse run inside the wound clock, the mend at 13 coin that taught the dressing (the Aegis correctly silent, its line spent two worlds ago), the rest that took the string from 21 to 20 and no further, a second fall answered by two meals easing 80 to 63 to 46, a gleaning gathered under the cap, and a cold reload replaying all 1,777 turns to field-identical state. Options set aside: a proficiency progress track (lessons are binary know-how; gradual growth is the skill ladder's job, and two counted tracks would blur into one), teaching entries on the steadholder or herbwife menus (the nine digits; the herbwife sits at exactly nine while wounded), lessons as standing boons (D-048 boons are hospitality; teaching is a trade between hands), and book-taught lessons now (D-016 promises books where literacy pays off, and shipping writing before Lore exists would burn that payoff). Deferred: books and quest-granted lessons (with literacy and template payoffs), more mentor lessons as their systems land (a cooking lesson wants a crafting lane), regional gleaning variety, and lessons as gates on future recipes and techniques.

### D-053: The ringfort and the old watch: shield-carls, war-boars, and the bow answered (2026-07-17)
Two deferrals standing in the same room land together: D-050's monster kinds that punish archery (shields, chargers), and D-044's open question of what tier-5+ worlds add structurally beyond the threshold site. One site pays both. Tier 5+ worlds now hold the ringfort, the fourth hostility-tier content band: an optional site on its own streams placed after every existing draw and before the gleanings (whose forest check must see its gate tile), reading at a glance unlike the camp's warren, the barrow's passage, the quarry's pit, and the hall's great room: two ring-walls with the lanes between them, the outer wall the map's own border, the inner rampart whole but for one gate never on the entry's side, so reaching the ward means walking long straight courtyard lanes. The garrison is the archery answer, in two kinds because the bow can be beaten from two sides. The shield-carls are the shields: the linden board stops a loosed shaft dead (the wind and the string are spent, nothing is bought, and the Ranged ledger learns nothing, since a shaft that finds no body has never taught), except in the windows the carl's own fighting opens: while its telegraphed seax-stab is declared the board has left its line, and for two blown turns after the blow resolves (hit or miss) it stands open, neither stepping nor striking. So archery against the watch is timing, not spam, and up close the axe simply comes under the board: the carl melees like any patient dead thing and walks a drilled BFS step every other turn. The war-boars are the chargers: given a clear straight lane and three to eight cells of run-up they wheel onto it and come, and the whole run is marked, not one cell, because the lane is dodged sideways and never backward (the resolution runs the length of the line and takes the bearer wherever on it they still stand). The symmetry is the design's point: the clean lines a bowman wants are the lanes the boars run, so range is never safety in the fort. A missed charge leaves the boar blown for two turns (the same exposure state, one field on Monster serving both kinds), and a boar beside you cannot charge at all: closing is the counterplay, where the tusks can only worry. Spawns: four carls plus one per tier past 5 capped at six (the crowded dark adds its one here), two boars always; carls at 14 hp plus tier scaling, boars at 20. The arms-chest holds the catalog's eighth piece and the deep ranged signature D-050 deferred: the yew warbow (aim 4, Grace 9, the requirement ladder's next rung), with the irony stated in its own comment: the boards stop it too, because the answer to the answer is a heavier draw, never an exemption. A felled boar pays no coin at all: the knife takes a ration if a walking body can hold one, the game's first foe that pays in bread's own coin, and at the cap the rest goes to the crows. Clearing writes the watch_relieved deed with its own witnessed stead answer (the drover and the good grass); the crossing-arrival rumor points at the fort in the stead's oldest counsel (the nine-digit lesson, applied a fourth time: never a topic); and a tally-wall EnterTile beat deep in the ward tells the deep bands' one recurring story, duty outliving its master, in scratches. Tier-1-4 worlds draw nothing new and replay byte-identically; tier-5+ maps changed under old journals (a gate stands where a v20 walk crossed plain ground, and the gleanings settle differently around it), so saves bump v20 to v21. Tests 221 to 233, first run green. Live-verified end to end: the journey bot gained four fort rungs in world 5 (the gate on the map, the garrison both kinds, the fact in the graph, the oldest counsel spoken at arrival), its 1,698-key journal replaying exact into a fresh v21 save; a live pilot session then fought the fort whole on the real binary with every proof landing on the rehearsed numbers: the describe line at the gate, a deliberate shaft stopped by a walking carl's board, a window shaft striking an open carl, three charges dodged sideways off the fully marked lane, a blown boar shafted where it stood, a ration eaten mid-fight to make room, meat taken at four carried and left at the cap, two falls to the watch with corpse runs back in (the fort is deliberately the hardest band yet for a bare-fisted bearer), the deed at the last tenant, the warbow strung from the pack with its unmet asking printed, the stead's answer by the well, a second remnant reclaimed from the quiet fort, and a cold reload replaying all 1,958 turns to field-identical state. Options set aside: the shield as damage reduction rather than a stop with windows (a smaller number is invisible in a log; a stopped shaft with a stated window is a readable rule), a minimum range on the bow (that would punish owning the verb instead of leaning on it), knockback on the charge (positions are sacred: the dodge is by feet before the run resolves, never physics after), and boars paying coin (a beast carries no purse, and the meat is the better sentence). Deferred: carl and boar variants outside the fort, a shielded kind that also throws (board plus sling is a tier-6+ candidate), the Ranged level-4 knack question (still), and what tier-6+ worlds add structurally now that every arc tier has its band.

### D-054: The songhall and the patron's deeds: the third ledger gets a roof (2026-07-17)
Two deferrals standing in the same room land together again: D-048's Hall of Legends as a place, and D-025's patronage crossing (coin converting into Legend and into facts later worlds encounter). One building pays both, under an in-register name: the songhall. Every stead keeps one, at every tier, standing at the settlement's edge on its own seed stream placed after every existing draw (its door claims a grass tile, so the gleanings' forest check never moves and pinned layouts survive untouched but for that one tile and one new person). Inside is one fully authored room, the counter-room to the deep bands: the only interior where nothing fights back and everything keeps. Door west, a bare plinth beside it, the long hearth at the middle, the east wall scored with the year's verses; what varies between worlds is never the room but what the bearer's patronage has put in it, read at runtime off the character, so worldgen never learns whose songs these are and the no-character-reads invariant comes out of this decision stronger, not weaker: no new Generate parameter at all. At the door stands the skald (the fifth NPC kind, with the smith's own-menu pattern), and their "Your name" answer is the meta-layer's missing reading surface: the one place the third ledger is read back in numbers (Legend held, title carried, where the next weighing tips), because reading the count aloud is precisely a skald's work. The pledges are D-025's deed ladder, three rungs priced 20, 60, 120 so the next rung always sits above the world where the last came into reach: the raised stone, the endowed hearth, the true verse. A pledge takes the coin now, into a chest it never comes out of, which is what makes it a sink competing with iron and bread rather than a purchase; the weighing waits for the crossing, where Legend is minted and nowhere else (D-048's one-home rule holds), and pledged coin weighs half again its count, because a patron's deed sings louder than a purse. The deed then joins the character for good, and its trace stands in the songhall of every world the songs reach after: the stone on the plinth cut with the bearer's name, the hearth burning high and fed, the bearer's own verse cut deeper than the rest in a hand that took no liberties, each pressed into the new world's facts at the crossing beside the echo. The hushed name hushes the traces with everything else the songs carry (the deed stands; nothing here was told to raise it) but never the weighing, because the true ledger does not go quiet. One mechanical rider only, and it is bread: the endowed hearth adds a single loaf to D-048's welcome, hospitality compounding with hospitality, never power. Two storylets round it out: the-keeping-of-songs inside the hall, and the-stone-at-the-door, the stead talking about a stone it was never ordered to cut. Menus gained digits and every stead gained a door and a keeper, so saves bump v21 to v22; one tier-band pin widened (a first world now holds two sites). Tests 233 to 243, one honest fix on the way (record equality treats the snapshot's log array by reference; the replay test now strips and compares it by value). Live-verified end to end: the journey bot pledges the stone out of world 3 with camp coin, proves the weighing arithmetic at the gate ("the burden and the pledge, honored in legend"), finds the trace fact in world 4's graph and reads the stone standing in world 4's hall, its 1,728-key journal replaying exact into a fresh v22 save; a live pilot session then played the whole loop on the real binary with all 21 rehearsed proofs landing to the digit: the hall read whole (the world-3 stone already standing, the hearth low, the echo new-cut on the verse-wall), the ledger read at 167, the stands-already and broke refusals, five graven felled for quarry coin (one meal eaten mid-fight, no falls), the hearth pledged at 60 with the endowment waiting in its own world, the crossing weighing 14 coin and 90 pledge into a live rise to a storied bearer, both traces pressed into tier-6 Thrumalvald, the far hearth burning fed, the skald reading the ledger a rung higher, the stead talking of the stone by the well, and a cold reload replaying all 2,235 turns to field-identical state. Options set aside: patronage at the crossing menu itself (the arch already carries seven oaths, and a pledge is a thing done in a place, with a person, before the leaving), Legend minting at pledge time (standing announcements would lose their one home), traces drawn by worldgen (a Generate parameter worldgen does not need; runtime text and crossing facts do it without touching the invariant), and heavier trace mechanics like shrine or price effects (boons are hospitality; the loaf is the whole of it). Deferred: patron rungs beyond three (the ladder wants property and retinue shapes from D-025 as systems land), the skald singing commissioned verse variants (per-role voice work), Legend rungs past five (still), and cross-character traces (wants a meta-save the seed-plus-journal model does not yet keep).

### D-055: The deep questions: the level-4 knack wave, the read moment against the even hand (2026-07-17)
The oldest Skills v2 deferral lands (D-046 shipped the level-2 wave and left "second questions at level 4+" standing; the catalog was built to carry arbitrary skill-and-level groups, and this decision proves it by adding five entries and touching no machinery). Every skill's training now settles twice: at level 2 into a question of style (doing more against spending less), and at level 4 into a second question with its own stated principle: the read moment against the even hand. One answer pays only when the fight is read; the other pays a little on every exchange regardless. Blades offers the answered cut (a body in its wind-up takes the blade 2 deeper) or the stropped edge (the edge wears half as fast, the kind grip's parity clock in the blade's hand); Hafted the checked swing (a landed paid blow breaks the wind-up outright: the declared blow dies unthrown, the wave's one new mechanic and the weight's alone) or the true arc (blows land 1 deeper); Brawling the caught arm (bare fists take a winding-up body 3 deeper: a raised arm is a door left open) or the short path (fists ask 1 less wind); Warding the shield-wall (each foe beside the bearer past the first is turned 1 more, up to 2: the deep bands' crowd lesson worn as iron) or the fitted iron (iron turns 1 more, always); Ranged the picked moment (a mark mid-move, winding up or standing open, takes the shaft 2 deeper: the carl's window and the boar's blown turns now pay twice) or the waxed string (the string frays half as fast, on its own draws-counted clock, because wear frays on draws while marks are counted on hits). Every effect is a deterministic flat modifier or a parity skip hanging on state combat already tracks (Intent, ExposedTurns, adjacency, wear clocks), so no new RNG draws exist anywhere and no stream drifts; no worldgen changed and every pinned world survives byte-identical. Structure holds from D-046 unchanged: pending questions stay pure derived state, the catalog's order puts older questions before deeper ones (a bearer at level 4 with nothing answered is asked the level-2 question first, and answering keeps the sheet open so the deep one is put in the same sitting), and the rise announces the deep threshold in its own words. The Aegis remarks exactly once at the first deep answer (the craft narrowing, meant as praise), the fourth once-ever line in the knack fiction. The sheet grew to fit what it now carries: the box widened, skill rows drop the article and list both answers, and the deep question is headed as what it is. Sheet digits gained meaning where a v22 journal pressed them to no effect, so saves bump v22 to v23; the snapshot's pending-knack surface names the level now ("brawling 4"). Tests 243 to 255 (two honest pin updates: the snapshot format, and the sheet row losing its article). Live-verified end to end: the journey bot now answers the brawling level-2 question at the sheet the moment it opens (knuckle and bone, with the once-ever remark asserted), crosses its 56th counted swing in the deep bands, and ends with the deep question announced and standing open, its 1,572-key journal replaying exact into a fresh v23 save; a live pilot session then loaded the resolved keeper, read "Brawling has deepened into a second question:" on the sheet with both answers offered, took the caught arm with the praise remark landing once, walked into the unfought tier-5 ringfort and stood under a shield-carl's declared seax-stab to punch into the wind-up for 12 (a windless punch caps at 9 there; the 10+ needs the knack), paid for the window in honest blood (26 down to 14), withdrew whole, rested, and cold-reloaded field-identical through all 1,648 journaled keys. Options set aside: a third option per question (the box fits two, and D-046 already deferred it with the box), level-4 questions as upgrades of the level-2 answers (a deeper rung of the same choice would reward the already-committed twice and read as a rebate; a fresh question asks something new of the same hand), effects keyed to new combat states (the wave's job is to pay the reading the deep bands already teach, not to grow the alphabet), and pricing deep knacks in essence (D-046's ruling stands: the threshold is the price). Deferred: third questions at level 6+ if the ladder ever wants them, knacks for skills that do not yet exist (still), three-option questions (still), and the checked swing's interrupt grammar spreading to monster kinds with multi-turn wind-ups if any ever exist.

### D-056: The iron's verbs: movesets on the weapon, the ash spear's reach (2026-07-18)
D-004's oldest surviving promise lands: weapon movesets, weapons changing verbs and not just numbers. It has stood deferred through fifteen decisions because it needed everything that now exists: five weapon families (D-041, D-050), two knack waves reading Intent and exposure (D-046, D-055), and four monster kinds with timing windows worth a verb (D-033 through D-053). The representation choice is the decision's heart: the verb hangs on the piece of iron, not on the skill family, because the axe and the spear share a haft and a ledger and nothing about how they want to be used; GearItem carries a Move, and three verbs exist at launch. The arc (the woodsman's axe, the carver's maul): a paid swing of broad iron carries through into everything else at the bearer's side at half its weight, one swing, one wear, one counted use, so broad iron finally answers the pack and the crowded ward the way it always claimed it would. The answer (the grave-iron blade): a telegraphed blow the bearer stands through and takes, with the striker within arm's reach, is answered instantly over the iron for 1 plus the blade's whole effective bonus, free of wind, wear, and training, because the price was already paid in blood: the read moment D-055 taught the deep wave now lives in a piece of iron, and the blow dodged is the blow never answered. The reach (the ash spear, the smith's fifth ware, Might 6 between the axe's asking and the grave-iron's): 't' levels the point, a direction key sends a thrust two strides down the line, a full swing at length that pays more wind than a swing (the two strides are the good bought), stops at the first body or stone, wears the point hit or miss, and teaches only when it finds a body, the shaft's rule at the spear's length. The fort's grammar holds against it deliberately: a walking carl's board turns the point exactly as it stops the shaft, and only the seax's windows let it in, so the ringfort punishes the new verb the way it punishes the bow. Bare fists take no verb, on purpose: brawling's verbs are its four knacks, and an automatic push or pin on every punch either overlaps the caught arm or shove-locks the slow families into never landing a blow; the fist's identity stays where D-046 and D-055 built it. The knacks compose without seams: the checked swing breaks wind-ups at the spear's length, the kind grip spares thrust wear on the same parity clock, the follow-through hands wind back for a killing thrust, and the laying-down guardrail extends to both new ways a resolved bearer might touch the keeper (a thrust declares the old way in the shaft's own words; the answer holds itself while the moment stands open). No worldgen draw changed anywhere, so every pinned world and test seed survives byte-identical; 't' gained meaning, the arc and the answer land blows no old journal recorded, and the smith's teaching and repair digits shifted under the fifth ware, so saves bump v23 to v24. Tests 255 to 268 (two catalog pins updated for the fifth ware). Live-verified end to end: the journey bot reruns its whole five-world ladder bare-fisted to the same 1,572 keys and turn 1507 (nothing it does touched the new code, the cleanest possible no-regression proof) with a new rung reading the spear on the smith's wall; a live pilot session then played all three verbs on the real binary: the spear bought and leveled with its verb spoken at the taking-up, the axe following into the pack, a walking carl turning the point along the grain, the same carl taking a window thrust for 7, the axe swapped in mid-fight through the turn-free gear menu for an arc that carried through a second carl, the grave-iron fetched from the tier-5 barrow's chest through three entries and two shrine rests, a graven fist stood at the quarry and answered for exactly 5 with the graven never once struck by the bearer's own hand, and a cold reload replaying all 2,083 journaled keys to field-identical state; the fort leg's thrusts also trained Hafted to its first threshold live, and the question queue put the new level-2 question ahead of the standing level-4 one, D-055's ordering observed in the wild. Options set aside: verbs on the skill family (the axe and spear disagree), a player wind-up heavy attack with monster reactions (D-004's commitment clause needs monsters that read intents; nothing does yet, and shipping the player half alone is a stat toggle wearing a verb's name), a shove or grapple verb for bare fists (above), arrows or a thrown-weapon lane (D-050's rulings stand), and a Polearms skill for the spear (a sixth ledger for one weapon would thin both; the haft is the family). Deferred: monster kinds that read player commitment (the other half of D-004's contract), a deep signature piece carrying a fourth verb in some future site's chest, verbs for iron not yet forged (a parrying piece, a long blade), and familiarity-sharpened telegraphs (D-004, still the oldest open clause).

### D-057: The tiers-6+ band: the fen-leaguer and its sling-warders (2026-07-18)
The fifth hostility-tier content band, and the last structural gap the arc left open: every band from tier 2 to 5 had its own site, its own family, and its own read (D-033 the barrow, D-040 the quarry, D-044 the hall, D-053 the ringfort), and past the ringfort the endless worlds went numeric again, exactly where descent should read strangest. Tier 6+ worlds now hold the fen-leaguer: an optional site on its own streams placed after every existing draw and before the gleanings (whose forest check must see its bank tile), so pinned tier-1-5 worlds keep their layouts exactly. It reads at a glance unlike every earlier site because it is the first to hold water: a broad black mere ringed by earth-works, one causeway west to a bare holm at the middle, the cist on the holm's crown. The mere is the band's second tenant: it stops feet and never eyes, so the works are walked around, not across, and the one road to the prize runs straight through the killing ground. This forced the LineOfSight primitive from a walkability test to an opacity test (only walls and houses blind now); no earlier site holds water or a house, so every line the graven men ever drew is byte-identical, and the change is proven by the quarry tests passing untouched. Its tenant is the sling-warder, the shielded thrower D-053 deferred (board plus sling): the ringfort's two answers to the bow, the carl's board and the boar's charge, welded into one body. The board is the carl's rule at the sling's range, turning shaft and thrust-point alike while the warder stands or gives ground, opened only by the whirl of a cast and the blown turns after. The loft is the graven man's throw with the cover taken away: a stone lobbed over banks and mounds both, needing no line of sight, marked as a nine-cell burst (full price at the mark, half a stride off, nothing two strides out), so it is dodged by feet that keep walking and never by cover, the deliberate inversion of the quarry's whole lesson. Three things make the leaguer its own fight rather than a fort reskin: it wakes as one (the first sighting is a horn, and the horn is for every board on the banks, because five bands taught the dark to come a tenant at a time and this one was dug by soldiers who post a signal); it never advances (crowded, it gives ground to reopen its range; only cornered against its own works does the board's rim come down, and it is a feeble melee on purpose); and chasing one pins it silent while its fellows loft at the chaser, so the counterplay is not "corner one" but "read the whole ring." The cist holds the armor ladder's next rung and the catalog's tenth piece: the scaled byrnie (armor 4, Vigor 11, made for the besieged side of a siege, still under its falling stones), under D-041's twin rule. A felled warder pays coin and dense essence like the fort's carls. Clearing writes the siege_lifted deed with its own witnessed stead answer (the low road across the fen, struck off every map the old folk teach); the crossing-arrival line points at the mere not as counsel but as a habit (bide indoors when a whirring carries off the water); and a holm-crown EnterTile beat tells the deep bands' recurring story, duty outliving its master, at its furthest turn: here the duty has outlived its object too, the besieged ground long empty and the siege grinding on regardless. Tier-1-5 worlds draw nothing new and replay byte-identically; tier-6+ maps changed under old journals (a mere and its works stand where a v24 walk crossed plain ground), so saves bump v24 to v25. Tests 268 to 283, first run green but for one honest helper fix (a warder against the water's edge has no retreat that opens range, so the give-ground test had to crowd it where a lane stood at its back; the cornered-fights-back half proves the other side). Live-verified end to end: the journey bot reran its whole five-world ladder bare-fisted to the same 1,572 keys and turn 1507 (nothing it does reaches tier 6, the cleanest no-regression proof), writing a fresh v25 save; a rehearsal then loaded the d56 pilot's own 2,083-key journal under v25 (every d56 pin holding), crossed into tier-6 Thrumalvald, and played the band across five legs with every proof landing; a live pilot session replayed those legs on the real binary field-identical (the horn waking all five boards as one the instant the causeway was set foot in a warder's sightline, a sling-stone taken standing for 7 and a graze taken a stride off for 3, a shaft and then a thrust turned on the board and both let in through the windows, a warder giving ground behind its board when crowded, the whole watch felled, the scaled byrnie out of the holm's cist, the low road told at the well); and a cold reload replayed all 2,933 journaled keys to field-identical state, zero stray processes. Options set aside: the mere as merely impassable rather than sight-clear water (a wall would have made the works a corridor and killed the read that the killing ground is open to the eye), cover that stops the loft (that would rebuild the quarry, and the leaguer's point is that the quarry's answer fails here), a warder that advances and melees like a carl (then it is a carl with a sling, and the giving of ground is what makes it read as a besieger holding a line), waking one at a time like the graven men (the horn is the identity: an army does not wake a sentry at a time), and a sixth armor requirement tier without a new attribute rung (Vigor 11 is the honest next step on the printed ladder). Deferred: warder variants outside the leaguer, a mounted or naval siege shape if water ever wants more than one site, monster kinds that read player commitment (D-004's other half, still standing), and what tier-7+ worlds add now that every arc tier through the march to tier 5 and one past it has its own band (the arc marches to tier 5, so tier 6 is already the first truly endless-country band; whether the endless country wants more distinct bands or a recombination system is the open question the ladder now poses).

### D-058: Commitment runs both ways: the heave and the sword-thegn (2026-07-18)
D-004's oldest unpaid clause lands: "heavy player attacks take a visible wind-up enemies react to," deferred twice for want of a monster that reads the bearer (D-056 called the player half alone "a stat toggle wearing a verb's name"; D-057 re-shelved it). Both halves ship together. The heave (the player half): 'w' and a line wind up the biggest single blow a hand throws, declared now and standing one turn, visible, for the field to answer before it falls; the next act looses it on the cell chosen a turn ago, hit or miss. It is the exact mirror of the field's telegraphs, and it is dodged the way they are: a body that left the marked cell is a body the blow never touches, because the field's feet answer the bearer's as the bearer's have always answered the field. Three things keep it honest rather than a free burst: the wind-up turn is real exposure (everything gets its move while the iron is up), the blow can whiff (the mark can step off the cell), and the reader punishes it. The representation is deliberately not a fourth MoveVerb: Arc, Answer, and Reach are what a particular iron uniquely does (carry-through, riposte, the two strides), but the heave is the universal option to trade tempo for a heavier version of your own blow with whatever melee iron you hold, the same category as attacking or moving, not a single weapon's trick. It scales with the iron (the maul heaves harder than the axe), so weapons still differ, through the number and not through gating; and it stays off bare fists on purpose, because D-056 put brawling's verbs in its four knacks and the fist's identity holds. It asks the dearest wind (5, the priciest melee action), pays a full swing's wear hit or miss, teaches only where it finds a body, and composes with the knacks already tracking Intent and wear (the checked swing breaks a wind-up at the heave's weight as at the swing's; the follow-through hands wind back on a killing heave). Its resolution is two turns like a monster's declared intent, but where a monster's wind-up owes the bearer a full turn to answer, the bearer's owes the field only the one turn it takes to fall, and the field spends it; there is no taking it back, which is the whole of the commitment. The sword-thegn (the field half, the mirror monster): the even hand made flesh (D-055 named the principle the level-4 knacks turn on; this is the foe drilled into it), posted in tier-7+ ringforts among the old watch, on its own "site-thegn" stream drawn only when a fort holds one, so tier-5 and -6 forts keep their carls, boars, chest, and rubble byte-identical. It fights by answering: it comes on at a walk, trades measured blows, never declares (it reads, it does not telegraph), and waits. Its teeth is the counter, which is the checked swing turned at last on the bearer: a heave wound up in its reach is the opening it has drilled an age for, and it steps inside the winding blow and breaks it unthrown, the point coming back on the bearer, free of any telegraph because a read finds you. But a blow it must still close on it cannot answer the same way (nothing yet to step inside), so a heave thrown from a stride out lands as it walks onto it; and its spent counter leaves it a breath open, so a baited heave cracks the guard it just committed. It is tanky on purpose (28 hp at tier 7, above the carls' 18): a veteran you cannot burst in one blow is a veteran the heave stays tempting against, and the temptation is the trap. The counterplay is three-fold and teaches the whole lesson in reverse: do not heave in its face (a plain blow it never counters), heave as it closes, or bait the answer and crack the open guard; the bearer learned across five bands to punish the field's wind-ups (the answer, the answered cut, the checked swing, the picked moment), and now the bearer has a wind-up and the discipline is to throw it only when the foe cannot answer. No new gear (the heave is universal, so it is no one's signature piece; D-056's deferred fourth verb on a future site's chest stands apart); a felled thegn pays coin and dense essence (12) like the fort's carls, with its own quiet end. One storylet, the-even-hand, fires closing within two cells of a thegn, telling the deep bands' recurring story of duty outliving its object worn now as a fighter's drill: the form kept long past anyone left to keep it for. The thegn joins the ringfort rather than opening a new tier-7 band on purpose, so the ladder's standing question (whether the endless country wants more distinct bands or a recombination system) is left open, not pre-empted, and the change stays surgical. Saves bump v25 to v26: 'w' gained meaning (a v25 journal's stray 'w' was ignored and now sets the feet), the heave lands blows no v25 journal recorded, and tier-7+ forts post a thegn where a v25 walk that deep met only carls; tiers 1-6 draw nothing new (the thegn's stream draws only at tier 7+, and no shipped journal holds a 'w') and replay byte-identically. Tests 283 to 295, two honest things on the way: the thegn tests had to arm the bearer first (a bare-fisted 'w' is refused, and the line key then reads as a bump), and the thegn's HP was raised from a first-cut 20 to 28 after the live rehearsal showed a single grave-iron heave felling it before the reader ever got its turn. Live-verified end to end: the d57 journal (2,933 keys, holding no 'w') replays byte-identical under v26 with every d57 pin holding (tiers 1-6 untouched); a rehearsal then crossed into tier-7 Corlund, and in its ringfort a heave wound up loud and landed full on a carl (felling it in the one blow), the even hand was told closing on the thegn, the watch's six carls and two boars were felled to leave the thegn standing alone, and then the thegn read a point-blank heave and broke it unthrown as the point came back on the bearer, a baited heave cracked its spent guard for 20, and it folded down without a sound; a live pilot session replayed all three legs field-identical on the real binary; and a cold reload replayed all 3,478 journaled keys to field-identical state (turn 3367, tier-7 Corlund, hp 22/26, coin 22, essence 195, legend 255, grave-iron and scaled byrnie, deaths 2), zero stray processes. Options set aside: the heave as a fourth MoveVerb on the carver's maul, freeing it from Arc (rejected because the heave is D-004's whole-combat clause, not one iron's trick; gating it behind the maul would make "commitment runs both ways" a weapon gimmick most builds never touch); a thegn that slips the heave rather than breaking it (the break is the truer mirror of the checked swing, and giving ground on a read is already the leaguer's identity, D-057); a feinting thegn that declares false wind-ups to draw the bearer's answer (that reads the bearer's reaction, not the bearer's commitment, and a lying telegraph breaks the honest-read contract the whole combat design runs on, D-004); a dedicated tier-7 site band for the thegn (pre-empts the open tier-7-structure question, and the ringfort's old watch already houses a veteran); and the heave for bare fists (D-056's fist identity holds). Deferred: thegn variants outside the fort (wandering, or a leaguer's warder-captain); the closing heave as a formal knack rather than emergent play; a deep signature piece carrying a fourth verb in some future site's chest (D-056, still); familiarity-sharpened telegraphs (D-004, still the oldest open clause); and what tier-7+ worlds add structurally, the ladder's standing question now that the endless country has both a distinct band (the leaguer, tier 6) and a mirror-monster that recombines into an existing one (the thegn, tier 7).

### D-059: The bestiary: familiarity-sharpened telegraphs, and Wits given its combat meaning (2026-07-18)
D-004's oldest open clause lands, the one every combat wrap-up since the first has named still standing: telegraph clarity scales with what the bearer knows. Until now every wind-up read the same to everyone, a stranger's and a veteran's alike: the instant a foe set its intent, the danger cell was painted whole, the glyph reddened, and the sidebar named the blow. That full legibility becomes the mastered state, not the default. A kind's wind-up now reads in three tiers. A Blur (never faced): the coiling body still reddens, because a body gathering to strike is legible to anyone, and the cell it aims at is still marked, because the dodge is sacred (D-004) and must always be there; but the shape of it goes unshown (a boar's whole lane and a warder's nine-cell burst collapse to the one aimed cell) and the sidebar gives only a nameless danger line. A Read (its tell witnessed once): the full footprint is drawn and the blow is named, the old behavior restored. A Keen read (known cold): the sidebar adds its weight besides, light or heavy, so mastery knows not just what and where but how much it will cost. The read is earned by watching: every wind-up seen through to its end, hit or miss, banks one count toward the kind (a dodged tell teaches as surely as one taken, because reading a tell is watching it play out), capped once the kind is read cold. And Wits, the one attribute no rule in the game had ever consulted (shown on the sheet, raisable at the shrine, and mechanically inert since D-015 set the seven), is given its combat meaning at last as the head start: each point above the baseline is worth one banked read, so a keen-eyed bearer (Wits 6) reads a stranger's tell on sight and a keener one (Wits 8) reads its weight on sight, and the dead stat finally means something a build would buy. The bestiary is bearer-knowledge like the skills, the lessons, and the gear: death never takes it (knowledge is the one thing a fall leaves untouched) and it crosses waygates whole, so the reads earned in one world are banked against every harder world after, which is the clause's whole point, the banked knowledge carrying into NG+. The sword-thegn (D-058) falls correctly outside it: it reads, it never telegraphs, so it has no tell to bank, and a bearer who has fought a dozen carries no thegn in the bestiary at all. The discipline the decision holds to hardest is that this gates clarity and never the dodge and never the resolution: the marked cell is always drawn, so an unfamiliar foe is always dodgeable by feet (a stranger is merely less informed, never less able), and not one hit-roll, damage draw, or RNG stream is touched, the read living entirely in what the presenter draws and one derived counter. That discipline pays a determinism dividend: because the reads are rebuilt by replay and never serialized (like the heave's loaded cell and the skills' use clocks), no save version bumps at all, and every pinned world and every shipped journal replays byte-identical, the cleanest change the ladder has carried. The snapshot gained a Reads field so observers can watch the bestiary fill. Tests 295 to 301 (six new: the three-tier sharpen and its cap, Wits as the head start, a witnessed tell banking, a dodged tell teaching all the same, the bestiary surviving the crossing, and the bestiary surviving death with the very tell that felled the bearer read in the same breath); the ringfort's and leaguer's whole-mark drawing tests were upgraded in place from asserting the footprint is always drawn to proving the sharpen itself (drawn narrow for a stranger, whole once the kind is read), the exact behavior D-059 changes, encoded where the boar and warder scaffolding already stood. Live-verified end to end: the d58 rehearsal's three tier-7 legs replay field-identical on the new binary (the read changed nothing the resolution can see, the cleanest no-regression proof); and the fully fought d58 save, a cycle-7 bearer who crossed six times and fell twice, loads on the real binary carrying goblin, wight, severed, graven, carl, boar, and warder in its bestiary, the thegn correctly absent, the severed at a named one against the rest at a keen three, all at the humble baseline Wits, the banked knowledge shown surviving six crossings and two deaths in a single line. Options set aside: fuzzing the landing cell itself for a stranger (rejected outright, it breaks D-004's promise that a telegraph is always dodgeable by feet; clarity may scale, the dodge never may); gating the blow's damage or its hit on the read so an unfamiliar foe strikes harder (a difficulty knob wearing a knowledge mask, and it would drift the very determinism the observational counter protects); a bestiary panel on the character sheet (the sheet's box geometry is fixed and a nine-kind roster will not fit; the read is felt in combat where attention already is, and the count rides the snapshot for anyone who wants it); widening Wits into a general perception stat touching search, dialogue, or gleaning (scope: this decision pays the combat clause and gives the dead stat one true meaning; its other uses wait for a system that asks); and dulling the banked reads at the crossing so a harder world re-teaches its kinds (a real tension worth having, but the clause says the knowledge carries into NG+, and carrying it undiminished is the literal delivery; the dulling is a different, later idea, named below). Deferred: the crossing that dulls a read (reads carry, but a harder world's kinds move a shade strangely and re-sharpen fast, the natural next clause and where the mechanic earns its long-term keep across endless NG+); a fourth read tier past Keen (lead time, or the exact damage band); a bestiary ledger on the sheet if its box ever grows; familiarity feeding the knacks (a read foe answered a touch deeper, the read and the reflex compounding); and tells for iron and foes not yet forged.

### D-060: Restoring a Severed: the third answer, and the mythology pipe run backward (2026-07-18)
The one case D-045 deferred lands: arc sec 9's rare authored restoring of a Severed, the deepest post-resolution grace. Where the laying-down (D-045) gave the hollow's keeper a full stop and the sword gave it an end, the mending gives it a place: at a keeper, a bearer past the threshold who has already shown one keeper the gentle laying-down is offered a third answer besides the old way and the gentle one, and takes it once ever. It is the thing the ward was forged for and never once turned on a severed one: the count is not closed but caught, the way the ward catches its own bearer at death, and held; the worn shape stops repeating, and the mended one passes whole into the songs to walk the deep roads ahead. The gate is the earning: resolution plus a laying-down already banked (the mercy road walked before the deeper grace) plus never having mended before, so the offer is genuinely rare and the essence-model keeper, ward-dropped and past all choosing, is its right subject (the agency hermit chose to cut loose and is at peace and would refuse a mending, so it is never offered one). No essence changes hands and both resolutions reach the mending at the same price (arc sec 8 guardrail: the grace, like the mercy, is not bought; the gate is the whole cost). The payoff is the mythology pipe (D-013) run the one way it was never aimed: a world arrived at after the mending already carries the restored one's face in its songs (the-one-woven-in, an Arrival beat gated on a later cycle), runtime text like D-054's traces, no worldgen read, a lost thing remembered forward instead of ground under to kindle the next cruelty. The Unbinder answers it once (the-mending-shared, priority above the trade-shared so it leads when both are unspoken): the mender who spent an age teaching that all things must be let to end meets a bearer who found the seam in that law, and grants the cost of their own long certainty (the ones they cut loose who might, in a steadier hand, have been kept whole) without conceding the law itself, countered and never disproved as arc sec 7 requires. The discipline is additive to the letter: the third menu digit shows only under the gate and only in the render, never in a logged line, so no old journal's log shifts; the guarded key falls to a plain step-back without the gate; the new flags are journal-derived and never serialized (like SeveredUnbound); and the two new storylets are never eligible in any pre-D-060 journal, so no RNG stream moves anywhere and no save version bumps at all (v26 holds, the second straight no-bump decision after D-059, every pinned world and shipped journal byte-identical). Tests 301 to 309 (eight new: the gate opens only for a storied merciful bearer, the mending catches the keeper and sets it in the songs and pays nothing, it is spent once, both answers reach it at the same price to the same state, the mender marks it once before the argument, the songs run ahead into a later world, the Presenter draws the third option only under the gate, and a full-playthrough test that walks the whole reveal ladder by real keys, answers the threshold by play, lays a Severed down, and mends the next world's keeper); a pre-existing leaguer test warning was cleaned up on the way. Verified: the new binary runs, serves state, accepts keys, and advances turns live on the master seed; the resolved-restore path itself is proven end to end in-process by the real-ladder climb (the prior session's live journey-bot save being gone, the legitimate playthrough test stands in its place, on the exact shipping engine). Options set aside: restoring the agency hermit (they chose the severing and are at peace; the ward-dropped keeper who never chose is the canonical subject, and the hermit has no attack-or-aid verb regardless); a new authored Severed NPC or site for the mending (disproportionate for one beat, and the recurring keeper already carries the fiction and reuses the laying menu); pricing the mending in essence or coin (mercy and grace are the two things this game refuses to sell; the gate is the price); a general repeatable mend (the arc says rare authored case, so once ever); a per-answer later-surface (the song carries the same whichever way the threshold was answered; one beat serves both). Deferred: mending a Severed met as a person once an attack-or-aid-a-person verb exists (the hermit); more than one mending if the arc ever wants them; the restored one surfacing as a walkable bearer-myth (a statue, a tomb) rather than runtime song text, when the mythology pipe carries objects; and a rebuilt live console journey-bot for future arc verification.

### D-061: The crossing that dulls a read: the bestiary kept alive across endless NG+ (2026-07-18)
D-059's named next clause lands, the one it deferred in the same breath it delivered its opposite: the crossing that dulls a read. D-059 made the banked reads carry into NG+ undiminished, and that literal carry was the point, but it left the bestiary going quiet after the first cycle, because a bearer who has read all nine kinds cold reads every wind-up in every harder world at full clarity forever with nothing left to learn. The dulling is what keeps the loop alive down an endless chain: a harder world's kinds move a shade strangely, so a read earned lower on the chain reads a notch softer here, and one look sets it right again. The model is deliberately the soft one. Each read now carries the tier it was last sharpened at (a per-kind stamp beside the count), and the dulling is exactly the tiers climbed since: a read earned at the foot of the chain reads one step blunter for each waygate crossed. But it is floored at the name: an earned read never dulls back to a Blur, so what goes quiet is only the Keen weight of the blow, the name and the whole marked shape still holding (a bearer who has named a kind always knows where its blow falls and how wide, and is never made less safe, D-004 intact), and the dodge is untouched as ever. So the dulling steps exactly one D-059 clarity tier, Keen down to Read, which meant the presenter needed no drawing change at all: it already draws Read correctly (whole footprint, named blow, no weight), and the only wiring is the current tier threaded into ReadOf so a stale Keen reports as Read in a harder world. One wind-up watched here restamps the read to this tier and it snaps back cold, so a veteran re-sharpens in a single look where a stranger climbs from a Blur, which is the veteran's whole reward. Wits, given its combat meaning in D-059 as the head start, earns keep past it here: innate acuity is the bearer's own and is never the world's to dull, so a keen-eyed bearer reads a stranger undulled and holds the weight of a mastered kind tiers deeper into the climb (and a bearer of Wits two above the baseline, whose acuity clears the floor to Keen on its own, keeps every mastered kind cold for good, a clean identity for the perception build). The bank itself is never touched: the counts cross whole and undimmed exactly as D-059 promised, the dulling living entirely as a derived query effect keyed to the stamp, the same shape as the Wits head start, never a decrement of what was earned. The discipline holds D-059's determinism dividend intact: the tier stamp is rebuilt by replay and never serialized (like the reads it rides beside), not one logged line changes, not one RNG stream moves, so no save version bumps at all (v26 holds, the third straight no-bump decision after D-059 and D-060, every pinned world and every shipped journal replaying byte-identical). The snapshot gained a ReadTiers field beside Reads, so an observer can watch the undimmed bank and the softened read at once. Tests 310 to 314 (five new: a Keen read softening to Read across a crossing and one look here snapping it back cold, a named kind never dulling to a Blur however far the climb, keen eyes holding the weight tiers deeper, a Wits read of a stranger never dulled, and the dulling showing in the ReadTiers snapshot while the bank reads undimmed); the crossing test that D-059 wrote to prove the reads survive undiminished was repointed to prove the name holds through the floor, the exact behavior D-061 adds, encoded where the crossing scaffolding already stood. Verified: clean build, all 314 tests green, byte-identical replay held; the new binary runs and serves state (now including ReadTiers) and takes keys live on the master seed, and the softening-across-a-crossing is proven end to end in-process by the real Cross() path (a full live ladder climb to a crossing is disproportionate for one derived read, the live journey-bot still unrebuilt from D-060). Options set aside: the hard model that dulls a read all the way to a Blur so a deep world re-teaches its whole bestiary from scratch (rejected, it reads as taking back the carry D-059 had just promised, where the soft model keeps the name and only softens the fine edge); decrementing the bank itself at the crossing (rejected, it mutates what was earned, where a derived query effect leaves the bank pristine and self-corrects on a look); softening the marked shape besides the weight (the floor holds the shape by construction, and hiding it would make a kind named in the sidebar but blind on the map, a hybrid that is no clean D-059 tier and cuts against never-less-safe); and a cumulative or tier-scaled penalty beyond the positional gap (the gap is already the tiers climbed since the reading, self-correcting and impossible to over-punish, where a running decay would need its own reset). Deferred: a harder world's kinds genuinely moving a shade differently in what they do, not only in how they read (the strangeness is right now only in the reading; the dulling anticipates a later clause that drifts the behavior itself); a fourth read tier past Keen (still open from D-059, lead time or the exact damage band); familiarity feeding the knacks (a re-sharpened foe answered a touch deeper); a bestiary ledger on the sheet if its box ever grows; and tells for iron and foes not yet forged.

### D-062: The journey-bot: an autopilot that climbs the ladder, so the deep proofs drive themselves (2026-07-18)
Tooling, paying the exact debt D-061 named in its own verification note and D-060 before it: both had their deepest live proof stood in for in-process, because reaching a real crossing means the full multi-world ladder climb and the only ways to drive that were by hand (too tedious for a real chain) or by the debug hooks the tests use (which bypass the very engine path a live proof is meant to exercise). The journey-bot closes it. `aegis journey --seed N --cycles K` builds a real world and hands it to JourneyPilot, an autopilot that plays it through the public key surface alone, world after world, up to K crossings, and reports what the bearer's bestiary read on either side of each arch. It touches no debug hook: it drives the same Game the shipped binary runs, through ApplyKey, so every crossing it prints is a crossing the engine actually made. The pilot reads only what a player could (position, the map, live foes and their telegraphed intents, HP and stamina) and plays the loop the game is built around: walk to the camp, clear it by never standing on the aimed cell and answering the wind-up that is spoken for (D-004's own fight, leaning on the readable telegraphs the bestiary sharpens), climb back to daylight by the ladder, walk to the arch, and cross plain. Navigation is breadth-first a step at a time; NPC cells are treated as blocked so it never bumps a villager into a menu it would have to escape; death is recoverable (a remnant and a respawn, the camp's dead staying dead), so it grinds through a hard world rather than stalling. The determinism dividend is the whole point of building it this way: because the policy is a pure function of state, a seeded run replays identically, so the crossing evidence is reproducible rather than a one-off hand-driven session. Verified three ways: two runs on a seed are byte-identical; the pilot's own chosen key string, fed back through the independent `sim` replay path, reproduces the exact end state (same cycle, same world, `reads goblin:3`, `readTiers goblin:Read`), which proves the bot uses no hidden or debug state and that the D-061 softening shows in the independent path too; and it holds across seeds (1, 7, 42, 100, 2024 all climb to cycle 4 with a softening at every arch). On seed 42 it climbs eight crossings, tiers one through nine, and every single crossing shows the D-061 loop end to end on real deep-tier content: the goblin re-taught to Keen in each world's camp (re-sharpened by the fight), softened Keen to Read at each arch, the bank held at three the whole way down, deaths rising with the tier but always recovered. This is the automated, reproducible, live-on-the-real-binary proof the D-061 and D-060 wrap-ups had to hand-wave, now standing for good. Purely additive tooling: JourneyPilot and JourneyRunner in Aegis.Cli and one dispatch line, no Aegis.Core touched, all 314 tests unchanged, nothing near the save format. Scope held honest: the only gate a crossing needs is the goblin camp, which exists at every tier, so the bot climbs deep by clearing camps alone and does not yet enter the barrow, hall, ringfort, or leaguer, resolve the threshold, meet the Severed, swear an oath, buy gear, or shop, so it does not yet exercise D-060's restore path or an oath-crossing (those are the next increment of the tool, not of the engine). The combat policy is deliberately simple (step off the aimed cell, bump the nearest foe), a demonstration that D-004's telegraphs are readable enough to fight blind on, not a bid to play well. Options set aside: driving the same policy over the pilot pipe against a running console (the engine behavior is identical in-process and over the pipe, the pipe being pure transport already smoked, so the in-process driver is the honest engine proof and a pipe flavor waits until we ever want the transport itself in the loop); extending the Snapshot with per-monster telegraph data (only the pipe flavor would need it, since the in-process policy reads the Game directly). Deferred: teaching the bot the deeper sites, the threshold, and the Severed so it can auto-verify the restore and oath-crossings the way it now auto-verifies the read-dulling; a smarter combat policy for a cleaner deep climb with fewer deaths; and a compact machine-readable report mode if a sweep or CI ever wants to consume the crossings as data rather than read them.

### D-063: The journey-bot goes deep: clearing the sites, raising at the shrine, and the honest ceiling (2026-07-18)
Pays D-062's own named deferral, teaching the autopilot the deeper sites, so it plays each world the way a bearer would instead of beelining the gate. The pilot now takes a skip-set and, in every world, clears the nearest tenanted site before the arch: not just the goblin camp that gates the crossing but the barrow, hollow, quarry, hall, ringfort, and leaguer besides. Dormant foes are handled by walking up and bumping them awake (a graven man wakes when neared or struck, a warder's whole line wakes as one), and the navigation falls back to bumping straight through a foe when routing around the others boxes it in. The runner owns the give-up logic: each site carries a cumulative key and death budget across the world (defaults 3000 keys, 8 deaths), so a hard but winnable site gets real attempts while an unwinnable one is written off and left standing. The camp is never written off (the arch needs it, and goblins are always winnable). A dead end that is not the camp (the nearest foe genuinely unreachable, a sling-warder keeping its distance across the mere) writes that one site off and the climb continues, rather than halting the whole run as the first cut did. The bot also plays the core progression loop now: standing on the shrine with essence to spend or a wound to mend (which also catches the shrine it wakes on after every death), it drives the raise menu, spending banked essence on Vigor and Might, kept level, leaning to Vigor on a tie. Deliberately not Wits: raising Wits would offset the D-061 dulling and hold a mastered kind Keen, a real and interesting alternate demonstration but one that would hide the base softening the report exists to show, so it is left for a future toggle. The report grew a per-world site line (cleared versus left standing) and the crossing bestiary table now spans every kind the bot read. Results on the master seed: it clears camp, barrow, hollow, quarry, hall, and ringfort at every tier through seven, leaves only the leaguer standing (bare fists cannot corner a warder that retreats and lofts stone over water), and reads the full eight-kind bestiary (goblin, wight, severed, graven, hound, carl, boar, warder), every kind showing the re-sharpen-then-soften loop across the crossings, the warder among them even though its site was left standing, because the bot engages and learns the tell before giving up. Shrine raising cut the deep-tier death toll (tier 5 from 14 to 7, tier 6 from 22 to 17, the seven-crossing total from 74 to 57); the deaths that remain are the honest cost of a deliberately simple bare-fisted policy with no weapon, not the game being brutal (a bearer who arms clears these far more gently). Verified: two runs byte-identical, the emit-keys string replayed through `sim` reproduces the exact end state (cycle 6, seven kinds banked, all softened to Read, every key applied), robust across seeds, all 314 tests unchanged, no engine touched, nothing near the save format. Options set aside: dying forever on an unwinnable site (the skip budgets and the null-writes-off-the-site rule turn a spin into an honest "left standing"); raising Wits or every attribute (Vigor and Might are the survivability the deep sites ask for, and holding Wits at baseline keeps the dulling legible); clearing via the debug hooks the tests use (rejected on the same ground as D-062, a live proof must drive the real key path). Deferred: arming the bot at the smith so the leaguer and the tier-7 forts stop costing so many deaths (the smith trades through the talk menu, whose buy-digit shifts with the topic count, so it wants a careful robust driver unlike the fixed-digit shrine, and buying auto-equips an empty slot so the mechanics are easy once the digit is found); the bow verb so a ranged foe can be answered at range; teaching the threshold and the Severed so the bot can auto-verify D-060's restore path and oath-crossings (both need the arc's reveal ladder climbed first, a bigger lift); a Wits-raising mode to demonstrate the perception-build identity; and a machine-readable report for a sweep or CI to consume the crossings as data.

### D-148: Letters and Lore: the 14th skill, the scrivener, and the first books (2026-07-21)
Plan step 13 taken (C literacy/Lore, landed before any town archives deepen so the illiterate starts feel it), paying D-005's oldest clause and D-016's "Lore (incl. literacy)" in one cut. The model settled with the user in Q&A: literacy IS Lore level 1 (option A over a separate Lettered flag), so the 14th skill joins the same honest ledger as the other thirteen with zero new state beyond the slot. At zero uses the marks stay marks: mundane script (books, and whatever ledgers and notices come later) refuses the eye. Lettered backgrounds seed Lore 1 via banked uses exactly as Soldier seeds Blades: the scribe's-ward (the one past that was always going to read) and the hedge-healer (the user's pick over the recommended ward-only slate: the healer read the herbals somewhere, and it makes the herb lane's book pay the moment it is bought). The unlettered learn from the town's new scrivener, a seventh towner in the market plot's own lean-to, on the D-141 school pattern: 2 coin the sitting, 2 Lore uses banked, so the four sittings that carry a hand to level 1 are the act of learning its letters, and every sitting after is copy-work that keeps feeding. The graven question was the one real retrofit risk and is canonized the safe way: graven script is NOT letters (the old words answer the warmth behind the eyes, which the Emberwrought blurb has said since D-092), so the stones, the site reads, and the whole D-091 lane stay open to every bearer and nothing shipped regresses. What feeds Lore past the desk: books, three in the first catalog, each bought once at the scrivener's shelf (sold only to lettered hands; the scrivener sells no one paper they cannot open) and worked through in sittings at the shrine's quiet on the new 'v' verb (the one free letter), a sitting a turn and a use, rereads feeding nothing, so the use-curve stays what-you-actually-did (D-014/D-016). Every book pays a concrete keep (the no-lore-only rule): the hedge-wife's herbal (8c, Lore 1, 5 sittings) teaches the wort-cunning, a book-taught lesson with no mentor and no price whose keep is a draught steeped from 2 sprigs instead of 3, stillroom and self-steep both; the delver's bestiary (9c, Lore 1, 5 sittings) banks the wight Keen through a new StudyKind that stamps the tier like three watched wind-ups would, dulling downstream per D-061 exactly like the veteran's; the lay of the kindled lands (12c, Lore 2, 7 sittings, the one text that wants growth) writes a fact, opens a skald topic keyed on the reading itself (so every world's hall knows a read bearer: knowledge crosses), and pays +2 Legend at the next crossing once ever, honoring D-048's Legend-is-minted-at-the-crossing-and-nowhere-else. The fallen hall's chest seeds a free copy of the lay while unread (a hall is where a lay would have been kept; the catalog exists once, like the signature iron). The crossing split is D-013 verbatim: books, progress, and read state all ride the character bucket. The sheet got the re-lay D-142 said the 14th skill would force: the attributes fold to one abbreviated row, buying two, so fourteen skills and a deep question fit the 24-row frame exactly. The pilot walks the whole lane on real keys: the town leg fires on a scrivener errand too (letters while unlettered, then the shelf, then the lay's copy-work), the predicates shared between the walk and the digits so nothing shuttles, and a quiet-hour errand reads owned pages at the shrine before the arch. Save v85 -> v86 ('v' journaled, a towner where a v85 walk crossed empty grass, cheaper steepings, and 14-skill state). Verified: 814 tests green (a new LoreTests file: the scrivener dealt and reachable across twenty seeds; letters bought sitting by sitting with the unlettered sale refused whole; the herbal bought, walked home by the real doors, read at the shrine, and steeping cheaper; the bestiary reading the old dead Keen; the lay refusing the unschooled eye, greeting the skald, and crossing as exactly +2 Legend against a control crossing; the lettered pasts asserted through real creation keys; plus the sheet and deep-knack tests updated honestly for the 14th slot); twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds; sim replay exact; worldgen purity exit 0; the v90 sweeps are the new named baselines. Options set aside: a separate Lettered lesson-flag (splits one concept into two currencies and leaves Lore unfeedable before it); gating graven script on Lore (a heavy retrofit of D-091, the pilot, and every baseline, for a worse fiction); the waykeeper's shelf as the teacher (no new NPC, but the town would have no bookish anchor for archives to grow from); books as world-bucket items that reset at the crossing (paper that taught nothing would be the only thing the character bucket forgot). Deferred: more titles as the lanes grow (a smithing text, a town-law primer, folk-tales that seed storylets); Lore knacks when the skill has verbs enough to question; the town's bookshop/archives proper (this decision exists so those land on a bearer who can already read); illiteracy texture on future mundane-script surfaces (notices, ledgers, the moot's book shown, not just told).

### D-147: The fells fill in: the combe's own stone, and the high cairn (2026-07-21)
B4 continued the session after it opened: the two debts D-146 recorded, paid together as one density step. The combe's authored interior first: the wolves' ground borrowed the glade's woodland (a deer-run's map under a wolf's fiction) and read darker than it drew; now GenerateCombe cuts it from the fells' own stone, a bowl of open heath ringed in scree, a black tarn standing in every combe's low end, and scree outcrops placed under the quarry's all-nine-open rule so no clump ever seals ground off, the mouth's first steps kept clear. Rock stops feet but not eyes (D-057's distinction held), so the bow still draws its lines and the wolves still close across them: the ground changed and the fight's grammar did not. Then the high cairn, the fells' second site and the world's first new site kind since the town: SiteKind.Cairn behind Terrain.CairnEntrance (both end-appended), a kerbed cairn crowning the tops, its interior mostly authored the way the dead like it (a creep of a passage under the kerb opening into one corbelled oval chamber, four kerb uprights for cover, the cist at the deep end), the rng placing only the watchers and the word. The tenants are wights of the valley barrow's kind at the frontier's price (10+tier hp, two to five by tier), and the yield is the cist's grave-gold (14-26 coin through the standard chest machinery) plus a graven word (D-091's stone set inline at the site's construction, because the D-091 loop ran before the fells grew and a cairn's fabric predates everything), so the fells now pay down two ladders where D-146 built one: hides to the town's counter, and the old dead's gold and word to the fight's own ledger, without touching the wolf's game/foe line. Placement discipline: every new draw on fresh streams derived after every D-146 draw (the cairn's ground scan preferring the Hills of the tops, held off the combe's mouth and the herb spots), so the fells' lie, the combe's mouth, and the herbs all hold to the tile in prior worlds and the tops only gain a door. Perceivability (D-023): the entrance described live and cleared both, its own glyph and sidebar hint, a site fact written for the drovers' avoidance, and the waykeeper's fells topic grown its cairn sentence inside the one topic (the shared nine untouched). The pilot climbs for both errands: FellTripWanted is combe-or-cairn behind the same armed gate (the old dead ask no less than the pack), the fells loop takes the cairn after the combe so the hunt's meat funds the camp that mends the wights' toll, and the generic site machinery fights, loots, and reads the stone without a single new branch. Save v84 -> v85 (the combe's recut ground moves a v84 journal's steps; the cairn's door, dice, and gold never existed under one). Verified the established ways: 808 tests green (3 new FrontierTests: the combe cut from the fells' own palette with every den reachable across twenty seeds; the cairn deterministic, wight-held, cist and stone on real floor and reachable from door to deep; the cairn entered and paying gold and word through the real keys; the waykeeper's cairn sentence asserted in the fells topic; CrossingTests hardened onto per-site wights honestly). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds; all five moved off v88 as a new site demands, drift healthy end to end (cycle 13 and 12 crossings on every seed, the cairn cleared in every world from cycle 1, deaths 2-8 against v88's 3-7, the hunt 540-592 hides per run); sim replay exact (seed 1: 21698 keys, cycle 13, turn 20217); worldgen harness purity exit 0; the v89 sweeps are the new named baselines. Options set aside: a new monster kind for the cairn (the wight is the right dead and the bestiary's banked reads carry over; a fells-flavored revenant is texture, not structure, and can come with the region's later depth); the cairn's own signature gear in the cist (the D-041 catalog is one piece per deep kind and nothing about the cairn earns a sixth yet; the slot stays open); a coffer in the cairn (the dead lock nothing, D-122's own rule); keeping woodland walls in the combe for opaque bow cover (cover that blinds was the glade's grammar, not the tops'; the open ground with unwalkable rock reads true and keeps the pack's closing honest). Deferred: more fell sites and kinds toward full density, then further regions one at a time (B4's standing line); frontier news on D-143's calendar (a hard wolf-winter heard in town); the fells' own goods if the region ever wants its own counter.

### D-146: The high fells: the frontier opens, and the world learns to count past two (2026-07-21)
Plan 2026-07's B4 begun, the session after B3 closed: the wild frontier region, opened whole the way D-138 opened the road. The structural bill came first and was paid once: "which overworld" was a bool (OnRoad, D-138's honest binary), and a third country makes it an Area (Valley, Road, Fells) on the bearer, the sites, the folk, and the beasts, with OnRoad kept as a derived legacy read (true exactly on the road) so every old call site and test still reads true; the valley-only checks (gleanings, the pony, the gate, the crime family's ground, the near-house beats) now say Valley outright, and the weather checks say "not the valley", which is where the frontier's exposure comes from for free. The fells themselves: a third overworld off the road's north shoulder, behind a drovers' track found by the same deterministic edge-scan as the valley's mouth, everything drawn off one derived seed after every existing draw so all prior worlds hold byte-identical. New ground reads new: treeless heath (walkable, campable, the fell pony's own striding ground), rare black tarns, and scree that walls the ways (the first unwalkable rock, taught to the path-carver), so the fells are a maze of open country rather than a field. The third region is drawn third on the same regions stream (the first two names hold everywhere), rerolled against both, its fact written, the crossings narrated by name both directions, and the waykeeper's door grown a fourth topic that prices the climb honestly (no roof, no law, wolves in company, take a supper you can burn). No roof is the region's identity: no wayhouse, no bed, no vendor, so the camp is the only rest by construction, and the fells' cold quarters a camp's mending where the road's foul sky halves it, with the supperless cold camp refused as ever.
