The tenant is the moor-wolf, the hunt's first prey that hunts back: game to the harvest (hide and meat, no purse and no essence, so the frontier pays down the exact ladder the glade opened and the town's counter closes: the fells are the deep larder, and the region's economic meaning rides machinery the world already keeps), but a foe to the fight, with three pack rules that make it read as a pack: it closes from far off; at the ring's edge it commits only in company (the last of the pack past caring); and it works a prey in shifts, at most two at the throat with the bite's pack bonus capped where the closing is, so the swarm is dangerous rather than a blender. Its committed blow is the pounce, a telegraphed spring from a stride out (a new tell for the bestiary, the hound only ever lunging from touch), resolved on the marked cell through the standard intent machinery: dodgeable by feet, D-004 intact. One fairness rule the build itself discovered: a pack that kills at the door then camps the door, and every walk back in is an ambush no player chose; so the pack draws off with its kill (on the bearer's death among wolves, the pack goes home to its spawn ground, narrated), and the walk back in is the walk it was the first time. The pilot walks the whole frontier live: the road loop climbs the track when the combe is owed AND the bearer is armed for it (bow, iron, and armor: the waykeeper's warning taken as policy), the fells loop hunts, camps on the kill, picks the high heath's herbs (six spots, richer than the verges), and climbs back down into the market leg so the wolf hides sell in town; wolves are fought bow-first (a shaft into everybody crossing open ground), not hunted like deer. Two pilot lessons paid for in the sweeps and kept: a wilds site dispatches by tenant, not by kind (the combe under the hart-hunter's policy read "no harts, climb out" and shuttled through the pack's teeth forever); and the runner's give-up machinery must attribute a death on the very key that steps in ('>' pressed from the overworld) to the site behind the door, or a site that kills at its threshold never hits its own death budget and the run loops on the door. Save v83 -> v84 (a third overworld behind a track a v83 journal's presses fell dead on, new combat dice for the pounce, a fourth topic at the waykeeper). Verified the established ways: 805 tests green (12 new FrontierTests: the third country named and distinct across twenty seeds with map, mouths, site, and herbs deterministic; the track taken by name both ways; the combe entered and climbed out of on the fells; the wolf as game down the hunt's ladder; the pack holding alone and closing in company; the pounce landing on the kept cell and missing the left one with the tell banked; the fells' cold quartering the camp and refusing the supperless; the high herbs picked; the fall sending the pack home with its kill; the fall coming home to the valley; the waykeeper speaking the fells inside the shared nine; RegionTests and BarrowTests hardened onto the third country honestly). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds; all five moved off v87 as a new region demands, drift healthy end to end (cycle 13 and 12 crossings on every seed, deaths 3-7 against v87's 2-4 with the frontier's cost honest, the hunt doubled to 540-587 hides per run with the pack's ground the reason, 14-17 climbs, every salt sack still sold, and the teller's book reading the new heat: more calls for air on every seed, the frontier being exactly the pressure D-145 said the run lacked); sim replay exact (seed 1: 20882 keys, cycle 13, turn 19478); worldgen harness purity exit 0; the v88 sweeps are the new named baselines. Options set aside: the frontier hung off the valley (the geography is the story: the wild country lies past the settled one, and the road is how a walker gets anywhere); a new trade good for the fells (salt just taught that lesson, and the hunt ladder already pays; a second good waits until it has a reason to differ); wolves bearing coin or essence (double-pays the hunt and blurs the game/foe line the hart drew); the pack roaming the fells overworld (monsters live in sites; overworld tenancy is real machinery the moment a region wants it, not before); a roof or bed up top (the absence is the design). Deferred: the combe's own authored interior (it borrows the glade's woodland today and reads darker than it draws); more fell sites, kinds, and full-density depth as B4 continues (then other regions, one at a time); frontier news on D-143's calendar (a hard wolf-winter heard in town); the fells' own goods if the region ever wants its own counter.

### D-145: The teller's book: the pacing layer opens read-only (2026-07-21)
Plan 2026-07's step 12 (D1), the Phase D lane opened exactly as the plan demanded: a storyteller above the coarse tick, read-only first, so its editorial instincts are auditable across the sweep seeds before it is ever allowed to steer. The machinery is Storyteller (Pacing.cs), one observation per tick night from the tick block's own tail: it makes its call BEFORE admitting the night's events (an editor decides before the page is written), from carried state alone: a carried temperature at four or above calls Space (the run needs air; it would have held the season's deck and any other non-causal pressure), three or more consecutive heatless ticks call Press (the run coasts; it would have hastened an eligible card or storylet beat), anything else is Steady. Then it observes what the causes actually did and heats: a death since the last tick is the hardest beat at three, a night a scheduled future claimed whole (winter, washout, the mustered raid) heats two, a raid heats by its take (a plain night one, a bold unblunted night two, and the mustered raid reads as claim plus raid), and the carry cools a point a night. The output that matters is the disagreement: nights the season dealt a card straight through a call for air, and pressed nights that stayed quiet anyway, both counted, every reading kept in a book that spans the run (the carry resets at each crossing with the World bucket, because a new world starts cool, but the run is what the audit reads). The discipline that shaped everything: the teller draws no RNG, writes no facts, and adds no narration, so its presence cannot move replay, the deck's stream, or a single baseline key, and NO save bump is owed, only the third no-bump change since D-059/D-060/D-061's run. The parked authority question (which event classes it may one day delay or hasten, and which stay untouchable) stays parked on purpose: the raids' causal clock and the calendar's scheduled facts are observations here, never subjects, and this book is precisely the evidence that question wants. The journey report and JSON carry the ledger (nights watched, both call counts, both disagreement counters), which is the plan's "log what it would have done across the sweep seeds" delivered literally. Verified the established ways: 794 tests green (9 new PacingTests: the watching every night, a raid's heat read by its take across the plain and emboldened nights, the claimed winter night's charge, two deaths calling for air on the very next night, the coasting run turning to Press with the unanswered pressure counted, both disagreement counters held at the unit, a pressed night the season answers itself being no complaint, the crossing cooling the carry while keeping the book, and the same seed keeping the same book whole). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds AND every sweep byte-identical to the v86 baselines except the single added teller line, the strongest read-only proof the discipline allows: not one key, narration, or number moved; sim replay exact (seed 1: 18349 keys, cycle 13, turn 17075); worldgen harness purity exit 0; the v87 sweeps are the new named baselines. And the book already says something: across the five seeds the teller watched 95-105 nights a run, called Press 9-23 times against Space's 1-7, left 6-15 pressed nights unanswered, and caught exactly one card dealt through a call for air, which is the audit's first finding in numbers: the run coasts once the camps clear, and the pressure half of the layer is where the authority will earn its keep when it comes. Options set aside: letting the teller steer at birth (the plan's own sequencing forbids it, and the read-only season is what makes the later authority arguable rather than vibes); heat from the log or the fact graph (parsing narration is fragile and facts are the storylets' input, so reading them back risks the observer entangling with the observed; the tick block hands over plain deltas instead); counting the drovers' sale or the wedding feast as heat (a chosen trade and a feast are events, not wounds; stores lost to commerce is not drama, so only the raid's take heats); a per-world book (the crossing would wipe the very stretch most worth auditing, and the carry-reset already gives each world its fresh start); surfacing the teller in talk or the Snapshot (a read-only layer that narrates itself would not be read-only in the player's world; the report is the honest surface until it steers). Deferred: the steering itself, gated on this book's evidence and the parked authority question (the natural first authority: the deck's chance, pressed upward on coasting nights and held under Space, exactly the two counters now measured); act awareness and hostility-tier tuning (the storylets doc's full director, D-021.5); per-reading export in the JSON if the run-level totals prove too coarse.

### D-144: The caravan leg: salt on the cart, and the margin earned by the walk (2026-07-21)
Plan 2026-07's step 11 closes, and B3 with it: the economy's first buy-to-resell trade, D-025's "caravan investment as productive capital" at its smallest honest size, and D-124's stock-growth deferral cashed. One good, two prices, and the bearer as the caravan. The cart's fourth digit (appended after D-124's three, so no digit shifts, D-041) sells sacks of salt at 5 coin, the label counting the stock; the stock is per world and dealt from the tier alone (two and the tier, capped at six: deeper worlds sit on richer roads, no RNG drawn, so every existing stream holds byte-identical), sold down through the world and restocked whole with the next world's cart. The provisioner's second digit buys the sacks at 8, a lot like the hides and herbs: TownHaggle rides it (Commerce's level, the guild's mark, and the law's three teeth all apply, including D-143's road-spoken distrust), the town-book bar refuses it, and Commerce is fed one use per lot, cost-gated by construction exactly as D-140 framed the craft: the margin was earned by the walk. The salt itself is freight on the body (surviving death and the crossing like every good), so a leg interrupted mid-carry completes in the next world's town, and the Aegis marks the first resold lot with the run's first knowing-where-things-are-wanted coin. The pilot walks the whole leg live: with the valley settled and the road trip ahead it loads the cart sack by sack (predicate-matched at the walk, the talk handler, and the counter, the D-141 no-shuttle discipline), carries east, and sells at the provisioner inside the existing market leg; the journey report grew its caravan line. Save v82 -> v83 (a fourth digit at the cart and a second at the provisioner move coin, goods, and the craft where a v82 journal's presses fell dead). Verified the established ways: 785 tests green (7 new CaravanTests: the stock dealt by tier with its cap; the sack bought down to bare boards with the sold-through refusal; the no-slates refusal; the lot paying at the counter and feeding the craft; the empty pack; the barred hand trading no freight; the shared nine at both counters; PeddlerTests' cart board grown to four digits). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds; all five moved off v85 as predicted for a pilot with a new errand, drift healthy (cycle 13 and 12 crossings everywhere, deaths unchanged on every seed, 58-63 sacks loaded per run and every sack sold, town lots up from ~22 to ~32 with the salt lots feeding Commerce, seed 99 reaching level 3); sim replay exact (seed 1: 18349 keys, cycle 13, turn 17075); worldgen harness purity exit 0. Options set aside: a consignment/investment instrument (coin in, coin out on a delay: real caravan capital wants a carrier NPC and a risk model; the bearer-as-caravan teaches the trade first and the guild is the obvious later home); salt price derived from live facts (war and blockade pricing is D-025's full vision; two honest constants teach the leg before the derivation earns its complexity); the peddler buying town goods for the reverse leg (the asymmetry is the geography: the town is where things are wanted; a two-way arbitrage loop would print coin on a treadmill); more exotic goods at once (one good proves the machinery; the catalog grows the moment a second good has a reason to differ). Deferred: the guild running consignments on the bearer's coin (the caravan proper, sitting on D-141's bond); price spreads joining the journey drift measure (the roadmap's D-083 line); the reverse leg if a stead good ever earns a town premium; the frontier region's own goods when B4 opens.

### D-143: The named countries and the word that walks: regions as entities, news at freight speed (2026-07-21)
Plan 2026-07's step 11 opens (B3, region machinery's first half): the region becomes an entity, and news becomes a thing that travels between regions on the calendar's clock instead of not existing at all. The naming first, paying the box D-049 left open in its own words (no region existed to hang a name on; now one does). A Region is identity, not a map: id and name, two per world (the home valley, and the road's high country with the town at its end), because the overworlds already exist and what was missing was the name the world knows them by and the subject cross-country word can travel under. NameGen grows its region kind under the D-049 rules: regions borrow the world openers (a country sounds of its world's tongue) over their own closer pool of the land's plain words (wold, heath, shaw, holt, hurst, gill, scar, side, down, how), disjoint from every other kind's closers so a region can never share a full name with a world, stead, or person, spoken with "the" in front (the Brancorwold, the Isenheath), the road's rerolled against the valley's so no world names two countries the same. Drawn on their own derived stream ("regions") after every existing draw, so all prior placement, casting, and story stay byte-identical. The naming is perceivable at every seam it touches (D-023): region facts written at generation, the road's crossings narrated by name both directions, the stead topic pointing east into the named country, the town topic claiming its own. Then the word that walks, the plan's own sentence made machinery (the town's law hearing about the valley's named thief only as fast as news travels), built whole on D-132's calendar because news IS a scheduled future. Eastbound: the stead calling the bearer unwelcome (shame rung 2) puts word on the calendar, announced at the scheduling so the grace window is legible, due two coarse ticks out with the drovers, cancelled the designed way (the stead's book made even before they roll: restitution outrunning the news is the counterplay, and the cancellation is narrated because a future that quietly fails teaches nothing); landed, it writes the news fact and the town's third tooth engages: the haggle coin dies (chalk only, Commerce and bond both quiet) while the home shame still stands beside the word, and squaring the stead's book restores the town's trust the moment it is done, because the road carries the mending on the same clock it carried the wrong. Westbound the mirror with the opposite teeth: a name at the town's barred rung is freight, carried down two ticks later unless the moot rules the book back under the rung first, and at the valley's end it is talk alone, the doors saying plainly that it is being said while the stead's own book refuses to move for hearsay, because the home ledger judges what it sees under its own roofs (D-142's separate-books resolution held even once the books share news; the asymmetry is the design: a town's trust is commercial and moves on reputation, a stead's shame is witnessed and moves on deeds). The warden names the incoming word too, and prices it exactly (nothing in my book answers to another country's grievance; but counters listen to roads). Save v81 -> v82 (the news teeth move lot coin under old journals wherever shame stood). Verified the established ways: 778 tests green (5 new RegionTests: both countries named, written, and distinct from every other name across twenty seeds; same seed same country; the road taken by name both ways; the doors knowing the country at both ends; 6 new NewsTests: the unwelcome name scheduling the word with the announcement said; the word landing, killing the chalk trust, and releasing it the moment home stands even; restitution beating the drovers with the cancellation narrated; the warden naming the road's word; the barred name carried west and heard at the doors with the stead's shame untouched; the pleas beating the carts). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds AND all five byte-identical to the v84 baselines outright, the predicted crime-gated signature again (the pilot commits no crimes, and region names live in narration the report never prints); sim replay exact (seed 1: 17988 keys, cycle 13, turn 16801); worldgen harness purity exit 0 with the region facts struck to their skeletons. Options set aside: regions as maps or containers holding sites (the overworlds already are the geography; an entity holding identity is what B4's later regions actually need, and anything more is speculative structure); instant news (deletes the whole design: the delay is the counterplay window, and distance-that-matters is the region system's reason to exist); the westbound word moving stead regard or shame mechanically (hearsay moving a witnessed ledger flattens the two laws D-142 just separated); a townbook mark from the eastbound word (same flattening from the other side: the moot's book is the town's own arithmetic, and pricing another country's grievance in it makes one law of two); news as a wandering NPC or the peddler carrying it (the drovers are fiction over the calendar today, and a carrier with a position is real machinery the moment news needs one, not before). Deferred: the caravan leg itself (D-144, the step's second half); news of deeds beyond crime (the camp cleared, the muster broken, the wedding: the town could hear of them all on the same clock, a rich later widening); region entities for B4's frontier when it opens (the seam is cut); culture-flavored name pools and world epithets (still D-049's deferral, waiting on the race list and the mythology pipe).

### D-142: The town's depth, second half: the warden's book and the plea (2026-07-21)
Plan 2026-07's step 10 closed, and with it B2's core: the law and reputation web D-140 deliberately surfaced as talk (research/11's missed lesson) becomes machinery, and Persuasion, the 13th skill, is seeded in its mouth. The crime half first: the light hand now works inside the wall ('p' beside a towner draws the same Lifting dice the valley's lanes do, one try per pocket per world, the clean take paying coin and Sleight and writing the town's own secret fact), and a caught hand goes into the warden's book, not the stead's shame, because the ledgers are per-faction by construction (D-078/D-086) and a stead's suspicion has no eyes two days east: the town becomes the fourth faction (FactionId.Town, appended), and the book is simply its infamy count, cleared at the crossing like every ledger. The teeth are two, both legible at the counter where they bite (D-023's perceivability): while any mark stands the haggle coin dies whole (no counter trusts a booked hand's scales, so Commerce's level and the guild's mark both go quiet, which makes the law expensive for exactly the bearer who had built trade standing to lose), and at two marks every counter in town shuts (hands flat on the board, the same refusal at stall, forge, and guild door); the moot itself never bars, because a law that cannot be answered is not a law but a wall, and the deadlock (barred from the very door that clears you) is designed out rather than special-cased. The plea is the whole answer: the warden's board grows its one digit only while a mark stands (his counter-free board when the book is even was D-140's point about him, kept: a fine is not a price, it is an answer, and the digit goes home with the last mark), each plea answers one mark for one fine, and Persuasion is fed one use per plea that truly moved the book, cost-gated by the fine itself (D-014 in the law's coin: nobody grinds a craft whose every use costs a real fine over a real crime), with the craft's level shaving the fine from FineCoin 6 toward the floor of 2: a practiced pleader still pays, the moot just hears them faster. Restitution stays honest across the seam: making right in the wronged towner's hand (the D-086 repay, same key) squares the hand but never the book, because the mark belongs to the moot, so the two justice systems (private restitution, public arithmetic) compose instead of shadowing each other. The moot topic reads the book true, mark count and all. The sheet pays the 13th skill's rent: the attributes fold from two columns of four to three of three, freeing exactly one row, so thirteen skills and a deep question still fit the 24-row frame exactly (and the comment says plainly that the next skill has no row to take: the real re-lay is owed then). The parking-lot question (town law vs stead shame, D-131) resolves as: one infamy machinery, separate books per faction, which is what the ledger design wanted all along. Save v80 -> v81 ('p' in a town a v80 journal treated as nothing now moves coin, facts, and two crafts). Verified the established ways: 767 tests green (7 new TownLawTests: the lift's both outcomes hunted across seeds with each outcome's whole ledger held, clean paying the town's secret and caught booking one mark with the stead's shame untouched by either; the repaid hand leaving the book standing with the moot's own line said; the booked hand's dead haggle at a live counter; the barred rung refusing stall and forge whole while the warden still hears, fines, and feeds the craft; the plea going home mark by mark to an even book and a bare board; the practiced pleader's fine shaved to exact coin; the moot topic counting its marks; SkillsTests' snapshot and DeepKnackTests' exclusion grown the Sleight way). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds AND all five byte-identical to the v83 baselines outright, the exact predicted signature (crime-gated, pilot-unexercised, test-covered: the D-135 precedent for talk-gated depth); sim replay exact (seed 1: 17988 keys, cycle 13, turn 16801); no worldgen change, so no harness run owed. Options set aside: routing town crime through the stead's shame ladder (one unified book flattens the geography that makes the town a different place; the whole point of a second region is that its law does not know you); outlawry/gate-barring at the top rung (bars the plea's own door: the deadlock is the design flaw, not the feature; the counters are the town's real leverage anyway); the fine paid to the wronged hand instead of the stone (already exists as the repay; the moot's fine is the public half, and merging them collapses two systems into one verb); Persuasion fed by haggling or lessons (haggling is Commerce's coin, and a lesson-taught tongue undoes the craft's premise: pleading is learned by pleading, at real cost); scene-check pleading with Presence dice (D-117's scene machinery would carry it, but dice on top of fines double-charges failure; the flat fine is legible and the craft's shaving is the progression). Deferred: more Persuasion feeds as town life deepens (the court hearing disputes NOT the bearer's own, tournaments' side-wagers, the guild's advocacy); town burglary and Stealth (the crime family's movement half, D-131's C lane); the sheet's real re-lay before the 14th skill; tournaments and the property rung (the town-life boxes); witnesses and named accusers in the book (the law speaks in counts today; names would let the web deepen the D-110 way).

### D-141: The town's depth, first half: the forge's school and the guild's oath (2026-07-21)
Plan 2026-07's step 10 begun, taken in two bites so each lands decision-sized: this one builds the forge's school and the carriers' guild; the law's own book and Persuasion follow. The stitch changes first: the forge and the guildhall join market and moot as always-dealt plots (the town's four working institutions, the reason a wall was worth raising; the last two plots still draw and shuffle from the library, so towns keep sharing parts and never arrangements), the guildhall's authored-shut wall gets its door cut (D-140's deferral paid on schedule), and the cast grows to six: the forge-smith on his forecourt under the lean-to's south gap, the guildmaster standing his yard door. The forge is the school D-135 promised when it seeded Smithing at home: one sitting files the bearer's most worn piece by exactly the bench's arithmetic (base 4, +2 per Smithing level) and feeds the craft one use only when iron truly moved, but the sitting costs coin (3c, no slates: the moot has opinions about slates), so the town's copy of the verb is cost-gated by price where the home bench is gated by the walk west, and the two together make wear a real economy: earned in fights, filed at either counter, each counter with its own price. The smith also teaches: the drawn temper, the fifth lesson (14c), gated on Smithing 1 (the showing is wasted on hands the iron does not answer yet, and the gate gives the school's two digits an order: sit first, be shown after) and pointedly not waved by the stead's teaching regard (the stock-taking line now counts only the valley's own lessons, because a town's secret is priced like its coal); its keep is +2 wear off every sitting at any bench in any world, the lesson pattern's whole point (know-how crosses where ledgers do not). The guild is the town's first faction with a real ledger on the bearer: the bond sworn at the guildmaster's one always-listed digit (10c down, once per world, written as a guild/guild_sworn fact so it resets at the crossing like every ledger), gated on Commerce level 1 because the guild bonds trades, not intentions: the market's chalk is the reference the oath is sworn against, which makes the town's two new systems feed each other in the right order (sell lots, earn the name, swear the bond, sell better). The mark's worth is flat like Commerce's own coin: +1 per town lot, riding TownHaggle beside the level, seasoning without outweighing (D-140's per-lot discipline extended). The moot-warden's guild topic rewrites to point at the open hall and reads the sworn state; the guildmaster's own topic does the same; every new board stays inside the shared nine with room over. The pilot walks the whole of it live (D-072/D-082): the road's market leg now also fires on worn iron or an unsworn proven name, TownMove queues smith and guildmaster behind the stalls with predicates shared verbatim with the digit driver (the D-140 lesson about shuttling errands, applied at design time), and the journey report grew its school line. Save v79 -> v80 (the stitch deals different lanes, and digits a v79 journal never held file iron, teach, and swear). Verified the established ways: 760 tests green (6 new TownDepthTests: the sitting's arithmetic through the real keys, the true-iron and short-coin refusals both taking nothing, the lesson's gate appearing only for proven hands and its keep measured at the wheel afterward, the bond refused unproven then sworn once and never twice, the mark riding the lot beside the level, both new boards inside the shared nine; TownTests' cast check grown to six with reachability held across 30 seeds; RegardTests' stock-taking hardened to the valley's own lessons). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds; drift healthy end to end (cycle 13 and 12 crossings on every seed, the market walked 10-12 times, 165-192 forge sittings per run with Smithing standing at 7-8 by the twelfth world, the bond sworn in 7-9 of 12 worlds, deaths 2,3,2,1,1 against D-140's 2,2,1,0,4); sim replay exact (seed 1: 17988 keys, cycle 13, turn 16801); worldgen harness purity exit 0 with the two new folk struck to {person} in the skeleton audit; the v83 sweeps are the new named baselines. Options set aside: the smith teaching Smithing uses directly for coin (buying XP; the school sells sittings over real wear, so every use still traces to a fight that earned it, D-014 kept); the forge outfiling the home bench per sitting (a school that made home obsolete would unbuild D-135; the two differ in price, not power); guild membership persisting across worlds (reputation is per-world by D-089's discipline, and the lesson slot already carries the cross-world keep); a separate guild NPC roster or hiring board (B3's caravan work is where guild employment becomes play; today the guild sells standing, not jobs); gating the bond on lots-sold count instead of Commerce level (the level is the same number cooked down, and it reads on the sheet). Deferred: step 10's second half (the law's own book on the bearer with crimes feeding it and the moot as its mouth, Persuasion seeded in the pleading, the sheet re-laid for a 13th skill); tournaments and the property rung (the town-life boxes); the guildhall's interior holding anything (the property rung's natural room); guild work as errands (B3's caravans).

### D-140: The market town: B2's first cut, and the world's first peopled site (2026-07-21)
Plan 2026-07's step 9, the far country opened: the market town the wayhouse signposted (D-138) stands behind a gate arch at the east road's end, and with it the first cut of everything B2 named: authored chunks stitched per seed, a real market with fact-of-place prices, and Commerce, the 12th skill. The architecture call came first and shaped the rest: the town is a SITE, not a third overworld. A walled town is interior by nature, and Mode.Site already gives an interior all its rules for free (its own map behind an entrance tile, the overworld's verbs staying outside: no camping in a lane, no stride down a market row, the door-crime ledgers never confused), where a third overworld would have forced OnRoad's bool into a region enum today, which is exactly the machinery B3 is scheduled to build once B2 has forced its shape into existence; the plan's own sequencing, kept. What a site never had before is people, so the NPC machinery grew its missing half: Npc.SiteId places a person inside a site, NpcsHere reads area- and site-aware (the town's folk inside the walls, nobody at all in the fighting deeps), the bump opens talk in site mode, and the presenter draws and hints people wherever they stand. The map is the Daggerfall lesson at slice scale, authored chunks stitched per seed: a 46x26 walled rectangle, a main street from the west gate and two cross-lanes cut whole so no quarter ever seals, and six 14x11 plots filled from an authored library (the market row and the moot yard stand in every town, because they are why a town exists; house-lanes, a well square, gardens, and a sealed guildhall draw and shuffle on the town's own stream, so two worlds' towns share their parts and never their arrangement, and every piece of road laid before the town block is byte-identical by construction). The cast is four: the provisioner, the hidemonger, the herbmonger, and the moot-warden, each with the smith's register (short own topics, a working counter) and a market greeting that opens no stead's books on the bearer. The market is the arbitrage ladder's third rung and its longest walk: hides at 5c over the cart's 4c and the bench's 3c (D-124's spread extended), herbs at 6c over the stillroom's 5c and the bench's 4c (D-081's), and the market loaf at 4c under everyone (a town at a crossroads eats better than anyone; it does not undercut the levy because nobody carts bread two days home a loaf at a time). Commerce is seeded exactly as Smithing was (D-135's discipline): use-grown, fed one use per lot sold at a town counter and only there, cost-gated by construction because the town's prices stand above the valley's own by definition (the margin IS the walk east, and the goods were earned where they grew: D-014 extended to trade), and the practiced tongue adds its level flat in coin to every town lot (per sale, not per unit, so the craft seasons the trade without outweighing the goods). The law and reputation web is surfaced in a mouth before it exists as machinery (research/11's biggest missed lesson, taken at slice scale): the moot-warden speaks the moot-stone, the bearer's still-empty book, and the carriers' guild with its closed door, all of it step 10's build, none of it hidden. The waykeeper's far-country topic now names the town standing at its door. The pilot walks it live every world (D-072/D-082's line): the road trip grew a market leg (with the road's yield in hand, the gate before the mouth; inside, stall to stall, sell, and out), the talk driver sells the lot the counter buys, and the journey report and JSON grew the market's tallies. One honest render find: the 24-row sheet could no longer hold 12 skills and a deep question's second option (the suite caught the clipped row), so the sheet was re-laid dense, the blank rows gone and the key hint riding the bottom border. Save v78 -> v79 (a gate digit a v78 journal never held now walks the town, and its stalls move coin, goods, and a new skill's uses). Verified the established ways: 754 tests green (8 new TownTests: the stitch deterministic across 30 seeds with the whole cast walkable, distinct, and REACHABLE from the gate arch on foot, the gate crossing both ways onto the road, the market's arithmetic at both mongers with Commerce fed per lot and an empty pack counting nothing, the valley benches feeding no Commerce, the practiced tongue's level in coin, the market loaf with the short-coin no-slates refusal, the warden speaking law and guild with no counter inside the shared nine, and the town replaying key-exact; NpcTests hardened to per-site ground and (area, site, pos) distinctness, DeepKnackTests' exclusion list and the snapshot skills string grown the Sleight/Smithing way). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds; all five moved off the v81 baselines exactly as predicted for a pilot with a new errand, and the drift is healthy end to end: every seed reaches cycle 13 with all 12 crossings, the market walked 10-12 times and 20-24 lots sold per run, Commerce standing at level 2 in every finished run, deaths 2,2,1,0,4 against D-139's 1,1,0,0,2 (the market leg keeps the bearer out east longer under the road's weather; every world still cleans up); sim replay exact (seed 1: 17357 keys, cycle 13, turn 16432); the worldgen harness holds purity at exit 0 with the town riding the site census and the town's name struck to {town} in the skeleton audit (D-137's discipline both ways: the generator grew, the chart grew with it); the v82 sweeps are the new named baselines. Options set aside: the town as a third overworld (forces B3's region enum early and hands a walled town the wrong physics: the camp, the stride, and the crime ledgers all want a wall between them and a market); extending the road map eastward (a town inside the road's map is a suburb with weather, and its interior would fight the sky rules); chunk rotation and mirroring in the stitch (shuffle alone already de-correlates arrangements, and rotated authored rooms read worse, not fresher: parts are authored for a reading direction); Commerce as a per-unit price bonus (level 4 at five hides would out-earn the hunt itself; flat-per-lot seasons instead of dominating); a Commerce feed on any sale anywhere (selling at the wood's edge teaches nothing about worth: the craft is knowing both numbers, and only the walk east proves it); town npcs on the generic villager list (their boards would inherit nine topics they have no house for; the smith's register fits a stall). Deferred: step 10 whole (the guild joined and the law's own ledger on the bearer, smithing lessons at a town forge, Persuasion in the moot's mouth, tournaments and the property rung); road encounters and the peddler's restock still standing from D-138/D-132; B3's region machinery (the second settlement name pool is already straining toward D-049's region entities); the guildhall's sealed door (authored shut on purpose: step 10 opens it).

### D-139: The nine-key wall audited, and the granary's flood teeth (2026-07-21)
The two A3 v1 follow-ons (D-134) cashed as one small decision before B2 leans on the boards they guard. The topic-budget audit ran first and its suspicion was right, one door over from where D-134 pointed: a full world fills all nine general villager digits by itself (the stead, the raids, the shrine, the black arch, the long mound, the stone ring, the wanderer, the old songs, and D-133's season's-news made nine), and the audit's sharpest finding is structural: there are no plain villagers. Every Villager-kind door is a named one carrying at least one offer digit (the steadholder two, the woodward and herbwife one bench digit each), so in exactly the fullest worlds (tier 2+, a carried song, season news landed, the wanderer still talked of) the news topic pushed a bench-keeping board to ten entries on nine keys, the bench digit off the end of the keyboard: the sell path closed by gossip, which is D-041's wall failing in the one state the guard test never built. The fix follows D-134's own reasoning to its end. D-134 moved the news off the steadholder because the steadholder makes that news; D-139 finds the villagers' shared nine simply has no room for retrospect at all, and moves the season's news to the shrinekeeper's door, the one board in the stead with slack (three static topics, no offers, D-114's own note that the keeper's door is where the stead's side lives when the nine are full): the keeper sweeps the stead's center and watches its seasons pass for a living, so the label reads true there, and the D-109 every-fact-gets-a-reader discipline holds with the reader moved, not lost. A new fullest-world guard test (LessonsTests) builds the overflow state deliberately, tier 2 with a carried song and news landed, proves the woodward sits at exactly nine (the wall itself, no key to spare), and walks every talking door at or under it; the old tier-3 guard test stands beside it for the ordinary worlds. Then the granary's teeth, the weather clause D-134 tracked the day the deck grew the washout: a flood that comes down while the granary stands takes nothing, because the work the bearer funded sits on staddle stones above the water the old low granary could never argue with. The flood still claims its night whole (no raid rides drowned fords, in either direction: the calendar's claim rule is the weather's, not the take's), the event writes washout_stood instead of fords_washout, the keeper's news digit reads the morning after in the stead's own pleased voice (the coin credited without the bearer named, the D-106 register), and the levy stays uncalled because nothing was lost to call it over. This is the facility pattern paying forward exactly as D-134 designed it: the granary was already insurance against the winter by brim arithmetic; now it is insurance against the water by clause, and both derive off the one funding fact with no new state. Save v77 -> v78 (talk digits sit differently under a v77 journal wherever season news stood, and a granaried washout keeps its measure). Verified the established ways: 746 tests green (2 new: the flood turned away read through the real key surfaces, keeper's door and all, and the fullest-world audit; 2 hardened onto the news topic's new door, the D-121 precedent). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds; all five moved off the v80 baselines only inside the key stream, with equal key counts and all 191 report lines byte-identical per seed, exactly this change's signature: the pilot recomputes talk digits live, so the same ration buys press keys shifted by the news topic's departure and nothing else moves; sim replay exact (seed 1: 16926 keys, cycle 13, turn 16025); the v81 sweeps are the new named baselines. No worldgen change, so no harness run owed (the D-137 discipline cuts both ways). Options set aside: leaving the news at the woodward's and herbwife's doors with a yields-when-crowded rule (a topic that appears and disappears with the board's fullness is illegible, and D-041's law is that digits are dependable); trimming a general topic instead (every one of the nine is some fact's only villager reader, and the news was the youngest arrival with the best other home); giving the skald or smith the news (both sit at eight of nine already, and the keeper's board alone has room to grow); granary teeth against the drovers or the winter too (the drovers are a sale, not a loss, and the winter already lands on the granary's deeper brim by D-134's arithmetic: one clause per system, each honest). Deferred: the keeper's board is now the news' home and has five slots of room, which future season cards (A2 follow-ons) should budget against; B2 proper, next.

### D-138: The east road: travel as play, and the camp under the sky (2026-07-21)
Plan 2026-07's B1, the multi-region lane's opening move and the generator's first growth since D-137 charted it. The world gains a second overworld: the east road, a 72x16 drove-road map behind a RoadMouth tile scanned deterministically onto the valley's east edge, crossed both ways with the same '>' that opens every door, everything of it drawn off one derived seed ("road" off the world seed) with the valley mouth a deterministic edge scan, so no existing stream moves and pinned valleys keep their layouts to the tile (the D-137 harness confirms it: purity holds across the growth, and the waykeeper's name strikes to {person} in the skeleton audit, the first generator change to land with its range charted, which is exactly why D4 was sequenced first). The plan's rule was never ship distance that is only distance, so the road carries all four of B1's named pieces. Camp rests: 'm' (Command.Camp, a new journaled key) makes camp on any plain overworld tile, valley or road, opening the D-006 wilderness box: the kill is cooked first (raw meat into rations at the road's own fire, Cooking fed, the woodward's CookPlan reused whole), then the supper's ration buys the mending (6 base, +2 per Survival level, Survival counting a use only when the night truly healed, D-014's cost-gating), stamina and focus fill, fellows mend, and the night is real time (8 turns through AdvanceTurn, so the raids' clock and the calendar keep counting while the fire burns down); no supper is a cold camp, legs only. Weather exposure: the road keeps its own sky (RoadSky: clear, rain, cold), drawn per world and redrawn each coarse tick from its own stream wherever the bearer stands (replay's sameness), narrated only where it is felt: under rain or cold the step's +1 stamina is the weather's (factored into StepRegen), a foul-sky camp mends half, and the cold wind refuses a supperless camp outright. Encounter sites: the half-way glade, a road-tenanted Wilds site with its own map id and streams (a remnant dropped there is never confused with the valley trail's), tier-scaled harts, the hunt half a journey from any larder, which is what makes camp cooking a road skill. Supplies: the road's verges grow their own herb spots, and the wayhouse at the far end holds the keeper (NpcKind.Waykeeper, of the road and not the stead, with a greeting to match), three topics (the sky and the trail read live, the house, and the far country: B2's market town signposted in the keeper's own shrug), and two always-listed digits: road bread at the cart's flat price (TryBuyRoadRation shared with the peddler, D-124's counter generalizing) and a bed for 4 coin, the full rest with the bearer's taught habits riding it (tended iron and the steeped vial, factored out of DoRest so shrine and bed share one pair of helpers) but no shrine menu, because no essence is counted where the Aegis does not anchor. The two overworlds never bleed: sites, people, herbs, the pony, the gleanings, the stride, the bump, and the pilot's blocked cells all read through area-gated views (NpcsHere, HerbsHere, SiteHere, OnRoad flags on Site, Npc, and Mount), the crime family's door ledgers stay the valley's (the wayhouse's dark is a keeper's dark, refused with its own line), NearHouse beats stay the stead's lanes, and the beast at the bearer's side takes the mouth with them while one left grazing keeps its own map and waits. Death and the crossing both come home (OnRoad false: the Aegis catches where it anchors, and every world is entered from its own valley). The pilot walks it all live (the D-072/D-082 policy line): with the valley's sites settled it takes the mouth, hunts the glade, camps on the kill where the ground is plain, picks the verges, and comes home, deterministic as ever; the journey report grew its east-road line and the JSON its RoadsTaken/NightsCamped columns. The build also caught and closed a latent D-100 seam the road exposed: a ridden double-stride only ever ran the ground-pickup checks on its landing tile, so a rider could orbit a herb forever without picking it (found live: seed 88888's pilot burning a whole world's key budget circling one spot, jk jk jk to the 60000-key wall); GatherAt now runs for both tiles a stride crosses, with a regression test pinning it. Save v76 -> v77 ('>' at a mouth a v76 journal never held now walks a second overworld, 'm' passes real nights, and the wayhouse's digits move coin, rations, and rest). Verified the established ways: 744 tests green (9 new RoadTests: the road grown deterministically with the keeper walkable and the glade tenanted, the mouth crossing both ways and the glade's site looked up on its own map, the beast taking the mouth at the side and grazing when left, the camp cooking and mending and feeding both crafts through the real 'm', the cold camp restoring only the legs, the sky ruling the step and the night including the cold refusal, the wayhouse selling bread and the bed with the short-coin refusal, the verges picked from the road's own list with the valley's wood untouched, and the road replaying twin-identical; 1 new ForagingTests regression for the stride's stoop; NpcTests, BarrowTests, and CrossingTests' greedy bot hardened honestly onto the second overworld, the D-121 precedent). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds; all five moved off the v79 baselines exactly as predicted for a pilot that now walks a road every world, and the drift is healthy end to end: every seed reaches cycle 13 with all 12 crossings, the mouth taken 12 times and 1-4 nights camped per run, and deaths equal or fewer on every seed (1,3,3,2,3 down to 1,1,0,0,2: the road's herbs and camps arm the climb); sim replay exact (seed 1: 16926 keys, cycle 13, turn 16025); the v80 sweeps are the new named baselines. Options set aside: the road as a Site (Mode.Site would bar the beasts from the one lane built for them, and site rules are a dungeon's: the road is an overworld or it is nothing); extending the valley map east instead of a second map (re-deals every pinned layout and makes the road a suburb; a bounded second area behind a real mouth is the plan's own shape and prototypes B3's region machinery); dynamic weather per turn (a per-tick sky is legible and cheap, and the coarse tick is already the world's weather clock, D-132's winter and D-133's washout both living there); camping as a submenu (one key, one night, state-read behavior: the D-041 register); night encounters interrupting the camp (wants overworld spawn machinery no system owns yet; tracked as a follow-on, not smuggled in). Deferred: road encounters beyond the glade (ambushes, travelers met, the pacing layer's D1 eventually owning their cadence); exposure past the stamina-and-camp clause if the road ever wants a deeper bite; the far country itself (B2's market town, the road's east end opening); per-region prices at the wayhouse counter (B3, with the road's flat price as the seam); the road's own columns joining the worldgen measure when a chart wants them; camping in the deep sites, deliberately refused (the dark is not for sleeping in).

### D-137: The worldgen evaluation harness: the generator charted before it grows (2026-07-21)
Plan 2026-07's D4, the research/13 expressive-range recommendation, built now on the plan's own timing rule: before the generator grows again (B1's road, B2's town), so every later generator change lands with its range charted instead of guessed at. The harness is two halves. The measuring half is WorldEval in Aegis.Core, pure reads over a generated World with no I/O, no RNG, and no Game: Measure reduces one world to its numbers (names, the story drawn, cast size, fact-graph composition by type, compiled storylets, sites with their tenants and their stones and coffers, gleanings, herb spots, the pony, and the one generation-time price spread that exists today, the blight's bread base, honestly labeled a placeholder until B3's per-region spreads give the column real work); Summarize folds a tier's worlds into its row; Skeletons emits every prose surface a world generated (fact details and compiled story lines) with the generated names struck out by whole-word replacement (people, raiders, stead, world), longest first so no name half-replaces; AuditSkeletons counts what repeats across worlds; and Digest is an FNV-1a fingerprint over everything the measure and dump read. The batching half is `aegis worldgen` in the CLI (WorldgenRunner): --seeds N from --start across --tiers A-B, straight through WorldGen.Generate with no play in between, since direct sampling at (seed, tier) is the clean way to chart the generator's own range (the journey sweep stays the carrier for play-reached states, regard, wrath, raids, which it already reports). Three modes: the prose report (per-tier story mixes, fact and storylet spreads, site tenancy, name-variety counts, the skeleton ledger's most-reused lines), --json for CI (WorldgenReport through the same source-gen context as the journey's), and --dump, the generate-then-curate feed the prose-variety work wants (plan D3's correction at adoption: talk-gated prose is pilot-unexercised by design, so the repetition audit needs every surface a world CAN produce, pilot-visited or not; the dump is exactly that, and D3 and D4 now share the one harness as planned). Every world is generated twice and its digests compared, so a generator that grows a hidden input fails loud in the same run that charts it; exit code 2 on any mismatch, so CI can gate on it. The first 240-world run (30 seeds, tiers 1-8) already reads clean signals: purity holds everywhere; the tier bands stand exactly as built (tier 1 three sites and one eligible story, tier 6+ eleven sites and six); settlement names 30/30 distinct and world names 29/30 (the one collision is honest: direct generation passes no takenNames, that ledger being the character's); story mixes are visibly uneven at depth (gold-rush 12 of 30 at tier 6+ against war-of-faiths' 3), the harness's first real finding, recorded as data to watch rather than a thing to fix; and the skeleton ledger says what the design already knew, that worldgen prose is authored texture with name slots (220 distinct skeletons over 10220 surfaces), the baseline D3's fact-keyed fragments will be measured against. No save bump (v76 holds): WorldEval is pure new code, Game untouched, no RNG stream moved, no key digit added. Verified the established ways: 734 tests green (6 new WorldEvalTests: the measure reading the world it was handed and the tier-1 band, the deep bands and the camp's tenants in the measure, the digest pure across calls and telling seeds and tiers apart, the skeletons striking every generated name while leaving tokens rather than holes, the audit counting repeats across worlds on hand-built data, the tier summary folding its worlds with the mandatory mouths landing one per world); all three CLI modes exercised live (the JSON parses and carries the full report, the dump feeds the raw surfaces). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds and all five byte-identical to the v78 baselines outright, exactly as predicted for pure new code; sim replay exact (seed 1: 13395 keys, cycle 13, turn 12550); the v79 sweeps are the new named baselines, byte-equal to v78. Options set aside: extending journey --json as the carrier (the plan's own "cheap to start" suggestion, corrected at adoption for D3's half and equally wrong for D4's: the pilot never talks, so play-derived output structurally under-samples the prose, and a batch of direct generations charts range faster and cleaner than a batch of climbs); driving real Games through debug-hook crossings to sample deeper tiers with lineage (pollutes the chart with one pilot's path, and the debug hooks are the tests' door, not a harness's; direct (seed, tier) sampling is the standard expressive-range shape); baking the harness into the test suite as assertions on the distributions (a chart is for reading, not asserting; the distributions will move on purpose as the generator grows, and only the purity check has a right answer, which is why it alone gates the exit code); a name-frequency chart beyond distinct counts (wants NameGen pool introspection; the distinct counts already catch collapse). Deferred: wiring `aegis worldgen --json` into whatever CI shape the repo eventually grows (the command is the contract; there is no pipeline yet to call it); the D3 build-out proper (fact-keyed prose fragments composed per surface, measured against this baseline); per-region price spreads joining the measure when B3 lands them; charting play-reached faction states across seeds if the journey sweep's single-seed reports ever want aggregating; the story-mix unevenness at depth, watched not fixed, until a session decides the eligibility weights want tuning.

### D-136: The crossing split audited, and the builder's echo (2026-07-21)
Plan 2026-07's A4, the stead layer's closing check, and it resolves the parked legacy-echo question in the same stroke. The audit half found the layer already split clean, which is the D-089 derive-from-facts discipline paying out: every piece of stead state added since D-131 sits in the right bucket by construction. World bucket, gone at the arch: the calendar (cleared and re-seeded by ScheduleWorldFuture, D-132), the season deck's stream and its once-per-world card guards (a re-derived Rng and the facts the cards write, D-133), all five works and the two derived caps they raise (the works read event facts, StoresMax and DraughtCap read the works, D-134/D-135), the raids' clock, the stores, the watch, the levy, and both faction ledgers. Character bucket, crossing whole: the Smithing craft, the gear and its wear (the bench's medium), and the satchel's vials, where the audit's one judgment call stands: a bearer who crosses carrying three draughts under a wing-raised cap keeps all three in a cap-2 world, because the cap gates the drawing and never the carrying, so the third vial is a consumable earned like any herb, not the wing leaking forward. One benign latent found (_nightSpokenFor survives the arch but is reset at the head of every tick before any read) and one hygiene fix made: the crossing's refill now reads `Stores = StoresMax` instead of the bare constant, equal today and right by construction should a world ever open with works standing. The echo half answers the parked question with the smallest true shape: story only, never mechanical. When the old world's stead had works standing, the crossing presses one fact (legacy/builders_hand) into the new world's history by the exact road the D-054 patronage traces walk, inside the hushed-name gate, because raising a stead's walls with open coin is the bearer's song and a hushed world was never told who paid; the fact never compounds (only the last world's stead echoes, no accumulating list on the bearer), and its one surface is a NearHouse storylet, the-builders-hand, the founding talk retelling a drover's story of a stead a walker built up, with a whole-ladder variant when all five works stood. Nothing stands pre-built, no coin moves, no regard pays: the fresh-world promise holds whole, and the parking-lot entry closes. No save bump (v76 holds, the D-061 precedent): no new digits, no RNG stream moved, and the echo path needs a funded work, which no journal-driving pilot ever does. Verified the established ways: 728 tests green (3 new SteadFacilityTests: the whole ladder funded and the echo heard in the next world's founding talk through the real NearHouse surface, with the works gone, the lofts and the satchel back at their bare brims; a bare stead leaving no echo; the hushed name stilling it), and the per-piece crossing coverage the audit leaned on already standing across nine suites (ScheduleTests, SteadDeckTests, RegardTests, RaidsTests, SteadFacilityTests among them). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds and all five byte-identical to the v77 baselines outright, exactly as predicted (the echo is fund-gated and the pilot never talks); sim replay exact (seed 1: 13395 keys, cycle 13, turn 12550); the v78 sweeps are the new named baselines, byte-equal to v77. Options set aside: an accumulating echo, one fact per built world riding a persistent list on the bearer like PatronDeeds (the plan said keep it light, and a chain of built steads reciting itself is a saga, not talk; one drover's story is the register); a mechanical echo, discount or head start on the next ladder (dilutes the fresh-world promise the question was guarding, and a coin echo is a coin-to-power channel across worlds, the D-109 discipline extended); pressing the echo per work with five facts (five surfaces for one idea; the whole-ladder text variant carries the distinction at one fact's cost); skipping the hushed gate on the D-120 argument that stories cross regardless (the silences cross unhushed because they were never the bearer's song; the building is exactly the bearer's song, so it hushes with the rest). Deferred: the A3 v1 follow-ons still standing (the shared-nine topic-budget audit, granary teeth against the washout); a later world's stead asking after the builder by name if the mythology pipe ever grows hands; D4's worldgen evaluation harness, the plan's next step after the layer closes.

### D-135: The ladder's second rung: the stillroom's wing, the smithy bench, and the Smithing seed (2026-07-20)
Plan 2026-07's A3 v2, built the session after D-134 and closing the stead half of the D-025/D-036 sink ladder at its planned five works. Two entries join the steadholder's bench, appended so the older digits hold (D-041), each funded once per world for the D-131-guarded single point of regard. The stillroom's new wing (35 coin) deepens the remedy the way the granary deepened the lofts: the satchel's draught cap rises two to three while the wing stands, and because the cap became one derived property (DraughtCap reading the event fact, the StoresMax shape exactly), the herbwife's pot, the Stillcraft rest-steep, the drink on the road, and the vial tipped between a downed guest's teeth all read the deeper rack with no further code. The smithy bench (45 coin) is the rung the plan flagged as a seed, and it earns one design novelty: it is the one work whose standing entry becomes a verb. The digit that raised the bench is the digit that works it after, so the bench costs the ladder no second digit and the menu keeps room for nothing it does not need; unbuilt it reads as a price, built it reads the most worn piece by name and wear (state-read labels, D-041's law, and the trade menu re-labels after every press). A sitting files wear off the bearer's most worn piece, 4 base plus 2 per Smithing level, and Smithing, the 11th skill, counts a use only when the sitting truly moved iron: cost-gated by construction (D-014), because the wear itself was earned in the fights that put it there, the same discipline that feeds Spellcraft only on workings that worked and Sleight only on lifts that came away unseen. The craft is deliberately seeded at home and schooled elsewhere: B2's town smith holds the lessons, so the stead bench starts the ledger the town will deepen. The smith's wheel sink (D-025) stays honest by texture, not by gate: the bench is slow (one piece a sitting, a green hand at four wear a press) and priced in the turns the raids' clock keeps counting, while the wheel stays instant and whole for coin, so deep in a bad season the coin road still gets taken. Everything derives off the funding facts (the D-089 shape): no reset code, the crossing takes the wing and the bench with the World bucket, and the test says the rest in one line, the works were the world's, the hands are the bearer's, so the Smithing uses cross untouched. Save v75 -> v76 (two bench digits a v75 journal never held now raise works, deepen the satchel, file iron, and feed a skill). Verified the established ways: 725 tests green (3 new SteadFacilityTests walking the real key surface: the third vial drawn at the herbwife's own bench after the refusal at two, the filing 10-6-2-0 with uses counted only while iron moved and the nothing-for-the-file sitting counting none, the crossing taking both works and neither the cap's old two nor the craft's one use wrong; the shared-nine guard test now opens the steadholder's bench too; two enum-growth hardenings, DeepKnackTests' exclusion list and the snapshot skills string, the Sleight precedent). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds and all five byte-identical to the v76 baselines outright, as predicted (talk-gated, pilot-unexercised, test-covered, the D-107/D-124 precedent); sim replay exact (seed 1: 13395 keys, cycle 13, turn 12550); the v77 sweeps are the new baselines. Options set aside: a separate always-listed work-the-bench digit (spends a digit for nothing the fund digit cannot carry once its selling job is done; the one-digit life-cycle is the pattern a growing ladder wants); a coin price per sitting (the bench's price is patience and the season's turning, and a bench that charged coin would just be the wheel moved indoors); regard per sitting (a bearer filing their own axe is housekeeping, not a perceivable deed; only the raising pays, D-131's guard); working every piece per sitting (one piece keeps the sitting legible and the label honest about what is on the bench); knacks for Smithing (the craft is a session old; the Spellcraft precedent says let a skill settle before it asks questions). Deferred: B2's smithing lessons and forge work proper (the town school); the A3 v1 follow-ons still standing (the topic-budget audit, granary teeth against the washout); A4's crossing-split audit across a sweep, the layer's closing check.

### D-134: The stead's works: the facility ladder's first rung (2026-07-20)
Plan 2026-07's A3 v1, and the stead half of the aspirational sink ladder open since D-025/D-036 finally begun: the home stead becomes something the bearer invests coin in and comes home to. The surface is the steadholder's own bench, the woodward's proven D-071 pattern: one talk digit ("The stead's works") walks the bearer out along the fold walls into a trade menu of three works, always listed with state-read labels (D-041's law), each funded once per world. The palisade (40 coin) modifies the raids: every greedy raiding night, the mustered ones included (D-132's answer meets the timber too), is blunted to a plain measure, under its own narration each way; the plain raid was deliberately never its business, so the war still bleeds, the boldness ledger still climbs, the watch still posts on the greed the stead can see, and clearing the camp stays the one true exit (D-023's rule: coin buys relief, never the ending). The watchtower (30 coin) modifies the watch: eyes on the hills from first dark need half the bodies, so a turned night eats no upkeep and the watch can no longer bare the stead guarding it (D-105's dark exit closed by purchase, which is exactly what a tower is for). The granary (25 coin) modifies the stores: the lofts' brim rises two measures (StoresMax), recovery, levy answers, and the far fields all fill to the new brim, the made-good season is named at eight, and the hard winter lands on a buffer instead of a levy; the price bump stays keyed to the old line by construction (bread is already at its floor above six, so the deep measures are insurance, not deflation). The discipline throughout: each funding writes the event fact its effect hangs off, and the standing works and StoresMax are derived straight from those facts, so replay rebuilds them for free and the crossing clears them with the World bucket without one line of reset code (the D-089 shape); regard pays exactly once per work, +1 at the raising (D-131's guard held: a coin-priced ladder is never a recurring reputation channel). The real cost surfaced was digits, not coin: the steadholder's fullest board already stood at the shared nine (D-041's wall, guarded by a live test), so the bench digit was paid for by the steadholder ceding two topics that other doors carry whole: "The season's news" (D-133's own test already read it at another door; the steadholder makes that news and should not also be its gossip) and "The wanderer" (the well's talk; the pointing-the-way test repointed to another villager, the D-121 hardening precedent). The Snapshot gains the three works and StoresMax for observers and the D4 harness. Save v74 -> v75 (steadholder digits that fell dead under a v74 journal now open the bench and move coin, facts, and the raids' arithmetic). Verified the established ways: 722 tests green (7 new SteadFacilityTests, every funding walked through the real key surface, bench digit and all: the palisade blunting the greedy and the mustered nights, the tower sparing the watch its bread, the granary climbing to the new brim and eating the winter as a buffer, the once-only funding and once-only regard, short coin raising nothing, and the crossing taking the walls). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds and all five byte-identical to the v75 baselines outright, exactly as predicted (the pilot never talks: the works are talk-gated, pilot-unexercised, test-covered, the D-107/D-124 precedent); sim replay exact (seed 1: 13395 keys, cycle 13, turn 12550); the v76 sweeps are the new baselines. Options set aside: three always-listed offers on the steadholder's talk menu itself (busts the shared nine in the fullest state, and the ladder means to grow: the bench is the pattern built for exactly this); the palisade turning plain raids too (the war must keep bleeding or the raids' whole economy closes for coin and the camp stops mattering); a regard gate on funding (the stead takes any honest coin for its own walls, and the guard that matters is D-131's, already held); serializing the works (derived from facts, the same reason the calendar and the ledgers never serialize). Deferred: A3 v2 (the stillroom extension and the smithy bench, which seed Smithing at home and want their own benches' room); the shared-nine topic-budget audit the digit work exposed (the news topic can still co-occur with a full board at the other offer-keeping doors); granary teeth against the washout when the deck grows a weather clause.

### D-133: The season deck: the stead's own news on the tick (2026-07-20)
Plan 2026-07's A2, built on D-132's calendar the session after it landed. On any coarse-tick night no scheduled future has claimed, the season may deal one card from a per-world stream (`SeedTree.Derive(World.Seed, "stead_deck")`, one-in-three a tick): small, mixed-valence, consequence-dense, the fanfic test applied to events. Four cards open the deck, each dealt once per world and guarded by the fact it writes. The far fields (fortune): an outlying farm's cart puts a measure back, eases bread, and can lift a standing levy, dealt only while the war still prices the lofts (a recovered season climbs on its own, D-089). The drovers (a trade with two faces): the stead sells a measure at a hill price, coin in its box and dearer bread on the bearer's board, dealt only while the lofts hold three or more (the stead keeps its own line above the levy's). The fords washout (foreshadowed weather): the river read aloud one tick, the flood down the next through the calendar, claiming its night whole like the winter, taking what the low granary held, able to bare the lofts by water and call the levy; nothing cancels weather. And the banns (the calendar's first cancellable promise): a wedding put on the calendar in the open, the feast spending a measure on purpose if the lofts hold three, put off with its own narration if the season eats them below the line first; the muster (D-132) showed a threat with an exit, this shows the exit runs both ways, and the wedding deliberately does not claim its night (the dens do not check the banns; a raid riding through the feast is the season's own kind of story). Delivery keeps every discipline: each card writes a fact, narrates as it lands, gets an omen reader storylet in its warning gap (the-river-read, the-banns-heard, the-signs-read's pattern), and a new "The season's news" talk topic reads the latest season event back after the fact, which also pays the D-132 follow-on for event/hard_winter and event/muster_broken readers. A held-deck test hook keeps the nine choreographed tick tests about the cadence they test; the deck's own nine tests cover the cards, and an organic test proves the season deals from the world's own seed. Save v73 to v74 (deck draws move stores, prices, and the calendar under a v73 journal). Verified: 715 tests green; twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds; sim replays exact on seeds 1, 7, 2024; live in the sweeps every card deals and every leg fires (winters 9-10 of 12 worlds, washouts land 6-9, weddings come off 1-5 with one put off on seed 2024, drovers 4-6, far fields 1-7, all three omen readers speak); the season's-news topic is talk-gated and pilot-unexercised, the D-109/D-111 precedent, test-covered. Option set aside: dealing the deck through the StoryletEngine (a Tick trigger) was rejected because tick events are simulation that fires wherever the bearer stands and touches engine state, while storylets are near-player delivery; the engine's role stays what D-132 set, readers over facts the simulation writes.

### D-132: Scheduled future facts: the world learns a calendar (2026-07-20)
Plan 2026-07's A1, the stead layer's opening move and the KDM timeline translated into Aegis's idiom (research/12: the future as visible, mutable data). The machinery is ScheduledFact (Schedule.cs): a future pinned to a coarse tick (the same clock the factions act on, D-079) with an optional foreshadow tick (the omen that makes it perceivable ahead of itself), an optional CancelWhen (the designed exit, D-023's rule extended forward in time, every cancellation narrated because a future that quietly fails to happen teaches nothing), and an optional HoldWhen (a due night that cannot fire waits a tick, the den-under-attack idiom). The calendar is runtime state rebuilt by replay (entries come from the world's seed or from replayed deeds), never serialized, cleared at the crossing: the World bucket's, like the ledgers. It runs at the head of the coarse tick, and a night a future claims is claimed whole: no raid rides through a blizzard, and no recovery cart creaks in behind one. Two first uses. The hard winter: every valley schedules one at the bearer's arrival, its tick (3-5) drawn from the world's own seed, foreshadowed one tick ahead (omen/hard_winter written, the stead's talk turns to weather, and a new storylet reader, the-signs-read, lives only in the gap between warning and weather); when it lands it takes two measures from the stores, so bread rides the same price bump the raids move, it can call the levy at the last measure, it bares the lofts under its own narration if it takes them to the boards (standing down an unfeedable watch, a hole the tests caught: D-105's rule reached by the weather's road), and nothing cancels it: the counterplay lives in the omen's tick. The dens' muster: the cull that first teaches the dens dread (wrath's second rung, D-078) sets their answer on the calendar, announced the day it begins (omen/dens_muster, the raids topic reads the hills while it looms), due two ticks out, held while the bearer stands in the camp, fired as that night's raid, greedy by force of numbers whatever the dens' nerve, riding every rule a raiding night keeps (a posted watch meets it, bared lofts leave it nothing); and cancelled the only way such things are, the camp emptied first, narrated as the fires going out one by one. Save v72 -> v73 (every world's stores now move on a tick no v72 journal kept, and the storylet draws shift under the new facts). Verified the established ways: 706 tests green (8 new ScheduleTests: the seed-drawn winter, the omen ahead of the landing, the claimed night, the cull setting the muster and the topic reading it, the mustered raid two ticks out, the camp emptied breaking it, the held night, the crossing leaving the old world its futures; three pre-existing tick tests hardened honestly onto the new season: the cull test now breaks its own muster, the bare-out test's dark exit closes by the winter's road on seed 42, the levy recovery waits out the claimed night). Twins byte-identical on all five sweep seeds; all five moved off the v73 baselines exactly as predicted for weather in every world, drift healthy end to end: 12 crossings everywhere, deaths identical on three seeds and +2 on two, seed 7's keys byte-identical with only the narration moved; sim replays exact (seed 1: 13395 keys, cycle 13, turn 12550; seed 7: 16950, 13, 13005; seed 2024: 12517, 13, 11788); the v74 sweeps are the new baselines. Live in the sweeps: the omen speaks in 10-11 of 12 worlds and the winter lands in 9-10 (the rest crossed first, honestly), the signs are read at the well 3-4 times a run, and the muster is set and then broken in all 12 worlds per run (the pilot clears camps inside two ticks: the cancellation is the live path, the mustered raid itself pilot-unexercised and test-covered, the D-109/D-111 precedent). Options set aside: the peddler's announced return as the second use (the plan's own example, but it exercises no cancellation, and the machinery's whole point is a threat that can be answered; the restock stays a tracked deferral toward D-124's stock growth); checking cancellations between ticks (news travels at tick speed, and the tick-checked muster-breaks reads right); serializing the calendar (replay rebuilds it for free, the D-089 shape). Deferred: A2's stead event deck writing to the calendar (several entries foreshadowed via this machinery); the peddler restock as a scheduled future; talk readers for event/hard_winter and event/muster_broken after the fact (the winter remembered, the answer that never came); the calendar surfaced in the Snapshot for observers and the D4 harness.

### D-131: The July 2026 plan adopted: four lanes, the stead layer first (2026-07-20)
Design-only session, no code (the D-114 precedent). The July research sweep (research/11 Daggerfall's scale lessons, research/12 Kingdom Death: Monster's settlement phase, research/13 the wide procedural-generation survey) was distilled into `design/plan-2026-07.md`, and this decision adopts that plan and wires it into the tracker. The five calls made with the user stand as recorded there: world scale is multi-region (a handful of regions at valley density, never a Daggerfall sprawl); the stead layer is the facilities version (scheduled future facts, a stead event deck on the coarse tick, a 5-8 facility coin-sink ladder, the crossing split, with named stead folk deferred not rejected); all four lanes run with the stead layer first and worldgen scale-up after its core lands; the first new region is a market town with market, smithy, law, and guild texture; and NG+ freshness is worlds that differ in kind, a per-world twist library as a long-term pillar. The wiring: the plan's items are now roadmap boxes (a stead-layer section for A1-A4, a pacing-and-freshness section for D1-D4, B-lane lines joined to the boxes that already existed: the facility ladder onto the open D-025/D-036 sink-ladder line, region machinery onto D-049 and the caravan/arbitrage and peddler-stock deferrals, the evaluation harness onto D-083's journey JSON), the roadmap's phase map now points at the plan as the current sequencing story, and the plan's open questions joined the parking lot. Three corrections were found at adoption and folded into the plan rather than left to trip over: B1 claimed camping among its existing verbs, but camping is an open D-006 box and lands as part of B1; the freeze-the-generator principle claimed current practice honors it, but a save-version bump re-deals live worlds rather than preserving them under their birth generator, so the principle is only half honored, deliberately, and true generator-version pinning is parked as a pre-save-format-freeze decision; and D3's repetition audit named the journey sweep as its carrier, but talk-gated prose is pilot-unexercised by design, so the audit needs a generate-then-curate surface dump (every storylet and topic a world can produce, pilot-visited or not), which is also D4's natural seed, so the two share one harness. One guard was added to A3: a built facility may pay regard once as a perceivable deed, never per-tier or recurring, because a coin-priced ladder that pays regard repeatedly is a coin-to-reputation channel and the D-109 discipline (restitution never turns a profit) extends to purchases. Options set aside: leaving the plan as a standalone artifact with the roadmap untouched (the tracking rule exists precisely so work does not live in one document; two competing sequencing stories is how they drift); treating the twist library as a new open question beside the tier-7+ one (it is a third candidate answer to D-058's question, so they are parked together to be reconciled, not run in parallel); and starting A1 in the same session (the wiring is its own small honest unit, and A1 deserves a full session). Deferred: everything the plan sequences, starting with A1 scheduled future facts; the parked questions (named stead folk depth, legacy echo scope, town law vs stead shame, twist governance, pacing-layer authority, generator pinning).

### D-130: The Long Siege: the pool's sixth spine, and the fen-leaguer gets its story (2026-07-20)
The sixth story template ships, paying the parking-lot deferral tracked since D-121 closed the named launch list, and it binds to the one deep site that had a whole identity and no spine: the fen-leaguer, which D-057 built as "the siege that outlived its object" and whose own deed line already knew the ending ("it never yielded, bearer; it only emptied, and no one thought to tell them that either").
