Save v37 to v38 all the same, by the v35 precedent: three storylets enter the weighted draws, so a v37 journal's talk and lane events can resolve to different winners from the first eligible hook on. Verified the established ways: 392 tests green (seven new: the reckoning said to the thief's face once and only once, waiting for the third door, stilled by restitution; the tale carried on the lane once per world; the keeping shown to the stead's own, withheld below the rung, withheld from watched hands); two journeys byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through sim exact. Options set aside: a talk-menu topic for any of the three (the nine-digit wall, respected a sixth straight time); the confrontation as a forced scene or a mechanical turning-out (barring doors and prices is D-086's ground already; the reckoning is the stead's voice, and the storylet channel is built for voice); a coin or goods payload on the cellar beat (the showing is trust made visible, and D-077's collision lesson holds at the top of the ladder; the `secret` fact is the payload); gating the reckoning on the shame fact alone (facts are history; the saying must still when the ledger clears, the same live-state call three decisions running). Deferred: content consuming the `confronted` and `secret` facts in their turn (a making-right beat at the sills, the cellar mattering in some later raid or storm); the raid tick growing toward true state vectors; and the pillar's remaining forks as before (raider-perceivable transgressions, wrath facts on a first consumer, multi-axis state vectors, all wanting steer).

### D-087: The stead's teaching: the own rung's boon, paid in the coin that crosses (2026-07-19)
The eighth faction increment, paying the deferral both D-085 and D-086 carried: the top of the bright ladder finally pays. "The stead's own" (rung 3, regard 5, reachable in a world whose raids are ended and whose mound goes quiet) was the only rung with no boon, a title and nothing behind it. The boon chosen is the teaching: every showing the stead sells is shown freely to its own. Mechanically small (lesson prices read as zero under the gate) and deliberately so, but the currency choice is the design: the friend rung's boons pay in this world's coin, the purse (D-077) and the price (D-080), and by D-077's collision lesson the top rung must not pile onto them; lessons are the one currency in the stead's gift that crosses the arch, banked on the bearer past death and waygate alike (D-052), so the top of a ladder that resets every world pays, fittingly, in the only coin that does not. The wiring follows the established grammar exactly: a SteadsTeaching gate beside FriendsPrice, an OwnRung constant beside FriendRung, the crossing narrated the moment it lands (D-023's rule) with a taking-stock variant when every showing is already the bearer's, the bench and smith labels renaming themselves ("freely, to the stead's own": label text only, so no menu digit moves, the D-041 rule), and the coin's refusal itself narrated at the moment of the showing ("Not from you"). Suspicion closes it the way it closes the friend's terms (D-086): know-how is trust made plain, so an unwelcome bearer crossing the own rung is told, in so many words, that the saying is not said to them, and because the gate rides live Shame, not the shame fact, restitution at the sills reopens the craft, the designed exit honored again. The herbwife's clean dressing (price 0, taught through her hands) is untouched by construction. Save v36 to v37: a v36 journal that bought a showing while at the own rung paid coin this version does not take, so it replays richer from that press on. Verified the established ways: 385 tests green (four new in RegardTests: the opening at the own rung with the free showing and the graph fact, the price standing below the rung, the withholding under suspicion and the reopening after restitution, and the taking-stock line when there is nothing left to show); two journeys byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through sim exact (cycle 13, turn 10425, four deaths, the D-085 baseline held, since the pilot buys no lessons); the four-seed sweep unchanged in shape. Options set aside: a fourth, unbuyable lesson taught only to the stead's own (the richer version of the same idea, but a new lesson wants a mechanical effect worth having, its own teaching surface, and a teacher present at the crossing moment, which the raid-word reaching the bearer at the camp cannot honestly stage; deferred, not rejected); a coin or goods gift at the rung (duplicates the welcome purse's currency, the exact collision D-077 warned about); a steadholder menu surface for the boon (the nine digits are full, the same wall four decisions have respected); gating on the `regard` own fact instead of the live rung (facts are history; the gate must close if suspicion rises after, so it rides live state, the same call D-086 made from the dark side). Deferred: the fourth lesson above, if a mechanical effect worth teaching arrives; rung-3 content through the storylet channel (a beat only the stead's own are shown); and the pillar's remaining forks as before (raider-perceivable transgressions, wrath facts on a first consumer, multi-axis state vectors).

### D-086: The stead's suspicion: pilfering, shame, and the coin on the sill (2026-07-19)
The seventh faction increment, and the pillar's missing half begun: the home faction's Infamy axis, opened by the game's first transgression verb. Until now there was nothing the bearer could do that the stead would count against them, which left D-023's dual reputation standing on one leg; the fork was what the first transgression should be, and theft won it: the grab key beside an overworld house (an inert "nothing here to take" until now) pilfers the door, a ration's worth taken, once per door per world. Thematically it is the sharpest choice available, because taking grain by night is precisely what the raiders do, so the verb casts the bearer as the thing they have been slaying, and the stead's ladder answers in kind. The ledger is the keyed dictionary's dark twin: a `_factionInfamy` map beside `_factionRegard` (the raiders' wrath migrated into it, since wrath was always their count against the bearer, which leaves both dictionaries meaning one thing each), with the stead's entry read as Shame. The ladder is the plainest in the game and on purpose: three houses, thresholds 1, 2, 3, one rung per door robbed (in a stead of three doors nothing taken stays secret, so there is no witnessed-versus-unseen mechanic, only witness flavor: a villager within four tiles marks you from the lane, else the counted loaves will speak by morning). The rungs cost in three distinct currencies, D-077's collision lesson applied in reverse: watched (rung 1) closes the hearthtale's telling (the D-085 storylet gains a live-shame gate) and opens a closed-doors NearHouse storylet, the shame fact's first authored consumer, D-085's seam read from the dark side; unwelcome (rung 2) closes the friend's price and the welcome purse (both narrated when withheld, per D-023's perceivability rule); named a thief (rung 3) bars the steadholder's larder outright. Shame runs beside regard, never against it: the camp-slayer who robs a door is a friend to the stead and unwelcome in it at once, both titles on the HUD rail, the dual-axis point made visible. The designed exit (D-023's no-stalemates rule) is restitution at the scene: the same grab key at a robbed door leaves six coin on the sill, twice the loaf's worth, and walks the ladder down a rung, each easing named; a repaid door is closed both ways. Precedence is the one subtle call: at a corner shared between a robbed door and an innocent one, repay outranks theft, because a mistaken press must never commit a worse deed than the one intended, so a thief who wants the next door must find an angle their conscience is not standing on. Rung crossings write `shame` facts (watched, unwelcome, thief) as permanent history even after restitution clears the live count: a stead remembers having watched a bearer, and the storylet gates ride live Shame precisely so paying the sills back reopens what suspicion closed. Everything is replay-rebuilt, nothing serialized; the crossing clears both infamy and the door ledgers with the world. Save v35 to v36: the overworld grab press changed meaning, so a v35 journal that pressed g near the stead replays differently from that press on. Verified the established ways: 381 tests green (thirteen new in ShameTests covering the ladder, the theft's full narration, once-per-door, the full pack turned away, restitution door by door, the precedence rule, the friend's price closing over untouched regard, the withheld purse, the barred larder, the withheld hearthtale, the closed-doors storylet, and the crossing reset); two journeys byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through sim exact (cycle 13, turn 10425, four deaths, matching the D-085 baseline exactly, since the pilot never robs a door); the four-seed sweep unchanged in shape. Options set aside: striking a villager as the first transgression (combat plumbing reaches the talk surface nowhere today, and a violence verb wants the combat-depth pillar's steer); theft through a talk or trade menu (the steadholder's nine digits are full, and a menu theft reads as a shop exploit rather than a deed); a witnessed-versus-unseen shame split (dead content when three clustered houses and three adjacent villagers make every door watched; kept as flavor only); shame subtracting from regard (rejected as the whole point of dual axes: the deed ledger and the trust ledger move independently and both show); fact-gating the storylets on the shame fact rather than live shame (the fact is history and would hold doors closed after restitution, cutting against the designed exit). Deferred: a transgression the raiders can perceive (aiding a den is unimaginable today, but the relation matrix will want it); wrath facts when a first consumer exists; theft scaling if worlds ever grow more houses; the stead acting on its suspicion beyond commerce (a confrontation beat, a storylet of being turned out); and rung-3 boons on the bright ladder, still open.

### D-085: The rungs written to the graph, and the friend's hearthtale (2026-07-19)
The sixth faction increment, delivering the last of D-077's named friend-rung boons, the rumor the folk keep from strangers, and more importantly the seam it rides on: regard rungs are now facts. Every rung a stead's regard crosses writes a `regard` fact into the world graph (known, friend, own; a two-rung stroke like the camp-clear writes both it passed), which makes reputation queryable by everything the fact graph already feeds: storylet Requires, ask-about topics, and the template compiler's casting patterns. That is the structural payload: authored content can gate on reputation with a one-line declarative pattern, no engine hook, and the locality D-076 promised holds by construction because world facts die with the world. The rumor itself is the seam's first passenger, carried by the storylet channel exactly as D-080 said it wanted (the villagers' talk menu has no digit to spare): a Talk-triggered, world-scoped, priority-8 storylet requiring the friend fact, so once per world a villager tells the bearer the story the stead keeps inside its own fence (the hard winter, the unsaid name, the tarred door-posts), the Aegis marks that no ledger buys the telling, and a `rumor` fact is written for later content to build on. No stranger ever hears it: the requirement is the gate, tested from both sides. Narrative-only by design, the honest reading of storylets v1's additive contract (D-077's own lesson stands: a friend-rung boon must stay out of the stead's existing gift currencies, and the welcome and the friend's price already pay in coin). Save v34 to v35: a new Talk storylet enters the weighted draw, so a v34 journal's talk events can resolve differently from that world on. Verified the established ways: 368 tests green (three new: the rungs written to the graph and dying with the world, the hearthtale told to a friend once per world, and kept from strangers); two journeys byte-identical; the emit-keys string, now read cleanly off the JSON report's keys field, replayed through sim to the exact end state (cycle 13, turn 10425, four deaths); the sweep run through --json for the first time, tidier for it (all seeds cycle 9, regard peak 5). The journey totals held: the bot talks to villagers only when the arc asks, so the new draw rarely enters its path, while a talkative player's journal moves, hence the bump. Options set aside: a talk-menu topic for the rumor (the nine-digit cap, the same wall three decisions have now respected); a mechanical grant riding the telling (rejected on D-077's currency-collision lesson and the vision's hospitality principle); regard facts for the raiders' wrath too (nothing gates on them yet; written when a first consumer exists, the same discipline that deferred the faction type in D-076). Deferred: content that consumes the `rumor` fact (a topic, a later storylet calling back to the telling); rung-3 boons (the stead's own); and the pillar's remaining forks as before (stead-Infamy on a transgression verb, multi-axis state vectors).

### D-084: The keen-eyed walk: --wits, the perception build demonstrated live (2026-07-19)
Tooling, closing the last deferral standing from D-063: `aegis journey --wits` plays the perception build. D-063's default raise policy deliberately leaves Wits at the baseline so the report can show D-061's dulling at every arch, and named the Wits-raising mode a future toggle in the same breath; this is that toggle. Under the flag the pilot raises Wits first, until innate acuity sits two over the baseline, the exact point D-061 called out as the perception build's identity (the head start alone clears the dulling floor, so every mastered kind reads Keen for good), then hands the shrine back to the usual survivability rotation. The demonstration lands exactly as the design promised: on seed 42 across six crossings, the baseline run softens every mastered kind Keen to Read at every arch, and the keen-eyed run holds every one of them Keen at every arch ("held", six for six), with the trade-off honestly priced in the same report: eight deaths against the baseline's five, the survivability raises deferred while the eye was bought. That is the build fantasy and its cost in one diff of two reports. The header names the mode in prose, and the JSON object (D-083) carries a witsDemo flag so a sweep can separate the two policies. One parameter threaded from the runner through NextKey to RaiseDigit; the default path is proven untouched by hash (a post-change default run is byte-identical to the pre-change master run). Verified: two --wits runs byte-identical; the --wits --json object parses with the flag set and Keen on the far side of the last arch; all 365 tests unchanged, no engine or save touch. Options set aside: raising Wits forever rather than to the floor-clearing point (past baseline+2 the read gains nothing, and the deaths only climb); a full policy-file surface for custom raise orders (two named policies are what the reports need; a policy language is tooling nobody asked for). Deferred: nothing; the D-062/D-063 tooling ledger is clear.

### D-083: The journey report goes machine-readable: --json for sweeps and CI (2026-07-19)
Tooling, paying the deferral D-062 named and D-063 repeated: `aegis journey --json` emits the run's whole report as one JSON object instead of prose, so a balance sweep, a regression gate, or CI can consume crossings as data rather than scraping sentences. The object carries everything the prose tells: the headline (seed, target, cycle and tier reached, stop reason, keys, turns, deaths), every economy counter the runner tracks (reclaim, chests and deep iron, knacks with their names, the hunt, the fire, the forage with the stillroom's coin, hides and their coin), the arc (reach label, the keeping's answer and cycle, the laying and the mending cycles), the Legend and the burden's share of it, the faction ledgers at their high-water marks (regard and wrath with their titles, raids suffered), and the full per-crossing detail (worlds and tiers, deaths in world, arms, sworn terms by name, burden, site outcomes, and the bestiary on both sides of the arch with bank and read). Implementation stays in the CLI's existing discipline: plain DTO records with enums flattened to lowercase strings, serialized through the same source-generated camelCase context the sim and pilot already use (AOT-safe, no reflection), with `--emit-keys` folding the key string into the object as a nullable field instead of a trailing line. The prose path is untouched, proven by hash: a post-change prose run is byte-identical to the pre-change run. Verified: two JSON runs byte-identical; the object parses and its numbers match the prose report's exactly on the master seed (turns 10425, four deaths, twelve crossings, 380 coin of herbs); all 365 tests unchanged, nothing near the engine or the save format. Options set aside: NDJSON per-crossing streaming (the report is one run's summary, and one object is what a sweep wants to collect per seed); reusing the internal Crossing records directly in the serializer (they carry engine enums and IReadOnlyList shapes; the DTO layer costs thirty lines and keeps the wire shape decoupled from the runner's bookkeeping); a --quiet flag alongside (JSON already replaces the prose entirely). Deferred: a sweep driver that fans seeds and collects the objects (trivial in PowerShell now, a first-class subcommand if it earns it); the --wits demo mode, still the oldest tooling deferral standing.

### D-082: The journey-bot takes the stillroom walk, proving the price spread live (2026-07-19)
The policy increment D-081 named on its way out, in the D-072/D-075 line: the autopilot now sells its herbs at the stillroom instead of the wood's edge, so the economy's first price spread is exercised live and reproducibly rather than standing untested in a menu. Purely Aegis.Cli (JourneyPilot and JourneyRunner), no Core touched, no save change, all 365 tests unchanged. The policy: herbs stop being the woodward's business (BenchErrand drops them; his bench digit no longer reaches for the herb entry), and a new low-priority overworld errand walks to the herbwife once the wood is picked, opens her bench through the same one-digit shape (the WoodEdgeDigit helper generalized to TradeOpenDigit, since it never cared whose bench it opened), sells the lot, and steps out; at her table only the simples are the bot's to sell, so the bench driver is context-aware rather than good-ordered. If she cannot be reached the sprigs simply ride the crossing as they always did. The runner's herb accounting switched from a price-constant multiply to reading the coin off the sale key itself, which is the honest general form now that two buyers pay differently, and the report names the stillroom's price. Results on the master seed: all 76 sprigs sell for 380 coin (five a sprig exactly, against 304 at the old bench), and the run came out shorter, not longer: 11101 turns to 10425, deaths held at four, because the stillroom stands in the village the bot already walks for the smith, so the sale slots into a road it was on rather than adding one. The sweep agrees across every seed (44 sprigs, 220 coin, all reaching cycle 9). Verified the established ways: two journeys byte-identical, the emit-keys string through the independent sim reproducing the exact end state (cycle 13, turn 10425, four deaths, satchel empty). Options set aside: weighing distance per world to pick the better bench dynamically (the stillroom pays strictly more and sits on the smith road, so a distance heuristic is complexity without a case; if a future world lacks a herbwife the null-guard already falls through); selling at whichever bench is met first and topping up at the other (the satchel zeroes at the first sale, so the split sale does not exist). Deferred: nothing new; the alchemy lane and the remaining faction routes stand as before.

### D-081: The herbwife's stillroom: the second bench, and the vendor pattern proven general (2026-07-19)
Pays the deferral D-074 and D-075 both named: the herbwife becomes the simples' true buyer, behind her own bench. One talk digit ("Trade at the stillroom") opens a trade menu of her own, exactly the wood's-edge shape (D-071), which was the point of building that shape as a pattern: the second bench cost two entries in BuildOffers/BuildTradeOffers and a pair of flavor lines, no new machinery at all, and the pattern is now proven general rather than a one-off. The stillroom holds two entries. First, the herb sale at the apothecary's price: five coin a sprig where the wood's edge pays four, priced by the buyer across the bench (a HerbPriceHere view over the two constants), because the woodward was always a middleman passing the simples on to the mending-folk (D-074 said so in his own sale line) and the mending-folk's own table pays full worth. That makes the first genuine price-choice in the economy, arbitrage in miniature: sell where you stand for less, or carry the satchel to the village for a coin more a sprig, the walk itself the spread (D-025's regional-price principle at the smallest possible scale). Second, the wound-dressing moved in off her talk menu, and this was forced, not decorative: in a full world the villagers' shared topics fill eight of the nine digits, and her old conditional mending offer plus a new trade digit would have burst the cap the moment both stood in a wounded world (the same wall D-076 and D-080 respected); the stillroom's table is where that work would happen anyway, so the bench absorbs it, always listed (digits never shift under a buyer's hand, D-041's law) and reading "you are whole" when there is nothing to dress. Save v33 to v34: her menu's digit layout changed (a v33 journal's mend-digit lands on the trade entry now) and herbs sold to her pay differently. The journey-bot is untouched: it mends at the shrine and sells its herbs at the wood's edge it already walks past, so every journey number held exactly, and whether the bot should learn the one-coin walk to the stillroom is left as a policy question, not an engine one. Verified the established ways: 365 tests green (three new: the stillroom paying five with the full-worth line, the dressing on the bench and off the talk menu, and the two benches quoting their own prices over one unsold satchel; two existing mending tests moved honestly through the new bench path, growing a BenchKey helper beside OfferKey). Two journeys byte-identical; emit-keys through sim exact (cycle 13, turn 11101, four deaths); four-seed sweep unchanged. Options set aside: the herbwife paying the same four as the woodward (rejected: two buyers at one price is a redundant menu, not an economy; the spread is the content); a poultice or salve for sale at the stillroom (wants the alchemy lane the herbs are ultimately for, D-074's own deferral, not a bench stub); moving the gleaning lesson to her too (it is the wood's lesson and the woodward's to teach). Deferred: the bot weighing the one-coin walk (a clean future policy increment in the D-072/D-075 line); the alchemy lane itself, for which the stillroom is now the obvious home; more benches as roles earn them (the smith's counter already behaves bench-like inline; the songhall's pledges are their own surface).

### D-080: The friend's price, and the stead's talk keeping its own ledger (2026-07-19)
The fifth faction increment, delivering the second of D-077's named friend-rung boons: the friend's price. Once a stead holds the bearer a friend (regard rung 2, earned by ending its raids), the steadholder takes a coin off bread: a new term in RationPrice's fact-derived formula, sitting beside the hearth-price (D-048) and stacking with it, inside the hungry road's doubling like every other scarcity-and-favor term. The design lines all follow from the ledger the pillar has already drawn. It is deed-earned, so the hushed name does not silence it where it silences the hearth-price: the two discounts are now a matched pair proving the local-versus-global split in a single formula (the name's coin off dies under the oath, the deed's coin off stands, tested side by side). It is per-world like the regard that buys it, gone at the crossing. And it composes with D-079 into a small economy of the same event: every raid prices bread up a coin, the deed that ends the raids prices it back down one, so the camp-clear now moves bread twice over (stops the hikes, opens the discount), which is the pillar's systems beginning to bear on each other rather than standing in a row. Perceivability: the offer label itself names it ("a friend's price") every time the menu opens, and the steadholder says it aloud once per stead on the first discounted loaf, a one-line acknowledgment tied to the deed. Alongside, the stead's talk starts keeping D-079's ledger: the goblin-raids ask-about answer sharpens with the raids suffered since the bearer walked in (one raid, or counted plural), naming the price each time, so the world speaks its own state back through the conversation surface with zero new topics (the villager menu sits at its nine-digit cap in full worlds, the same wall D-076 respected; texture goes into existing answers, not new digits). Save v32 to v33 (a v32 journal that bought bread after ending the raids keeps more coin from that turn on). Verified the established ways: 362 tests green (five new: the coin off at the friend rung, not given below it, not silenced by the hushed name, named in the offer label and aloud exactly once, and the raids topic counting the raids; two existing tests updated honestly where the new discount and the blight story crossed the old expectations, the grain-taken test now showing the raid's +1 and the friend's -1 standing side by side, and the below-the-rung test moved to an unblighted seed because a blighted world's barrow eases the base price through the story completing, a different mechanism than the one on trial). Two journeys byte-identical; emit-keys through sim exact (cycle 13, turn 11101, four deaths); four-seed sweep unchanged in shape. The bot's totals held again (it cooks rather than buys). Options set aside: extending the discount to the smith or the herbwife (the friend's price should start where the stead's own larder is, and one clean term proves the pattern; other benches can join when a boon ladder exists); a percentage discount (meaningless at a four-coin base); making the once-per-stead line a Player flag (a world-local bool reset at the crossing is the honest scope, like the unbindings). Deferred: the last D-077-named boon, a rumor the folk keep from strangers, wanting either menu-cap headroom or a non-topic channel (the greeting, a storylet) to carry it; boons at the third rung, the stead's own, so the ladder's top pays too; and the stead-Infamy half, still gated on a transgression verb (user steer).

### D-079: The raids are real: the first coarse-tick faction event (2026-07-19)
The fourth increment of the faction pillar, and the one that starts D-023's living-world half: a faction now acts on its own, without the bearer's hand in it. While a world's camp stands uncleared, the raiders come down on the stead every 160 turns (SteadRaids.TickTurns, the coarse tick), up to three times a world (the cap is the stead running out of things to lose): each raid writes an event fact into the graph, narrates the moment it fires (D-023's mandatory hook, the raid named and the cost named in the same breath), and thins the stores so bread costs one coin more at the steadholder for the rest of the world, folded into RationPrice's fact-derived formula between the blight base and the hearth-price discount, inside the hungry road's doubling since scarcity is what that oath multiplies. Design calls: the tick counts from the bearer's arrival in each world (a world-start turn stamp, not the global clock, so crossing mid-tick does not hand the new world's raiders a head start); the tick passes the bearer by while they stand inside the camp itself (a den under attack defends its own, which also keeps the incoherence of raiders raiding while being fought out of the fiction); clearing the camp is the designed exit condition, D-023's no-eternal-stalemates rule in miniature, but the grain already taken does not come back before the crossing, so dallying has a price that outlasts the deed that ends it. This turns the Raided Stead from backstory into live pressure: every turn spent foraging or dungeon-diving before the camp is a night the stead can be hit, the first time the game's clock has borne on anything but wounds, and it is machinery every later faction event reuses (the tick seam in AdvanceTurn, the event-fact-plus-narration shape, the price consequence channel). Surfaced in the snapshot (Raids) and the journey report's raids line. Save v31 to v32 (a v31 journal that bought rations in a raided world pays more and every coin downstream moves). Verified the established ways: 357 tests green (six new RaidsTests: the tick raiding perceivably with fact and price, the cap holding, camp-clear as exit, the grain not returning until the crossing resets it, the den defending its own while the bearer is inside, and the new world counting its tick from arrival); two journeys byte-identical; emit-keys through sim exact (cycle 13, turn 11101, four deaths, raids 0 in the fresh final world); sweep honest and seed-varied (12 to 25 raids per run: the bot clears nearest-site-first and forages, so it genuinely pays the dally price). The bot's totals held identical to D-078's, which is again informative: it cooks its own rations and rarely buys bread, so the price consequence does not touch its policy, while a bread-buying player's journal moves, hence the bump. Options set aside: the raid reducing the stead's regard for the bearer (rejected: the folk blame the raiders, not the one fighting them, and regard-negative events belong to the true Infamy half when a transgression verb exists); pausing the tick whenever the bearer is in any site (rejected: only the camp's own tenants are the raiders, and time passing elsewhere is the pressure working); the stores recovering when the camp falls (rejected: taken grain is taken, and the lasting price is what makes the timing of the deed matter); a random tick (rejected: a fixed tick adds zero RNG draws, keeps worldgen and combat streams untouched, and the player learning the rhythm is perception, not exploit). Deferred: raids escalating in kind rather than count (a burned house, a taken villager: wants consequences systems first); the stead visibly reacting (talk topics about the latest raid, the plaintiff's plea sharpening); other factions joining the tick (the true state-vector form, with per-faction vectors and transition rules, once a faction exists whose state has more than one axis); and the stead-Infamy half, still gated on a transgression verb (user steer).

### D-078: The raiders' wrath: the second faction, the enemy ledger, and the keyed store (2026-07-19)
The third increment of the faction pillar (D-023), and the one that makes it read as factions plural: the raiders now keep their own ledger on the bearer. Structurally this is the generalization D-076 deferred by name: the single Regard scalar on Game becomes a keyed per-faction store (a FactionId-keyed dictionary, with Regard and Wrath as views over it), so the third faction is a key and a ladder, not another field. The raiders are the stead's standing enemy, the oldest relationship on the ledger, and the wiring makes the relationship concrete rather than declared: emptying the goblin camp raises the stead's regard (D-076) and the raiders' wrath in the same strokes, a blow to one being a favor to the other. Wrath is the Infamy-shaped half of D-023's dual reputation, kept by the folk with most cause to count: it rises one notch for every raider slain (hooked in HarvestRemains, the one place every kill path meets, so melee, the loosed shaft, and the answered blow all count alike), climbs its own faster ladder (thresholds 1, 2, 4: hate compounds where gratitude steps; titles a name the raiders curse, a dread on the raiders, the bane of the dens), and resets at every crossing exactly as the regard does, because the next world's dens have not met the bearer. Perceivability (D-023's iron rule) is read honestly rather than noisily: the slaying itself is the perceived change (the bearer watched it land), so the ledger speaks only at rung crossings, three audible steps instead of a drumbeat of bookkeeping, plus the once-only Aegis aside the first time any den ever counts the bearer (hate is also a kind of regard, the deliberate mirror of D-076's nearer-weighing line). And the wrath has teeth the bearer can feel, the increment's mechanical face: past the dread rung (two raiders slain in a world) a raider's blow is feared before it is thrown and lands one point the weaker, never below one, applied to the raw damage roll after the dice so the draw count never moves and determinism holds by construction (RaiderWrath.Steadied, a pure function, unit-tested directly). In a tier-1 world of three goblins that means the third fights feared; in the six-goblin deeps the whole back half of the camp does, so the dread scales with the tier by the world's own arithmetic, no tuning knob added. Surfaced everywhere the regard is: the HUD's live rail in the enemy's red under the stead's green, the snapshot (Wrath, WrathTitle), and the journey report's new dens line beside the stead's. Save format bumped v30 to v31 (a v30 journal that fought deep into a camp replays those late blows softer and every downstream state with them); debug-cleared camps move no wrath, since no raider fell to the bearer's hand, which is also why every pre-D-078 regard test still reads a wrathless world. Verified the established ways: 351 tests green (eight new WrathTests: the compounding ladder and titles, the dread staying the hand only past its rung and never below one, a slaying raising wrath with the once-only aside, the second slaying crossing into dread with the aside spent, the camp emptied proving a blow to one is a favor to the other, the snapshot carrying wrath and title, the reset at the crossing, and the debug-cleared camp moving nothing); two journey runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through the independent sim reproducing the exact end state (cycle 13, turn 11101, four deaths, both ledgers reset in the fresh final world); and the five-seed sweep all peaking at the bane of the dens (wrath 6). The journey's totals happen to match D-077's exactly, which is itself informative: the dread softens late-camp blows, but the bot rests to full between fights, so the HP difference washes out before any decision it feeds; a played journal at the margin of death would feel it, hence the bump. Also fixed in passing: the v30 history comment in SaveCodec still described the abandoned coin-and-bread draft of the welcome; it now records the coin-only gift that shipped. Options set aside: wrath making raiders fight harder instead of softer (rejected: they already attack on sight, so there is no aggression left to raise, and terror of the bearer who empties dens is the honest reading, the Infamy that bites being the stead's cold shoulder, a different faction's ledger); a chain-wide wrath that carries across worlds (rejected on D-076's own locality principle, and the crossing already carries the global half in Legend); per-kill narration (rejected as combat-log noise, the rung is the perceived change); a formal relation matrix between factions now (deferred, one edge does not need a matrix, a third faction gives it two edges to hold). Deferred: the true stead-Infamy half, deeds that cost regard and a stead that turns cold, which needs a transgression verb the game does not yet have (there is nothing the bearer can currently do that the stead would count against them, and inventing one is a design fork worth the user's steer); the richer friend-rung boons D-077 named (a friend's price, a rumor kept from strangers, gated access); raider behavior actually changing under dread (fleeing, refusing to close) beyond the softened blow; and D-023's coarse-tick state vectors, the living-world half both ledgers sit under.

### D-077: The friend's welcome: regard's first boon, and the first faction touch on the save format (2026-07-19)
Pays the exact debt D-076 named in its own wrap, and turns the reputation from a thing the stead only speaks into a thing it does: the second increment of the faction pillar (D-023), taken the same night as the first, because a regard that is earned but never paid is a thinner v1 than the pillar deserves. The first time a stead comes to hold the bearer a friend (regard rung 2, reached by clearing the goblin camp, since the raids are its central grievance), its folk gather what coin they can spare for the one who has stood for them, a small purse pressed on the bearer with both hands. It is built as the deliberate opposite number to Legend's arrival-welcome (D-048), which the songs set out before the bearer has lifted a hand: this is earned here, by these particular folk, for what was done under their own roof. Three design calls hold it clean. First, coin, not bread: the arrival-welcome already gives bread, and a food gift on the most-cleared deed in the game piled onto every welcome the tests measure, so the purse stays clear of that larder and the boon reads as the folk's own thanks rather than a second helping; the purse always lands (D-023's perceivability rule, never a change the bearer cannot feel), and unlike the regard that bought it, the coin is the bearer's to carry off, crossing the arch as all coin does (the standing stays local and resets, but a friend's gift is kept). Second, once per stead: regard only ever rises within a world in this cut (no Infamy yet), so the boon fires exactly when the rung is first crossed and never again, with no flag needed; a barrow stilled first (rung 1) draws nothing, and the camp that follows crosses into the friend rung and pays. Third, and the sharpest line, the hushed name (D-051) does not silence it: that oath hushes the songs and every Legend favor because those ride on the bearer's name, but the stead's thanks is earned by a deed they watched with their own eyes, so it stands where the arrival-welcome would not; the songs may go unsung and the gratitude still comes. Save format bumped v29 to v30, the first faction change to touch replayed state: a v29 journal that ended a stead's raids now replays with five more coin from that turn on (D-076's regard itself was cosmetic and bumped nothing, exactly as it promised the boon would be the one to move the format). Verified the established ways: 343 tests green (four new welcome tests: the purse lands on reaching the friend rung, comes once per stead even across two deeds, does not come at the lesser rung, and is not silenced by the hushed name; plus six honest updates to crossing-math tests, Songhall pledge-weighing, oath-burden, and coin-conversion, each now carrying the extra five the gift converts to Legend, annotated so the underlying math stays readable); two journey runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through the independent sim reproducing the exact end state (cycle 13, turn 11101, four deaths); and the five-seed sweep unchanged in shape (all reach cycle 9 and peak at the stead's own). The gift reshapes the bot's economy a little (five coin per stead befriended funds a touch more iron), so its key and turn counts shifted slightly from D-076's while the death toll held at four, the expected mark of a real economic change under intact determinism. Options set aside: bread for the gift (rejected, it piled onto the arrival-welcome's own larder and muddied the welcome tests' very purpose, where coin stays clear); essence (rejected on the vision's own principle that boons are hospitality, never raw power, and essence buys attributes); a standing price break at the stead's own smith or bench (a real alternative and a good future boon, but it overlaps Legend's hearth-price and touches the pricing surface, where a one-time purse is the cleaner first step); firing the gift at a moment less trafficked than the camp-clear (rejected, the camp is exactly the deed the stead is grateful for, so paying there is the honest place). Deferred: the richer boons a friendship should open (a friend's price, a gift of goods, a topic or a rumor the folk keep from strangers, opened access), which is where the pillar goes next and where reputation starts gating real content; and the pillar's structural spine still unbuilt, the Infamy half, the keyed multi-faction ledger, a second faction with a relationship to the stead, and D-023's coarse-tick state vectors.

### D-076: Factions, first rung: the stead's regard, a local reputation the folk can perceive (2026-07-19)
The re-entry into the big design-fork pillars after the wilderness-living family (D-070 through D-075), and the first stone of the faction keystone (D-023, vision sec 2), taken as the piece that turns the world's existing deeds into a reputation the world holds back: the home stead now keeps a regard for the bearer. It is built as the deliberate opposite number to Legend's Standing (D-048): Standing is the songs of all the worlds and crosses every arch with the bearer, while Regard is these folk, this valley, this while, a per-world Fame that the far gate leaves behind when the world regenerates. Regard is earned only by deeds the stead can perceive, which is D-023's own load-bearing rule (a change the player cannot feel does not fire) applied as content: clearing the goblin camp ends the raids the whole stead lives under (+3, its central grievance), and stilling the barrow puts out the lights on the mound above the houses (+2), but the remote deep sites, a quarry hushed or a leaguer lifted leagues off, pass no regard at all, because the stead never learns of them; they are still real deeds, counted in Legend and written to the fact graph, just not felt at the hearth. The ladder is plain (rungs at 1, 3, 5: a known face here, a friend to the stead, the stead's own), so a world's two perceivable deeds walk it end to end. Every rise is surfaced the moment it lands (the perceivability rule from the other side): the deed line names what the stead now knows, a crossed rung names the new standing, and the first regard any stead ever holds draws the one line the Aegis keeps for it, naming this the nearer, warmer weighing that does not cross the arch. The regard reaches ahead of the bearer in the world too: once the folk hold you a friend, even a villager you have never met greets you as no stranger. It lives on the live status rail, not the permanent character sheet, because it is this world's and transient where the sheet is the bearer's for good. A SteadRegard helper (Faction.cs, mirroring LegendStanding) holds the ladder; the tally is a per-world field on Game, reset at the crossing beside the unbindings, journal-reconstructed like everything on the bearer and never serialized. No save-format change, and the reasoning is the point of this cut: Regard gates nothing mechanical yet, it only speaks and displays, so it feeds no RNG and no input branch and an old journal replays to identical mechanical state; the first regard-gated boon is the next increment and will be the version-bumping change. Verified the established ways: 339 tests green (nine new RegardTests: the ladder's step math and titles, the camp raising +3 perceivably with the once-only Aegis aside, the barrow raising +2 with the aside already spent, the reset at every crossing, the remote quarry deed passing no regard, the snapshot carrying regard and title, and the greeting warming to a friend while staying cold to a stranger); two journey runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through the independent sim reproducing the exact end state (cycle 13, turn 11129, four deaths, regard reset to 0 mid-final-world); and the five-seed sweep all coming to hold the bearer as the stead's own at their warmest (peak regard 5). Worldgen is untouched (no new draws), so pinned worlds stay byte-identical. The bot needed no new navigation: it already clears the camp and barrow, so it earns the regard incidentally, and the runner grew a "the stead: came to hold the bearer as {title} at its warmest (peak regard N)" line watching the counter's high-water mark. Options set aside: modeling the stead as a full Faction object with a state vector now (deferred, the single Regard scalar delivers the whole player-facing v1 and the data-model type earns its place with the second faction, where a keyed ledger replaces the scalar); one global reputation instead of per-world (rejected, it would collapse into Legend and lose the New Vegas local-versus-global split D-023 names); granting regard for the deeper deeds (rejected on the perceivability rule, which is exactly what keeps the pillar honest); a persistent talk topic reading the regard back (skipped to keep clear of the nine-digit villager menu cap D-071 fought, since the HUD and the earning-moment lines already surface it). Deferred, and this is the pillar's spine still to build: the Infamy half (a stead that turns cold, deeds that cost regard); generalizing the scalar to a keyed per-faction ledger; a second faction with a relationship to the first (the raiders as the stead's standing enemy is the obvious next, so a blow to one is a favor to the other); the boons and gates regard should buy (a friend's better welcome, a gift at a rung, access the stranger is refused), which is the next increment and the first to touch the save format; and D-023's coarse-tick state vectors with their mandatory narration hooks, the living-world half the reputation layer sits under.

### D-075: The journey-bot forages and sells its herbs, completing the wilderness family's live loop (2026-07-18)
Pays D-074's named deferral and, with it, closes the whole wilderness-living family into one autopilot-verified loop: the bot now hunts, sells hides, cooks meat, forages herbs, and sells them, every lane driven live and reproducibly. Purely additive tooling, JourneyPilot and JourneyRunner in Aegis.Cli, no Aegis.Core touched, all 330 tests unchanged, nothing near the save format. Two small pieces. First, a low-priority overworld errand: once the sites and any remnant are seen to, if this world still holds herb spots the bot walks to the nearest reachable one (ordered by Chebyshev then position for a stable pick), and because a spot is foraged on the step (D-074) the walk onto it is the whole errand; it clears the wood spot by spot until picked, then falls through to the arch, so it always terminates and never blocks a crossing. Second, the bench learns herbs: BenchErrand now also fires on a full satchel and BenchDigit sells the herb-lot (a new TradeDigit helper finds the good's digit), so the sprigs, which bank across the crossing, sell at the next world's bench beside the hides. The runner watches the Herb counter the same way it watches the hide: a step in the wood raises it (foraged), the bench drops it to zero (sold), and the report grew a "the forage: picked N sprig(s); sold M for K coin" line. The loop reshapes the bot's economy in a telling way: on the master seed it forages forty-four sprigs and sells them for a hundred and seventy-six coin, and that income arms it better at the smith, so the death toll actually falls from seven to five even as the run lengthens with the gathering. Verified the established ways: two runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through the independent sim reproduces the exact end state (cycle 9, turn 7236, five deaths, Herb 0, every sprig sold); and the five-seed sweep all forage their twenty-eight, sell them, and reach the mending, deaths 2-6, unchanged in shape. Options set aside: foraging before the sites (rejected, it would send the bot wandering for low-value herbs before the fights that matter, where gathering last, before the arch, costs only ground already near the crossing); selling herbs only when a hide sale already brought it to the bench (thinner, and the errand now brings it for herbs alone when that is all it carries); a value threshold so it skips distant spots (unneeded at four spots a world, and left as a tuning knob if the wood ever grows denser). Deferred: the same follow-ons D-074 named that are content, not tooling (the herbwife as a true herb-buyer with a bench of her own, the alchemy the herbs are for); and, still, the machine-readable report and the --wits demo mode.

### D-074: Foraging the wood: herbs, the Survival skill, and the last wilderness lane of the family's core (2026-07-18)
Rounds out the wilderness-living family's core (vision sec 6, D-006), the fourth increment in one night's arc that took the family from nothing to hunting, selling, cooking, and now foraging, all sharing the wood's-edge bench (D-071) so the surface stays small. Where the hunt yields hide and meat and the cooking turns meat to food, foraging is the gentlest lane: every world now grows herbs (World.Herbs, a new list placed on its own worldgen stream drawn after the gleanings, so pinned worlds and the gleanings' own layout are byte-identical, forest-only and spread out like them, and never on a gleaning tile so no spot is both), and a bearer who steps onto one picks it. Unlike the gleaning's hidden roots (D-052, which need the woodward's lesson to see), herbs are plain to any eye and take no lesson: anyone can stoop and pick, which keeps the barrier low and, for the autopilot below, keeps it from having to learn a lesson it does not buy. A picked spot yields a sprig (Player.Herb, a new counter, uncapped and journal-reconstructed like the hide, surviving death and the crossing) and grows a new use-grown skill, Survival (SkillId.Survival, the eighth, the wider wilderness lore of which foraging is the first feeder, with camping and tracking to come), whose level fattens the take the way Hunting fattens the hide. Herbs bank uncapped and sell for coin at the same bench, a little dearer than a hide apiece (HerbPrice 4 to the hide's 3) because the wood's simples are wanted for the mending-work (D-006's own example, the apothecary who pays well). So foraging feeds two of the vision's payoffs cleanly, a skill and coin, with no risk and no combat, the widest-open door into the wilderness family. The bench now holds four goods in stable order (hides, cook, herbs, teaching) and stays well under its nine. Save format bumped v28 to v29: a forest tile a v28 walk crossed plain now yields a sprig on the step, and the bench gained a sale entry, so an old journal that walked the wood or drove the bench replays to different state. The journey-bot was not taught to seek herbs or sell them in this increment (that is the clean follow-on, as bot-selling followed the hide-buyer in D-072), but because herbs need no lesson and are picked on the step, the bot forages them incidentally as its overworld pathing crosses a spot, so the gather path is exercised live even so: on the master seed it picks up two sprigs in passing while its behavior, keys, turns, and deaths stay identical to D-073's run (foraging is passive and Survival changes nothing the bot does), which is exactly the no-regression signal wanted. Verified the established ways: 330 tests green (four new ForagingTests: herbs grow in every world on forest, deterministic and clear of the gleanings; foraging a spot takes it, grows Survival, and needs no lesson; Survival fattens the yield; the bench buys herbs for coin; plus the skills-snapshot and deep-knack pins widened for the eighth skill); two journey runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through the independent sim reproducing the exact end state (cycle 9, turn 6072, seven deaths, Hide 0, RawMeat 22, Herb 2 foraged in passing); and the five-seed sweep all reaching the mending, deaths 2-5, unchanged. Options set aside: gating herbs behind the gleaning lesson like the food-gleanings (rejected to keep the barrier low and the future bot-forage lean, and because obvious plants need no teaching where hidden roots do); selling herbs to the herbwife, the canonical apothecary-buyer (thematically truer, but her talk menu is capped and giving her a bench of her own is the follow-on that would prove the vendor-bench pattern generalizes, where the woodward's bench already exists and the wood's simples are close enough to its trade); a Survival knack (deferred, as Hunting's and Cooking's were). Deferred: teaching the bot to seek herb spots and sell the satchel (closing the loop in the autopilot, the D-075-shaped follow-on); the herbwife as a second bench and true herb-buyer; and the rest of what Survival will feed, camping and tracking, and the alchemy lane the herbs are ultimately for.

### D-073: Cooking at the wood's edge: the first craft, fed by the hunt (2026-07-18)
The first of the vision's three craft trades (vision sec 6, D-006, D-016), and the piece the roadmap's own Phase-1 note named next: grow the wood's-edge bench. It pairs with hunting by design, the two wilderness/craft lanes chained into one loop (catch, cook, eat), and it lands where D-071 built room for it rather than standing up a new station, so the whole surface is small. The hart's yield changes to match: where D-070 handed over a ready ration, a felled hart now gives raw meat (a new Player.RawMeat counter, uncapped and journal-reconstructed like the hide, surviving death and the crossing), and only the hart, the dedicated quarry, does so; the war-boar keeps its field-ration (D-053), the meat grabbed in the thick of a fight. Raw meat is inedible as it is. At the woodward's trade bench (D-071) a new "cook" entry turns it into carried rations: a ration a cut, and a new use-grown skill, Cooking (SkillId.Cooking, the seventh, the first non-combat and non-hunting one), squeezes extra meals from the same carcass the way Hunting fattens the hide, all bounded by what the body can carry (RationCap) so a full larder cooks nothing and, crucially, wastes no meat: only the cuts that fit are put to the fire, the rest keep raw for later. That makes cooking feed two of the vision's payoffs cleanly, a skill and the larder, without ever punishing the bearer for hunting more than they can eat at once. The journey-bot learned to cook in the same increment, so the loop is exercised live: its bench errand now fires for hides to sell or meat to cook, and at the open bench it sells the lot then cooks what the larder will hold, one press each, before stepping back. Save format bumped v27 to v28: the hart pays differently and the bench gained a digit, so a v27 journal that felled a hart or drove the bench would replay to different state (the bump also covers D-071's own bench, which shifted the woodward's talk digit and shipped un-bumped). Results on the master seed: the bot cooks five cuts into five rations to fill its larder once early, then rests to stay whole so it needs no more, and hunts on, banking the rest of the meat raw; the arc still climbs to the mending untouched and the death toll holds at seven, unchanged from D-072. Across the five-seed sweep every seed cooks its five and reaches the mending, deaths 2-5. Verified the established ways: 326 tests green (five new CookingTests: the bench cooks meat to rations and grows Cooking, the skill squeezes extra meals, a full larder cooks nothing and wastes no meat, cooking fills to the cap and keeps the rest raw, an empty bag cooks nothing; plus the D-070 hart test updated to expect raw meat, not a ration, and the skills-snapshot and deep-knack pins widened for the seventh skill); two journey runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through the independent sim reproducing the exact end state (cycle 9, turn 6072, seven deaths, Hide 0, RawMeat 22 banked, Rations at the cap). Options set aside: cooking at the shrine or a new campfire (a new station and menu surgery, where the bench already had room and the bot already visits it, so the bench is both cheaper and the roadmap's stated intent); keeping the hart's ready ration and adding cooking purely on top (contrived, a hart giving both cooked food and raw cuts, where routing the hunt's food through the fire is the honest loop); making the boar give raw meat too (blast radius into the deep sites for no gain, so only the dedicated hunt yields cuts to carry); a Cooking knack at its thresholds (deferred, as Hunting's was, keeping knacks optional for a young skill). Deferred: teaching the bot to seek the bench when its larder actually runs low (it cooks opportunistically now, enough to prove the loop, but a hungrier policy would exercise it harder); recipes beyond raw-meat-to-rations (a hearty meal that tends wounds, herbs into the pot), which want the alchemy/foraging lanes that do not exist yet; and the rest of the wilderness and craft families.

### D-072: The journey-bot sells its hides, closing the economy loop in the autopilot (2026-07-18)
Pays the one deferral D-071 named in its own wrap: the sell path was covered by unit tests but not yet exercised by the journey-bot, so the hunt's whole loop (catch, cure, sell, coin) had no live end-to-end proof the way arming, looting, and the arc already do. Purely additive tooling, JourneyPilot and JourneyRunner in Aegis.Cli, no Aegis.Core touched, all 321 tests unchanged, nothing near the save format. The bot gains one overworld errand and one menu it drives on purpose. The errand sits just above the smith-buy in the priority ladder, so the coin off the wilds can fund the arming below it: with cured hides in hand and this world holding a woodward, it walks to the bench (the same NavKey-to-an-NPC-cell the arc-talk detours already use, every other person on the road blocked, the target's own cell allowed so the bump lands). The menu it drives is the D-071 bench: at the woodward's open talk menu with hides in hand it presses the "wood's edge" digit (found by scanning the offer list for TradeGood.Trade, since the woodward's talk offers do not shift), which opens the trade sub-menu, and there it presses the hide-sale digit; a single sale weighs the whole lot, so the next look finds no hides and steps back out, and because the errand only fires with hides in hand and a sale empties them, the bot makes exactly one selling trip per world's hunt, no oscillation. Both trade-menu dispatch lines sit above the StuckInMenu escape (which now also lists InTradeMenu) so the bench is driven, not fled. The runner watches the same Hide counter it already watches rise on a kill: a fall can only be a sale (death and the crossing carry hides whole, only the bench zeroes them), so a drop books hidesSold and coinFromHides (the drop times HidePrice), and the report grew a "the trade: sold N hide(s) at the wood's edge for M coin" line beside the hunt line. Results on the master seed (cycles 8): the bot sells all 34 hides it takes for 102 coin (the flat three apiece), the arc still climbs to the mending untouched, and the extra coin reshapes the downstream play (more iron bought, more pledged) so the death toll lands at seven, within the same 2-8 band the sweep already showed and not a regression. Verified the established ways: two runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through the independent sim reproduces the exact end state (cycle 9, turn 6072, seven deaths, Hide 0, every hide sold); and the five-seed sweep (1, 7, 99, 2024, 88888) all sell their whole take (hides times three: 48, 57, 45, 54, 48) and reach the mending, deaths 2-5. Options set aside: selling right before the arch instead of before the smith (one cleaner trip, but the hide-coin would only reach the next world through the crossing conversion, never this world's forge, so the hunt-to-arm loop would not show); teaching every villager a buy/sell surface (unneeded, the woodward is the built buyer and the only one the bot has an errand at); making the bot hold hides as a Legend play through the crossing conversion (a real alternative, but selling and arming is the richer demonstration and keeps the coin legible in the report). Deferred: growing the bench the bot visits (cooking, foraged-goods sale) is content for the engine, not the tool; a machine-readable report mode still waits on a sweep or CI ever wanting the crossings as data.

### D-071: The hunt's sell path: a hide-buyer at the wood's edge (2026-07-18)
Pays the exact debt D-070 named in its own deferral note, closing the coin half of the hunting lane so it now feeds three of the vision's four activity payoffs (skill, food, and coin) instead of two. The wall D-070 hit was structural: villager talk menus are single-digit-keyed and so hard-capped at nine entries, and every likely buyer is already at that ceiling (the woodward at up to eight shared world-topics plus its one lesson, the smith at nine when its gear is worn), so a flat sell entry had nowhere to go. The fix is the reusable vendor pattern the whole economy will lean on, and it mirrors machinery already shipping (the Unbinder's respec menu, D-016/D-034, reached from one talk digit): the woodward keeps exactly one commercial digit in the talk menu, "Trade at the wood's edge" (a new TradeGood.Trade that is not a good but a shopfront), and pressing it opens a trade sub-menu (InTradeMenu) with its own fresh nine digits. The Gleaning lesson (D-052), which used to sit as a top-level woodward offer, moves inside that bench, and the hide-sale joins it there, so the talk menu stays at or under nine (topics plus the one Trade digit) and the bench has room to grow (foraged goods, cooking, hunting gear, hunting lessons, all later). The woodward is the buyer on the fiction's own terms: it is the wood-warden and forage-teacher already, so the thing the wilds yields is its trade. Selling is by the lot: cured hides (Player.Hide, the D-070 counter that banks across death and crossings) become coin the bearer's own hand earned, told apart in the ledger from looted purse and from the dark's essence, at a flat few coin apiece (HidePrice, a property so a fur-prizing world or an oath can lean on it later; a good hunt buys a lesson or a modest piece of iron, it does not make the market). The Aegis marks the first sale once (Player.HideLineHeard), naming it a fifth ledger after counts, choices, songs, and lessons (D-052's four), and the first the bearer filled with no leave from anyone. No save-format change: the trade menu is transient and hides already persist as v27's journal-reconstructed counter, so a sale is just a journaled keypress. Verified the established ways: 321 tests green (a new hide-sale test that the bench weighs a lot for coin, flips the label to nothing-cured, sounds the fifth-ledger line exactly once, and refuses an empty bundle; plus the three woodward lesson tests rewritten to buy the gleaning at the bench a keypress deeper, and the nine-digit invariant test extended to assert the bench itself also fits nine, so the sell path has not simply moved the wall); two journey runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through the independent sim reproducing the exact end state (cycle 9, turn 5588, five deaths, thirty-four hides banked and unsold); and a five-seed sweep (1, 7, 99, 2024, 88888) all still hunting and climbing to the mending untouched. Options set aside: a brand-new tanner or furrier NPC carrying a flat sell offer (fits the nine within its own shared topics, but adds an NPC to every town and re-hits the ceiling at the next sellable good, whereas the sub-menu sets the pattern once and the woodward already exists as the right buyer); folding the sale onto the herbwife or the shrine (thematically wrong, and the herbwife's free digit only exists while the bearer is unwounded). Deferred: teaching the journey-bot to sell its hides, so the economy loop is exercised end to end by the autopilot the way the hunt itself already is (the bot has no bench errand yet, so the sell path is covered by unit tests and proven not to regress the climb; teaching town commerce to the bot is a clean separable follow-on); and the growth the bench was built to hold (cooking, foraging sales, hunting gear).

### D-070: The first activity lane: hunting the wilds (2026-07-18)
The first of the vision's four activity families to land (vision sec 6, D-006), and the re-entry into feature work after the journey-bot tooling arc (D-062 through D-069): a hunting lane, chosen as the most self-contained, highest-compounding start because it establishes the reusable activity-to-skill-to-yield pattern the other families and the dozen-plus unbuilt skills will follow, and reuses what already ships (the overworld, the telegraphed bow, the eat-and-heal loop). Tier-2+ worlds now hold the wilds (SiteKind.Wilds, own seed stream placed after every existing draw so pinned worlds stay byte-identical, a game-trail entrance stamped on the overworld), an open glade walled by treeline with a few gaps cut in it (the runs) and thicket-clumps for cover. It holds fleeing game, a new creature, the hart (MonsterKind.Hart), unlike every foe before it: it grazes until the bearer stalks within a few cells, then flees at the bearer's own speed, so it is never run down on foot, only taken by the bow at range (reusing D-050's loosed line) or herded into a corner where no step gains it distance and a bump ends it. A hart that reaches a run in the treeline is gone into the wood and leaves nothing, and nothing the hart does damages the bearer, so the whole of the wilds is a catch, not a fight. A hart brought down pays in the hunt's own ledger, not the deep sites': no essence and no coin, but meat for the pot (a ration, as the war-boar already paid, D-053) and a hide, and it feeds a new use-grown skill, Hunting (SkillId.Hunting, the sixth), whose level fattens the hide yield. The lane clears the vision's discipline that an activity feed at least two of skill, coin, food, or story: it feeds the Hunting skill and the larder. Save format bumped v26 to v27 (a new site shifts tier-2+ worldgen, and a felled hart pays in new ways). The journey-bot learned to hunt in the same increment: in the wilds it looses at a hart on a clear line or herds it into a corner, so the lane is exercised live and reproducibly like everything else. Results: on the master seed the bot hunts the wilds in every tier-2+ world and takes 22 hides across seven crossings while the arc still climbs to the mending untouched (D-068 unchanged), deaths holding at four; across seeds 1, 7, 99, 2024, and 88888 it takes 20 to 26 hides every run and reaches the mending every run, deaths two to eight. Verified the three ways: 320 tests green (six new HuntingTests: the wilds exists at tier 2+ and holds reachable harts, the hart grazes far and flees near, a felled hart pays hide and meat and Hunting but no essence or coin, the yield scales with Hunting, a hart at a run escapes leaving nothing, and the glade clears without writing the hollow's deed); two journey runs byte-identical; and the emit-keys string replayed through the independent sim reproduces the exact end state (cycle 8, turn 4589, four deaths, twenty-two hides). Options set aside: overworld wildlife (the first overworld-resident creature would rework the movement and turn loop, whereas a new site reuses the whole spawn-clear-pilot machinery, so it was the lighter and more consistent first cut, with roaming game left as a later, more immersive extension); making game fight back (a boar-style dangerous quarry in the wilds, deferred so v1 keeps the pure-catch identity distinct from combat); and character creation as the alternative Phase-1 start (deferred because its choices branch into breadth systems, races' cultures and backgrounds' skills, that do not exist yet). Deferred, and scoped out mid-build when it hit a wall: the hide-sell path, the coin half of the lane. Selling was in the approved plan, but the villager talk menus are hard-capped at nine digits, and the woodward (the natural buyer) is deliberately maxed at eight topics plus its one lesson, as is the smith when its gear is worn, so a sell entry has nowhere to go without a dedicated vendor menu. Rather than hack a fragile tenth digit, the sell waits on a hide-buyer with room to grow (a clean, separable next increment); hides bank on the bearer meanwhile, crossing the waygate like rations. Also deferred: a Hunting knack at its thresholds (the skill carries none yet, by the same choice that keeps knacks optional for a new skill); cooking the meat and leatherworking the hides; and the rest of the wilderness family (tracking, foraging, fishing, camping).

### D-069: The journey-bot swears its own terms at the arch, carrying a burden for Legend (2026-07-18)
Pays the last companion capability D-068's list named, and the one the original D-062 bundle named beside the restore path: the oath-crossings, the bot crossing plain where a bearer could set its own harder terms at the arch. Closed with a pure pilot addition, no Aegis.Core touched (the terms menu has stood since D-011 and D-047: at the arch, the digits toggle the seven oaths one by one, '>' crosses under the chosen set, and the burden carried through a world is honored in Legend, 10 per weight, on the crossing out of it). The pilot now drives that menu the way it drives the shrine's and the smith's: at the open arch it lights each sworn oath not yet lit with its catalog digit, then crosses; because a digit only ever adds a term there, the chosen set climbs monotonically to the sworn one and the cross fires exactly once, so the menu cannot loop. One rule sets which oaths it swears: take the ones this bot's own way of living already absorbs, refuse the ones that would cost it real blood or real growth. It bears the hungry road (it heals by resting at the shrine, never by bought bread), the spent edge (it re-arms at every forge and loots deep iron from each site, so a blade that wears twice as fast is one it was replacing anyway), and the hushed name (it never leans on songs or standing for power, and the arc's rungs turn on the bearer's own carried flags rather than a song crossing the arch, so a silent world climbs the very same ladder). It refuses the other four: the crowded dark and the old blood put more death in every den (the old blood the heaviest oath there is, a weight of 2), the slow mending lets a wound its step-off fight still takes linger and compound, and the lean dark would halve the essence its growth, and so its survival at depth, is fed by. The report grew a per-crossing terms line and a run-total oath line, the burden honored counted deterministically by reading the leaving world's burden as each crossing fires, which is exactly the count the engine turns into Legend right then. Results on the master seed: the bot swears all three oaths at every one of seven crossings, burden 3 each, and carries them the whole ladder down while the arc still climbs to the mending untouched (the keeping answered in cycle 5, a keeper laid down that cycle, the next mended in cycle 6, exactly as D-068 left it, proof the hushed name does not disturb the flag-gated reveal); 180 Legend is earned for the burden (30 at each of the six crossings out of a sworn world, the first leaving an unburdened one), and the death toll holds low at two. Verified the three ways the tool demands: two runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string (4231 keys) replayed through the independent sim reproduces the exact end state (cycle 8, tier 8, turn 4088, two deaths, oaths hungry_road,spent_edge,hushed_name, burden 3, Legend 1058), the engine's own log showing "the terms you carried through {world} are weighed with it, Legend grows by 30 more" at each sworn crossing, which proves the pilot drives the terms through no hidden state; and it holds across seeds 1, 7, 99, 2024, and 88888, every one swearing all three oaths at burden 3 and still reaching the mending in cycle 6, deaths two to five (seeds 1 and 7 tick up to five from D-068's one-to-four band, the honest price of the spent edge, small and well within budget). All 314 tests unchanged, no Aegis.Core touched, save format still v26. Options set aside: adding an oath path to the engine (it has stood since D-011 and D-047, so this was the pilot learning to set its own terms, not a rules change); swearing every oath for the maximum burden (rejected because the crowded dark, the old blood, and the slow mending put raw death in the deep sites, which would spike the toll and muddy the arc climb's clean determinism, so the burden the bot carries is the one its own play makes nearly free); swearing nothing until strong enough (unnecessary, since the three chosen oaths add no foe and no damage, so they are safe from the first crossing); scaling the burden up with the tier (a real option for a future demo, but a fixed principled set keeps the honored-Legend arithmetic legible and the determinism clean). Deferred, the last of the earlier lists: a Wits-raising demo mode for the perception-build identity; and the machine-readable report for a sweep or CI to consume the crossings, the arc, and the burdens as data rather than prose.

### D-068: The journey-bot walks the arc: the reveal ladder, the keeping, and the mending driven live (2026-07-18)
Pays the deferral every journey-bot decision since D-062 has carried, the last and heaviest: teaching the autopilot to climb the arc's reveal ladder by real feet and drive D-060's rarest grace, the restoring of a Severed, live on the shipping engine instead of standing it in for in-process (the D-060 proof-of-play test reaches the crossings only by Debug_ teleports, the very hooks a live proof must not touch). Like every increment since D-064 it is a pure pilot addition, no Aegis.Core touched: the whole ladder has stood since D-038, D-039, D-045, and D-060, its rungs gated on earlier rungs' flags and never on cycle counts, so a beeliner and a dawdler climb the same ladder in the same order, and this was only the pilot learning to walk it. The pilot now weaves the ladder into its overworld loop as flag-gated goals, each the exact mirror of its storylet's own When, so a single bump turns a rung and clears the goal and it never walks up to a face with nothing to say: it clears the hollow as always, which lands the first keeper's death and the truth with it; it rests at the shrine for the vision the instant the guilt has been spoken at a crossing (resting even with nothing to raise or mend, which it now seeks the shrine out to do); it seeks the mender for the two reveal tiers and the hermit for the one-at-peace, bearing the cost-witness for free by stepping onto the hollow's mouth on its way in; and once the commission is heard and the last stair stands in the world (tier 5+), it goes down the corridor to the Hearth, the step onto the stone opening the keeping, and answers it (refused, the answer the full-playthrough test walks, and the mending reaches it and the keeping alike at one price by the arc's own guardrail). Then the two menus it used to escape it drives instead: face to face with a ward-dropped keeper the laying menu opens, and it lays the first one down gently (the mercy road that earns the grace) and mends the very next, the third answer spent once ever. Two small disciplines keep it safe and honest. The reveal talks are opened for their rung then closed the next tick, the beat firing on the bump before the menu is even read, so the pilot's old talk-escape stays exactly what it was. And after resolution every keeper is met at the laying menu rather than the sword, the first laid down, the second mended, any after laid down again rather than fought, so the hollow stops being a fight at all and its deaths leave the column. The report grew an arc block: the furthest rung reached, and the cycles the keeping, the laying, and the mending fell on, each caught by watching its flag turn on the one key that turns it. Results on the master seed: seven crossings climb the whole ladder (the truth as the first hollow falls, the vision at the next shrine-rest, the two witnesses and the mender's tiers through the ledger world, the commission heard by the time the last stair first stands at tier 5), answer the keeping at the Hearth in cycle 5, lay a keeper down that same cycle, and mend the next in cycle 6, the restore path driven end to end by real keys, the death toll staying low at three. Verified the three ways the tool demands: two runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string (4291 keys) replayed through the independent sim reproduces the exact end state (cycle 8, tier 8, turn 4176, three deaths), the rest, talk, keeping, and laying keys all in the emitted stream reproducing identically, which proves the pilot drives the arc through no hidden state; and it holds across seeds 1, 7, 99, 2024, and 88888, every one climbing to the mending, answering the keeping in cycle 5 and mending in cycle 6, deaths one to four, so the climb is general and not a master-seed fluke (the flag-gated rungs are exactly why the geography can differ while the ladder does not). All 314 tests unchanged, no Aegis.Core touched, save format still v26. Options set aside: adding any arc path to the engine (all of it has stood since D-038 onward, so this was the pilot learning to walk the ladder, not a rules change); using the D-060 test's Debug_ teleports to reach the crossings (rejected on the same ground as every increment since D-062, a live proof must drive the real key path, so the pilot navigates to the mender, the hermit, and the Hearth by the same breadth-first walk it uses everywhere); killing keepers after the mending rather than laying them down (rejected because the gentle laying clears the ring at no risk while a fight courts a death, so mercy is also the survivable answer); answering the keeping kept rather than refused (either reaches the mending at one price, so refused was chosen to mirror the proven full-playthrough test exactly). Deferred: the oath-crossings, the one companion capability named alongside the restore path in D-062's list, still unbuilt, the bot crossing plain where it could set its own terms at the arch (a clean, separable next increment, the crossing menu being a simple toggle-then-cross); a Wits-raising demo mode for the perception-build identity; and the machine-readable report for a sweep or CI to consume the crossings as data.

### D-067: The journey-bot answers the sheet's questions, taking the knacks that fit how it fights (2026-07-18)
Pays the named deferral carried on D-064's, D-065's, and D-066's lists, the threshold questions the sheet puts as skills level and the bot had walked past unanswered, and closes it with a pure pilot addition, no engine touched (the knack path has stood since D-046: at a skill's level-2 and level-4 thresholds the sheet puts a two-way question of style, opened with c, answered with a digit for good, the sibling foreclosed, the whole exchange turn-free). The pilot now drives that menu the way it already drives the shrine's, the smith's, and the pack's: the moment a question stands open it presses c to read its own ledger, answers with the preferred side's digit, lets the next question be put if one waits, then closes; and because the sheet costs no turn it is safe to answer even mid-fight, no turn passing means no stone falls while the bearer settles the question, and the knack's edge is on the very next blow.
