One rule sets all ten picks: take the side that pays for reading the fight and pressing the attack, because that is the fight this pilot plays (it steps off the telegraph, strikes the body that is spoken for, finishes the weakest, and holds a line for the wind-up that drops a board), so at level 2 it takes the deeper blow over the spared wind or strap, and at level 4 the read moment (the extra bite into a blow already read) over the even hand that pays a little on every exchange. Two picks depart from the rule, both forced by how the bot lives: brawling takes the deep breath (+2 wind for good) over the harder bare fist, because it arms at the forge and never fights bare past the first camp, so the wind aids every blow and loose while the knuckle would die the moment it buys an axe; and the ranged pair, the hunter's eye and the picked moment, is the archer's own, sharpening the shafts the bot leans on hardest against a leaguer's warders, the picked moment above all, since it deepens a shot into a body mid-move, which is exactly the warder mid-whirl the bot times its loose to. The report grew a run-total knack line, counted deterministically by watching the perk list grow by one on the key that lands an answer, listing the knacks in the ledger's order. Results across the sweep: the same six questions open and answer in every seven-crossing run (the drawn cut, the follow-through, the braced shoulder, the hunter's eye, the checked swing, the shield-wall), and the read-and-survive knacks cut the death toll hard, the seven-crossing band falling to one-to-three from D-066's four-to-nine (seed 42 from eight deaths to two), because turning the telegraphed blow two further, breaking the wind-up the weight lands on, and closing ranks under a swarm are exactly the blows that used to kill; pushed to sixteen crossings the deeper ranged threshold opens and the picked moment is taken as designed, the headline pick proven on the real path. Verified the three ways the tool demands: two runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through the independent `sim` reproduces the exact end state (seed 42: cycle 9, tier 9, turn 4975, two deaths, the six knacks held, the c/digit/z sheet keys in the emitted string reproducing identically, the deep iron worn); and it holds across seeds 1, 7, 99, 2024, and 88888, each clearing all seven crossings with the same six knacks taken. All 314 tests unchanged, no Aegis.Core touched, save format still v26. Options set aside: adding a knack path to the engine (it was already there since D-046, so this was the pilot learning which side to take, not a rules change); a dogmatic sharp-side rule with no exceptions (rejected for the two departures above, since the bare-hand knuckle is dead weight on an armed bearer and the deep breath's wind helps everything); taking the wear-sparing side to stretch durability (rejected because deep iron carries high wear and the bot swaps gear freely, so wear is never the bottleneck, deaths are, and the damage-and-ward knacks are what lower them); answering only on safe ground after a site is cleared (unnecessary, since the exchange is turn-free and provably cannot let a blow land, so answering the instant the training clicks is both safe and puts the edge onto the current fight). Deferred, the last of the earlier lists: teaching the threshold and the Severed for D-060's restore path and the oath-crossings (still wanting the arc's reveal ladder climbed first, the heavier lift); and the still-open Wits-raising demo mode and machine-readable report for a sweep or CI.

### D-066: The journey-bot loots the deep iron and puts it on (2026-07-18)
Pays the named deferral carried on D-065's, D-064's, and D-063's lists, the site chests the bot walked past holding gear stronger than anything the smith draws, and closes it with a pure pilot addition, no engine touched (the chest path has stood since D-041: each deep site's chest holds coin and, in the five that carry it, a signature piece, the barrow's grave-iron blade, the quarry's carver's maul, the hall's wright's mail, the ringfort's yew warbow, and the leaguer's scaled byrnie, taken by pressing g on the chest cell). The catch the change had to answer is that the engine's AcquireGear only wears a found piece when its slot is bare; a piece taken while the slot is full, and after D-064 the bot arms every slot at the forge, lands in the pack and does nothing until it is put on. So the addition is two halves. First, the taking: once a site is held and its foes are down, the bot walks back over the won ground to its chest and opens it before climbing out to daylight, gated on a cleared site exactly as the reclaim is, never into one it gave up on or one still tenanted, because coin is worth nothing bought with the death that a live site deals. Second, the wearing: the bot drives the pack menu the way it drives the shrine's and the smith's, ranking every piece it owns for a slot by the good it gives here and now (the effective bonus, which already halves an under-met or a worn piece), then by raw bonus so a higher ceiling wins a tie and is worn early against the day the arm grows into it, then by stauncher wear and id for a stable order; it wears the best when that is not already the one worn, and closes the pack when every slot wears its best. Putting iron on is a free glance in the fiction, no turn spent, so the bot re-arms the instant it loots, and the ranking cannot oscillate: it shifts only toward the higher-requirement piece as attributes rise, and wear only ever moves a worn piece down, so it converges in one swap per slot and holds. The report grew a run-total loot line, counted deterministically by watching a chest's own flag flip as the g lands, the same signal a player reads in the log. Results on the master seed: the bot climbs to the top of all three gear ladders by mid-run and wears them, the carver's maul over the smith's axe and the barrow blade, the scaled byrnie over the shirt and the wright's mail, the yew warbow over the hunting bow, forty chests opened for six hundred and ten coin and all five deep pieces taken up, and the stronger iron more than pays its way: the death toll across the sweep falls to a four-to-nine band, mostly four to six, from D-065's eight to ten, because the deep weapon and armor roughly double the bearer's arm and ward. Verified the three ways the tool demands: two runs byte-identical; the full fifty-six-hundred-key emit string replayed through the independent `sim` reproduces the exact end state (cycle 9, tier 9, turn 5488, eight deaths, and the deep iron worn, the maul, byrnie, and warbow held while the superseded grave-iron, wright's mail, and smith wares sit kept in the pack, proof the equip picks the strictly better piece and keeps the rest); and it holds across seeds 1, 7, 99, 2024, and 88888, each clearing all 7 crossings with nothing left standing and ending in the full deep-iron set. All 314 tests unchanged, no Aegis.Core touched, nothing near the save format. Options set aside: adding a loot path to the engine (it was already there since D-041, so this was the pilot learning to take and wear the iron, not a rules change); looting opportunistically mid-fight whenever the bot happens to cross the chest cell (rejected in favor of the after-clear walk, since the chest sits deep and its contents are worth nothing until the ground is safe, and the cleared-ground rule is the same one that keeps the reclaim honest); buying nothing at the forge on the theory the chests out-gear it anyway (rejected: the early smith iron is what keeps the bearer alive through the first sites to reach the deep chests at all, and the equip step upgrades off it for free the moment a better piece is in hand, so the forge coin is not wasted); and always wearing the highest raw bonus (rejected for the effective bonus, which correctly keeps a met ash spear over an under-met grave-iron until Might reaches the blade's asking, and the hunting bow ahead of the warbow until Grace grows into the heavier draw). Deferred, still from the earlier lists: answering the perk questions as they open for the ranged knacks (the picked moment above all); teaching the threshold and the Severed for D-060's restore path and the oath-crossings; and the still-open Wits-raising mode and machine-readable report.

### D-065: The journey-bot reclaims its dead: closing the remnant leak (2026-07-18)
Pays D-064's own named deferral, the coin and Essence a death dropped and left forever, and closes it with a pure pilot addition, no engine touched (the reclaim path has stood since D-008: a death leaves a remnant where the bearer fell, one chance to walk back onto it and press g before the next death or the crossing forfeits it, and the drop already rides the pilot snapshot). The pilot learns to take it back at three moments, each guarded so the coin never costs more than it is worth. First, standing on its own remnant mid-fight it grabs it on the spot, unless a lofted stone is due on that very cell next turn, in which case the dodge answers first; a death here would forfeit the drop, so the sure coin in hand beats one more swing. Second, once a site is cleared and its foes are down, the bot walks back over the ground it fell on and reclaims before climbing out to daylight. Third, on the overworld with every site cleared or written off, before it sets a hand on the arch that would forfeit the drop, it detours to fetch a remnant left somewhere safe to walk back into, out on the overworld map, or down a site already cleared of its foes (the camp among them, since it is a normal site in the world's list and always cleared before the crossing). The one rule that shapes all three is that the bot never chases a remnant into a site it gave up on, nor back into one still tenanted for coin alone: re-entering ground that beat us hard enough to write off only courts the death that would forfeit the drop anyway, so the in-site reclaim fires only on a cleared site and the overworld detour only for a cleared site or the open map; a live site the runner is routing the bot into to clear takes its own remnant during that clear. The report grew a run-total reclaim line, counted deterministically by watching a drop vanish with no fresh death and no crossing to account for it, which leaves only a reclaim; the same signal a player reads in the log. Results on the master seed: the bot reclaims all six of its remnants (a hundred and eighty-five coin and three hundred and eighty-six Essence kept back that D-064 forfeited whole), and the recovered Essence funds enough extra raising that the seven-crossing death toll falls from seven to six and the bearer reaches tier seven a point higher in every attribute it trains (Vigor twelve, Might eleven, Grace eleven, against D-064's Grace ten). Robust across seeds: 1, 7, 99, 2024, and 88888 all climb the full seven crossings with nothing left standing, each reclaiming most or all of its remnants (a hundred and fifty to nearly five hundred Essence recovered per run), the few left unreclaimed being exactly the cases the design declines, a second death in the same site before the first was fetched (the earlier drop forfeits) or a death in a site later given up on, never a safe reclaim missed. Verified the three ways the tool demands: two runs byte-identical; the full forty-two-hundred-key emit string replayed through the independent `sim` reproduces the exact end state (cycle 8, tier 8, turn 4140, six deaths, the bow's own wear proving the shafts were really loosed, every kind banked at three and softened to Read); and it holds across the seed sweep. All 314 tests unchanged, no Aegis.Core touched, nothing near the save format. Options set aside: adding a reclaim path to the engine (it was already there, so this was the pilot learning when the coin is worth the walk, not a rules change); chasing every remnant including into the sites that killed us (rejected as the leak-for-a-worse-leak it is, a re-death forfeits the very drop being chased); reclaiming early to spend the coin at the smith this world rather than before the arch (unnecessary, since unspent coin converts to Essence at the crossing and carries whole, so the value is kept either way, and reclaiming last avoids thrashing the overworld route each loop). Deferred, still from D-064's and D-063's lists: looting the site chests it walks past (the grave-iron blade, the yew warbow, the scaled byrnie, all stronger than the smith's wares); answering the perk questions as they open for the ranged knacks (the picked moment above all); teaching the threshold and the Severed for D-060's restore path and the oath-crossings; and the still-open Wits-raising mode and machine-readable report.

### D-064: The journey-bot arms itself: the smith, the shrine sought out, and the leaguer finally taken (2026-07-18)
Pays D-063's own named deferrals in one stroke, arming the autopilot at the smith and handing it the loosed line, and in doing so it turns the leaguer from the honest ceiling into a cleared site. Three threads, each a pure addition to the pilot in Aegis.Cli with no engine touched. First, the trade: on the overworld the bot now walks to the smith whenever a slot of its own is bare and the coin is in hand, and buys through the talk menu the way a player does, computing the buy-digit live from the topic count plus the offer's place in the list (the smith's offers never shift under a buyer's fingers, D-041, so the digit is stable), taking armor first for the staying power the deep sites ask, then an edge, then the hunting bow; a bought piece auto-equips a bare slot and then rides the bearer through death and every crossing, so a world or two of the dark's coin leaves it shod for good. Second, a regression the arming itself exposed and fixed: cutting the deaths meant the bot stopped waking on the shrine for the free raise D-063 leaned on, so it under-grew; the pilot now seeks the shrine out deliberately, walking back to spend banked essence whenever a raise is in hand, and once a bow is on its shoulder Grace joins the Vigor and Might rotation (the eye that makes a shaft bite, D-050, and slips a lofted stone, D-057), Wits still held at baseline so the D-061 dulling stays legible. Third, the bow verb against the one foe a fist cannot reach, the sling-warder that keeps its distance and lofts stone over the mere (D-057): the bot reads the eight lines out of its cell as a shaft would fly them and looses when one would bite, or once to wake a dormant board and sound the horn; it dodges the lofted stone with feet, since the cast falls two turns after the whirl on the ground it was marked to, so a stone due to land next turn is stepped out from under while the shots wait for the exposed turns after. The key that unlocked the leaguer is that the warder's board hangs open the whole time it winds, so a shaft loosed into the wind-up connects, and the positioning is done by pathfinding to a firing cell (a clear bank-line to a warder at a shooting standoff, under the fewest slings at once) with the same breadth-first walk the rest of the pilot uses, because greedy stepping is useless around a water-broken ring where only a shot straight along a bank clears the mere; standing on the cell it holds for the board to drop, and works the ring a cluster at a time so the whole watch never bears on it together. Results on the master seed: the bot arms itself to ash spear, quilted jack, and hunting bow by tier three, levels into a Grace-ten archer by tier seven, and clears every site at every tier through seven, the leaguer among them at both tier six and tier seven (nothing left standing anywhere), with the seven-crossing death total falling from D-063's fifty-seven to seven and the tier-six leaguer world taking zero deaths outright; the bow is genuinely used, twenty-six shafts drawn across the run. Verified the three ways the tool demands: two runs byte-identical; the emit-keys string replayed through the independent `sim` reproduces the exact end state (cycle 8, turn 3980, 7 deaths, the bow's own wear proving the shafts were really loosed, every kind banked at three and softened to Read); and it holds across seeds (1, 7, 99, 2024, 88888 all climb the full seven crossings with nothing left standing, seven to seventeen deaths each), so the armed clear is general, not a seed-42 fluke. The report grew an arms line per crossing, the bearer's weapon, mail, bow, and Vigor/Might/Grace, so the arming and leveling read at a glance. All 314 tests unchanged, no Aegis.Core touched, nothing near the save format. Options set aside: adding the bow or the warder behavior to the engine (both were already there since D-050 and D-057, so the whole clear was the pilot learning where to stand and when to loose, not a rules change); a Grace-only archer build that drops Might (Might still buys the melee sites, so the rotation keeps all three); minimizing exposure so hard the bot never lines up a shot (an earlier cut did exactly that, surviving the leaguer on two arrows and clearing nothing, which is why the firing-cell hold replaced pure evasion). Deferred: reclaiming the death-remnant, whose coin and essence are still lost unrecovered, a real economy leak the bot simply eats; looting the site chests it walks past (the grave-iron blade, the yew warbow, the scaled byrnie, all stronger than the smith's wares); answering the perk questions as they open, since the ranged knacks (the picked moment above all) would sharpen the archer further; teaching the threshold and the Severed for D-060's restore path and the oath-crossings; and the still-open Wits-raising mode and machine-readable report from D-063.
